# Test-suite invariants

What each block of the test suite pins down, and which load-bearing rule
would break if an assertion were weakened. Relocated verbatim from CLAUDE.md.
Per-PR implementation history lives in `docs/changelog/`.

## Template rendering contract

Relocated out of `AGENTS.md` §8 so its size cap leaves the new SALT refusal
rule visible; the modifying-templates section links here.

`result_template.json` field names reach the summary PDF through
`agents/lib/pdf_render.py`, which is tracked and tested against this template:
a renamed field shows up in `tests/test_pdf_render.py`, not on a return.

## The suite
- Schedule CA (`test_ca_schedule_ca.py`) checks per-document components against
  inclusive federal totals, issuer jurisdictions, RIC status and each quarter's
  fetched asset threshold. It pins the at-threshold exemption, full addition
  below the threshold, taxable SS/refund amounts, source disclosures, legacy
  migration, standalone tie-out and receipt invalidation after source edits.
- CA payments (`test_ca_payments.py`) derives line72 from dated, state/year-tagged
  source records and opening applied credit, rejects duplicates and malformed
  records even inside a positive total, and verifies ownership and forward
  elections. Rollover preserves zero, separates federal/CA credits, resets
  confirmation after a changed carry and requires allocation review across
  status changes. Receipt tests invalidate edits to payments and disclosures.
- Checked Form 540 (`test_ca_540_builder.py`) pins the reviewed-year line map,
  published-table ceiling, exemption and tax floors, signed Schedule P
  adjustments, MFS addback and payment waterfall. CLI tests bind its verdict
  to the existing receipt, reject later edits and missing disclosures, and
  keep a federal scope waiver from waiving CA. A stale federal AGI must be
  replaced before the writer validates CA settlement. FTB5805 tests
  (`test_ca_5805*.py`) pin daily endpoints, half-year expiry, weighted payments
  and withholding, prior/current floors, reviewed prior-year facts and tax-base
  exclusions. Source changes, dropped markers, manual edits and missing method
  facts cannot preserve a checked penalty or its final balance.
- State withholding (`test_state_withholding_routes.py`) ties CA W-2,
  1099/W-2G and Forms 592-B/593 components separately, and NY State/NYC/Yonkers
  totals under either return variant. Other-state breakdowns survive record
  assembly, must sum, and cannot hide a computed state's payment. Generic
  estimates and genuinely external returns remain advisory.
- California scope (`test_ca_scope.py`) checks each blocking situation, state
  applicability, unanswered screening and explicit acknowledgements. Tie-out
  checks protect taxable Social Security/refund subtractions even if the CA
  section is missing, and preserve the worked FTB 3510 credit through rollover
  without an ISO exercise. Public SOI measurements distinguish checked
  subtractions from unsupported situations and municipal-interest bounds.
- Test suite: `source .venv/bin/activate && python3 -m pytest tests/ -v`
- Two tests skip; the rest pass, covering all computation functions, with hand-computed expected values from IRS worksheets, + FL type-none state module (template/freshness, sales-tax input, harvest banner, capability note, property calendar, registrations). **The suite's size is NOT restated here** — CLAUDE.md keeps every absolute in one place, the `MEASURED COSTS` block in `.claude/hooks/skill-invariants.sh`, and a test count is named there among the figures that go stale while still reading as fresh. This line held a second copy, updated by its own convention ("when adding a test, update the count above"), which is exactly the two-writer drift that rule forbids; read the block for the current figure. **Per-PR / per-Codex-round implementation history lives in `docs/changelog/` (one file per epic I–P + `pre-epic.md`); the load-bearing invariants each block must preserve are inventoried below.** When changing an assertion, update its worksheet comment.
  - **An artifact digest is not PII, and the bare-SSN rule cannot tell them apart (`test_pii_content_scan.py`):** a sha256 is 64 hex characters, and a maximal run of EXACTLY nine digits occurs inside one often enough that `requirements.txt` tripped the content scan 52 times the first time the tree was pinned with `--generate-hashes`. The scan's documented escape is `--no-verify`, so a gate firing on correct data every time the lock is regenerated would train contributors to bypass the PII scan itself — the exact failure this repo has already paid for. `strip_artifact_digests` removes the digest TOKENS before counting, scoped along BOTH axes so it can never become a hiding place: the exact PATH (`requirements.txt`/`requirements.in`, never a name pattern or a directory) and a token that must be the literal `--hash=sha256:` plus exactly 64 lowercase hex characters. The acceptance record is the second exempt path for the same reason on a file whose whole point is to be re-committed after a replay — four pins, rewritten every re-run — with its own token: the `sha256` word plus a BACKTICKED 64 hex characters, on one line. That shape is a contract in two files, so a test reads the live record and fails if any digest in it sits outside the token: sed applies a line at a time, and rewrapping a pin so the word and the digest fall on separate lines silently un-exempts it, the first symptom being a blocked commit mid-re-run. The rule is checked from BOTH sides, because this module is not one of the six the skill-invariants hook selects and its verdict therefore arrives in CI, after the commit the wrap blocks: `_pin_unstripped` in `test_claude_agents.py` — which an edit to the record does select — applies the gate's own strip and reports whatever digest survives it, while the two pin predicates around it go on flattening whitespace, which is what let a wrapped pin verify there in the first place. Asking the gate's question rather than "is this one pin well formed" is what also catches the renewal written `` sha256 `<new>` (was `<old>`) ``, where the pin the predicate found is well formed and the digest beside it is not exempt at all. Three tests hold the whole line — the two exempt files pass, a real SSN elsewhere in each SAME file still blocks, a digest written loose in the exempt path still blocks, and the identical token in `notes.md`, in a sibling under `docs/ops/`, and in a same-basename file at the root still blocks, because keying on token shape alone would let any secret be laundered by prefixing it. What the record's exemption was chosen OVER is the finding it answers: listing the nine-digit run inside one pin in `.pii-allowlist`, which is global and matched by value, so a valid-shaped nine-digit number would be suppressed in every file and every pathname in the repository — a real SSN equal to it would commit clean past the toolkit's one absolute rule. That alternative was declined, never committed; no revision of the allowlist has carried a digest's digits. Mutation-verified in three directions: the exemption removed (the false-positive test goes red), its `sha256` word dropped so a bare digest is exempt (the shape bound goes red), and its path widened to `*.md` (the leak test goes red).
  - **`A...B` excludes A, and for one base that is a hole rather than an optimization (`test_pii_content_scan.py`):** range mode resolves a merge base and enumerates `merge_base..head`, which omits the base commit — correct for CI, where the base is already on `main` and was scanned by the pull request that landed it. A ROOT commit is the single base for which that justification is false: nothing precedes it, so no earlier range can ever have covered it. The consequence was not theoretical — the full-history audit that gates publishing a private repository is `$(git rev-list --max-parents=0 HEAD)...HEAD`, and it reported `PII scan clean — 1 commit(s) inspected` over a root commit holding a full SSN. The per-commit loop already handles a parentless commit (it diffs against the empty tree), so the fix is to append the base to the revision list when `<base>^` does not resolve, and nothing else. Two tests, deliberately paired: the root base BLOCKS and names the file, and a non-root base is still EXCLUDED — without the second, widening `A...B` to always include its base would pass the first while failing every PR for content that already passed its own review. The first is mutation-verified to fail without the fix; the second passes either way by design, since its job is to prove the fix did not widen. The coverage-line test was rewritten to start from a non-root base for the same reason: it had been silently exercising the broken path, and its file count moved when the hole closed.
  - **600 on the files, 755 on the directory holding them (`test_archive.py`, `test_scenario.py`, `test_tie_out.py`):** AGENTS.md §1 requires `chmod 700` on the runtime directories precisely because a 600 file still sits inside a world-readable LISTING, and under `archive/` the listing is the disclosure — `documents/W2_Acme.pdf` names an employer before anyone opens it. Three `agents/lib/` sites created directories with a bare `mkdir` and then chmod'd only the files: `archive.py` (`archive/`, `archive/{year}/`, and the subtrees it moves in, which arrive carrying their root mode), `scenario.py` (`scenarios/`, whose FILENAMES are the scenario names), `tie_out.py` (`summaries/`, one file per source document, named after it). Every one landed at 0755 under a normal umask while the public `SECURITY.md` promised 0700. **Each test pins umask 022 for the duration**, without which the assertion is vacuous: `mkdir` asks for 0777 and umask 077 subtracts its way to 0700 unaided, so the test would pass against the unfixed code on a hardened developer machine and fail nowhere. All three are mutation-verified red before the fix. `mkdir(mode=0o700)` is not sufficient on its own and the chmod is not redundant with it — `mode` reaches the leaf only (Python creates `parents=True` levels at the default), and is ignored outright for a directory that already exists, which is the ordinary case for `scenarios/` and `summaries/`. The failure semantics match across all three sites, each raising its own error type on a chmod `OSError` rather than swallowing it: `archive.py` raises inside the try, so it rolls back and surfaces as `ArchiveError` with no half-created archive — a directory whose mode could not be set is one documents do not get moved into — and `scenario.py`/`tie_out.py` raise `ScenarioError`/`TieOutError` and remove whatever was written rather than leaving it at an insecure mode. `tie_out.py` runs inside the tie-out HARD GATE, but that is an argument FOR failing closed here, not against it: a filesystem quirk that silently left a report unsecured would be a worse outcome than a blocked return the gate already exists to produce. `test_atomicity_rollback_on_chmod_failure` was rewritten in the same change: it injected on chmod CALL #1, which is now a pre-move directory chmod, so it would have exercised the nothing-was-moved path and passed while proving nothing about rollback — it now targets the first chmod on a FILE and asserts it actually fired. What the tests did NOT cover was the larger half: the directories created by the AGENT rather than the library. That half is now covered by skill instructions instead of library code, and by `test_secure_fs.py`'s two skill guards rather than a filesystem assertion — `/tax-setup` creates and secures `documents/` and `summaries/`, and `tax-review`/`tax-project`/`tax-withholding` do the same for theirs. Only the WORKSPACE ROOT is still manual, because nothing in the toolkit creates it; `SECURITY.md` names that one boundary rather than claiming the mode outright.
  - **`os.replace` carries a file's mode across the rename — write-then-chmod on a `.tmp` predecessor does not guarantee the same thing (`test_rollover.py`):** `apply_changes` wrote `tmp_profile`/`tmp_result` via `Path.write_text`, which creates a NEW file at the shell's default mode (typically 644 under umask 022), then handed it to `os.replace`, which renames the file's content AND mode onto the real target — so the trailing `os.chmod(..., 0o600)` only tightened `taxpayer_profile.json`/`result.json` after the rename had already landed them at 644, and the restore path on a failed second replace (`targets.profile.write_bytes(original_profile)`) writes into the file at whatever mode the first replace left it, never tightening it at all. Fixed the way `scenario.py`/`tie_out.py` already were: create the `.tmp` file at 600 via `os.open` before any content is written, `fchmod`-ing the open descriptor (falling back to a path chmod where `os.fchmod` is unavailable, i.e. Windows) rather than trusting `mode=` alone, since POSIX ignores that argument when a `.tmp` path is left behind by a prior failed apply. `test_apply_changes_tmp_file_is_600_before_replace` spies on `os.replace` and asserts the SOURCE path's mode at the moment of the call — the only point in the sequence where the pre-fix bug is directly observable, since the trailing `os.chmod` on the final target passes either way.
  - **`os.chmod`/`Path.chmod` are POSIX-only enforcement wearing a cross-platform name (`test_secure_fs.py`, `test_scenario.py`, `test_tie_out.py`):** every `chmod 600`/`chmod 700` call site across `archive.py`, `rollover.py`, `scenario.py`, `tie_out.py` and `tax_calendar.py` was silently non-enforcing on Windows despite AGENTS.md §1 requiring owner-only access to every PII/financial runtime file and directory it names — `os.fchmod` does not exist there at all, and `Path.chmod()` only toggles the read-only DOS attribute, touching nothing about which other local accounts can read or list the file. `agents/lib/secure_fs.py` centralizes every one of those call sites behind `secure_path()`/`secure_fd()`: `os.chmod`/`os.fchmod` on POSIX (unchanged behavior), and on Windows PowerShell's `Get-Acl`/`Set-Acl` building a *protected* DACL — `SetAccessRuleProtection($true, $false)` blocks inheritance without copying the inherited rules down as explicit ones, then every remaining explicit rule is removed via `RemoveAccessRule` before the one new rule (the current user, Full Control, resolved from `WindowsIdentity.GetCurrent()` rather than a spoofable `%USERNAME%` string) is added. An `icacls /inheritance:r /grant:r` one-liner was tried first and REJECTED on review: `/grant:r` only replaces grants for the ONE user named, leaving every OTHER principal's existing explicit ACE (a prior owner, a share-level "Everyone" grant, anything left over from a move out of a shared location) untouched — a path could pass that command and still be readable by another account, exactly the gap AGENTS.md §1 exists to close. A failing PowerShell invocation, or the subprocess call itself erroring or timing out, raises `OSError` rather than silently leaving the file at its prior ACL; `test_secure_fs.py` covers all three with `os.name` monkeypatched to `"nt"` and `subprocess.run` stubbed, since real NTFS ACL enforcement can't be exercised from this project's POSIX-only CI — the stubbed assertions check the *script text* for the protect/clear/add sequence and the resolved-identity call, not a live ACL. The refactor moved the actual `os.chmod`/`os.fchmod` call out of `scenario.py`/`tie_out.py` into `secure_fs.py`, which broke two chmod-failure tests in each of those files that monkeypatched `Path.chmod` directly — a class-method patch, unlike `os.chmod`, which is a singleton module attribute reachable (and therefore patchable) through ANY importer's `os` name, which is why the `archive.py`/`rollover.py` chmod-failure tests kept passing unmodified through the same refactor. All four rewritten tests now patch `<module>.os.chmod` instead, matching the technique `test_archive.py`'s own chmod- and scandir-failure tests already used.
  - **A junction is a reparse point `Path.is_symlink()` cannot see (`test_secure_fs.py`, `test_archive.py`, `test_tie_out.py`):** an NTFS junction carries a different reparse tag (`IO_REPARSE_TAG_MOUNT_POINT`) than a symlink (`IO_REPARSE_TAG_SYMLINK`) and — unlike a symlink on older Windows — needs no elevated privilege to create, so `Path.is_symlink()`/`os.path.islink()` return `False` for one. Every symlink-refusal check this codebase already had (`archive.py`'s `_refuse_symlink`/`_refuse_symlink_destination`, `tie_out.py`'s inline `summaries.is_symlink()` guard) existed specifically to stop a runtime path from silently resolving outside the workspace, and a junction defeated every one of them on Windows: `archive/` junctioned to an external directory would pass straight through to `mkdir`/`secure_path`, which would then harden that external target and move a taxpayer's files into it. `secure_fs.is_reparse_point()` takes ONE `os.lstat()`, answers the symlink question from `stat.S_ISLNK(st.st_mode)`, and then — only when `os.name == "nt"` — reads `st.st_file_attributes & stat.FILE_ATTRIBUTE_REPARSE_POINT`, catching a junction alongside a symlink. That attribute is the GATE, not the answer — see the reparse-TAG bullet below. (An earlier version of this note described it as calling `Path.is_symlink()` first; it does not, and the reason is the fail-closed bullet below.) `archive.py`, `tie_out.py`, `parse_utils.py` and `tax_law_fetcher.py` all call it everywhere they previously called `is_symlink()` directly. `test_secure_fs.py` simulates a junction (real ones can't be created on POSIX) by wrapping `os.lstat`'s real return value with the reparse-point attribute added, careful to keep the real `st_mode` intact — `S_ISLNK` is consulted before the attribute and needs a working `st_mode` to answer correctly, and monkeypatching `os.lstat` (a singleton-module attribute, reachable through any importer's `os` name) affects that call too. A second fix rode along in the same function: `is_reparse_point()` used to re-wrap its argument in `Path(path)` unconditionally, and `pathlib.Path()` dispatches to `WindowsPath`/`PosixPath` based on `os.name` AT CONSTRUCTION TIME — so a test that monkeypatches `os.name` to `"nt"` to simulate Windows was, by that same patch, turning every `Path(...)` call in the process (including the one on an already-concrete `PosixPath` from a POSIX `tmp_path` fixture) into a `WindowsPath`, corrupting its own filesystem calls on the real POSIX runner. Fixed at the time by using an already-`Path` argument as-is rather than passing it back through the os.name-sensitive factory, and CLOSED later by dropping the wrap entirely — `os.lstat` accepts a string, so the conversion bought nothing and a plain string argument still went through the factory (see the Windows-spelling bullet below). `test_archive.py`/`test_tie_out.py` each add one junction-simulation test that patches `is_reparse_point` at the CALLING module's own imported name (`agents.lib.archive.is_reparse_point`, not `agents.lib.secure_fs.is_reparse_point`) — a `from X import Y` binding is a name lookup resolved once at import time into the importer's own namespace, unlike the `os`/`os.chmod` singleton-module pattern elsewhere in this codebase, so patching the source module's name would not be seen by a caller that already imported the function directly.
  - **`FILE_ATTRIBUTE_REPARSE_POINT` does not mean "this path redirects" (`test_secure_fs.py::TestOnlyARedirectingReparseTagIsRefused`):** Windows hangs a whole family of filter-driver features off that one bit — OneDrive Files On-Demand placeholders (`IO_REPARSE_TAG_CLOUD*`), Data Deduplication, the Windows Overlay Filter that compresses installed application files, container image layers — and every one of them is an ORDINARY file whose contents the filesystem produces on demand at the path you asked for. `secure_path`, `parse_utils` and `archive_year` all treat a reparse point as fatal, so the unconditional bit test refused normal taxpayer documents on any OneDrive-backed workspace: `/tax-parse`, `/tax-archive` and `/tax-setup` stopping with "symlink or junction" on a file that is neither. The refusal is keyed on the reparse TAG, and deliberately not on an allowlist of the two tags known to redirect — Windows marks every name-substituting tag with the documented `0x20000000` NAME SURROGATE flag, so testing the flag also refuses redirecting tags added after this was written (WSL's `IO_REPARSE_TAG_LX_SYMLINK` is already one), with `IO_REPARSE_TAG_APPEXECLINK` named explicitly because it redirects without setting the flag. A reparse point whose tag reads 0 is REFUSED: `st_reparse_tag` is Windows-and-3.8+, so 0 means the attribute said "reparse point" and nothing could certify otherwise, which is an uninspected path rather than a benign one. Six tests, both directions, all mutation-verified against the bare-bit version: four redirecting tags refused, four benign tags treated as ordinary files, the unreadable tag failing closed, the tag ignored when the attribute is unset, and — the reported symptom end to end — `secure_path` applying its ACL to a cloud-backed `W2_Acme.pdf` while still raising `PathRefused` on a junction. The `stat` module exposes `IO_REPARSE_TAG_*` only on Windows (`Modules/_stat.c` guards them with `MS_WINDOWS`), so both the module and the tests spell the values out; that is also what lets the branch run on the POSIX CI box at all.
  - **A shallow clone's boundary commit is indistinguishable from a root commit by traversal (`test_pii_content_scan.py`):** `git rev-list --parents -n 1` and `git rev-parse <sha>^` both report the parents THIS CLONE HAS, and in a shallow clone the boundary's parents were never fetched — so both describe it exactly as they describe a true root. Read as a root, the base is appended to the scan set and later diffed against the empty tree: its entire SNAPSHOT is scanned as newly-added content, including files carried down from history the range never asked about, and a pull request fails on content some earlier reviewed commit legitimately holds. The commit OBJECT always carries the true parent list — a graft only hides it from traversal — so `read_recorded_first_parent` reads it, and only the HEADER block, every line before the first genuinely empty one. That last part is the invariant `git cat-file -p | grep '^parent '` broke: it false-matches a commit MESSAGE line beginning "parent " (the pinned case is "parent bug tracked separately"), which reads as "this commit has a parent", excludes the base, and silently reopens the initial-import bypass the root special case exists to close. Inside the range the answer is the opposite: a commit recording a parent this clone does not have cannot be diffed against it, and the old `git rev-parse <sha>^ || echo $EMPTY_TREE` widened that silently into a whole-snapshot scan reported afterwards as "N commit(s) inspected" — coverage the scan did not have — so it now BLOCKS and names the fix (`fetch-depth: 0`). Written in pure bash rather than `grep`/`awk` for the reason the rest of the gate is: a `grep` that cannot RUN reports "no match", and no rule here may be turned off by a failure to test it. Three tests, each mutation-verified: the boundary base passing on a clean range, the root-with-"parent"-in-its-message base still blocking, and the missing recorded parent blocking rather than reporting a verdict.
  - **Skill instructions had the identical Windows chmod gap `secure_fs.py` was built to close, one layer up (`test_portability.py::test_every_bash_block_in_every_skill_parses`):** `tax-setup/SKILL.md`'s `summaries/` directory and `tax-parse/SKILL.md`'s per-document summary files (Step 3's per-file call and Step 6's backstop sweep) were hardened in an earlier round with a bare shell `chmod 700`/`chmod 600` — correct on POSIX, but under a Windows POSIX-compatible shell (WSL/Git Bash) that `chmod` binary hits the exact same DOS-attribute-only gap `os.chmod` had, bypassing `secure_fs.py`'s enforcement entirely since the skill never calls into it. That round missed three more sites in the SAME two skills that a later review caught: `tax-setup`'s `taxpayer_profile.json` chmod (Step 3) and `result.json`/`answers.md` chmod (Step 5), and `tax-parse`'s `parse_ledger.json` chmod (Step 6.5) — a documentation claim in `agents-compat.md` had asserted the migration was complete for "any `SKILL.md` step" while these three still ran a bare `chmod`. All now invoke that CLI instead (written `python3 -m agents.lib.secure_fs` at the time; every call site later moved behind `sh agents/lib/run.sh`) — a CLI the module exposes for exactly this, secured-per-path with the same `secure_path()` used everywhere else, silent on success and printing `Could not secure {path}: {error}` to stderr with a non-zero exit on any failure. `test_cli_secures_each_given_path`/`test_cli_exits_nonzero_and_reports_failure` in `test_secure_fs.py` drive it as a real subprocess (`python3 -m agents.lib.secure_fs`), the only way to exercise an `if __name__ == "__main__":` argv branch without executing the rest of the module's file-scoped code under a borrowed `__name__`. The module's `if __name__ == "__main__":` block now branches on argv: no arguments runs the existing self-test (unchanged), one or more runs the CLI — the same split `archive.py`'s own argv-driven CLI block already uses, so a bare `python3 agents/lib/secure_fs.py` (AGENTS.md §7's "every module self-tests when run directly") is unaffected. That round scoped `agents-compat.md`'s claim to `tax-setup`/`tax-parse` and named every other skill as a known gap; the round after it closed the gap and re-widened the claim — but re-widened it one skill too far. `tax-amend` still ran `chmod -R 600` over `archive/{year}/as_filed/` (a full copy of `result.json`, `taxpayer_profile.json`, `answers.md` and `summaries/`), and `tax-compute` had NO securing step at all, because it writes `result.json` and `answers.md` from `references/finalize.md` and the guard globbed `tax-*/SKILL.md` only. Both are fixed and both guards now read `SKILL.md` PLUS `references/*.md`: **a skill is a directory, and a check that reads only its body scores the progressive-disclosure files as green by not looking at them.**
  - **`is_reparse_point()`'s own inspection failure was the one path left fail-OPEN in an otherwise fail-closed module (`test_secure_fs.py`):** the Windows branch caught every `OSError` from `os.lstat()` — including a transient I/O error or an access-denied result from a misbehaving junction target — and returned `False`, certifying an uninspected path as "not a reparse point." A caller's `exists()`/`mkdir()` follows the same link the failed `lstat` couldn't read, so the one case most likely to indicate something IS wrong with the path was exactly the case treated as "nothing is wrong." Narrowed the except clause to `FileNotFoundError` only — the sole case where "not a reparse point" is actually correct, since a path that doesn't exist can't be one — and let every other `OSError` propagate. **The first version of this note claimed "both `archive.py` and `tie_out.py` already wrap their reparse checks in a broad `except Exception`", and that was false for exactly the three sites where it mattered**: `archive.py`'s two destination-component checks and its whole dry-run loop sit ABOVE `archive_year`'s `try`, and `tie_out.py`'s `summaries/` check sits in `write_report` outside any try — so the newly-propagating `OSError` escaped as a bare traceback past the `except ArchiveError` in `__main__` (the handler that prints the `ERROR: …` line the skill tells the agent to read) and past `_main`'s `except TieOutError`. Fixed by translating at the ONE place each module's checks route through: `archive.py` gained `_is_reparse_point_or_raise()` (and the three redundant `if is_reparse_point(src): _refuse_symlink(src)` guards collapsed to a bare `_refuse_symlink(src)`, which re-evaluates the same predicate internally — the outer `if` was a second `lstat` per entry buying nothing), and `write_report` wraps its own call. `test_is_reparse_point_false_on_windows_file_not_found` pins the narrowed case; `test_is_reparse_point_fails_closed_on_other_windows_lstat_errors` is mutation-verified red against the pre-fix code (an unqualified `except OSError: return False` swallows the `PermissionError` the test raises instead of letting it propagate); `test_uninspectable_destination_raises_archive_error_not_a_bare_oserror`, its dry-run sibling, and `test_write_report_translates_an_uninspectable_summaries_path` pin the translation at all three previously-uncovered sites.
  - **A permissions fault must not be able to mask a tax-correctness fault (`test_tie_out.py`):** `write_report` was called INSIDE `_main`'s main `try`, so once it was hardened to fail closed on a chmod/write error, any such error jumped to `except (TieOutError, RolloverError): return 1` — before the verdict was printed and before `2 if report.failed else 0` was ever evaluated. A return with a dropped 1099 (the CP2000 case the whole gate exists for) would therefore report exit **1** on an unrelated NFS/ACL hiccup, which `tax-compute` Step 12.7 documents as "`parse_ledger.json` is missing or malformed" — sending the agent to fix a ledger that is fine — and neither the markdown nor the `--json` block would be printed at all, so the failure was not merely mislabelled but invisible. The report is fully computed by that point, so the fix is ordering: print the verdict, fix `exit_code = 2 if report.failed else 0`, THEN persist, and on a write failure return `max(exit_code, 1)` — never below 1 (the write really did fail, so it is not a clean pass) and never below the verdict's own code. `test_main_failing_gate_exits_2_even_when_the_report_cannot_be_written` and `test_main_passing_gate_exits_1_when_the_report_cannot_be_written` are both mutation-verified red against the pre-fix ordering, and the failure output is the bug verbatim: `out=''` with exit 1 where 2 was owed.
  - **A protected DACL with a non-inheritable ACE is owner-only for the directory and for nothing inside it (`test_secure_fs.py`):** `_secure_path_windows` built its single `FileSystemAccessRule` with the 3-argument constructor, which means `InheritanceFlags.None`. On a directory that breaks both directions at once. Downward: `SetAccessRuleProtection` + `Set-Acl` propagates the inheritance change to existing children, so a `summaries/*.md` whose only ACEs were inherited from `summaries/` is left with an EMPTY DACL — the owner locked out of their own parse summaries, breaking `/tax-review` and `/tax-generate`. Forward: anything created inside afterwards inherits nothing and falls back to the creating token's default DACL, which normally grants SYSTEM and Administrators — where POSIX `0700` denies traversal and therefore covers every child for free. The script now branches on `$item.PSIsContainer` and gives a directory `'ContainerInherit,ObjectInherit', 'None', 'Allow'`, keeping the 3-argument form for a leaf (inheritance flags on a file are invalid and throw). `test_secure_path_windows_directory_ace_is_inheritable` asserts the branch and the flags in the built script text — the same POSIX-CI boundary every other Windows assertion in this file accepts.
  - **`secure_path()` itself refuses a reparse point, because the skill layer's own guard cannot (`test_secure_fs.py`):** `/tax-setup` Step 1 guards `summaries/` with the shell test `[ -L summaries ]`, which is POSIX-only — it is FALSE for an NTFS junction, the exact case `is_reparse_point()` was added for — and `mkdir -p` treats a junction to a directory as already satisfied. Since `secure_path()` performed no reparse check of its own, a junctioned `summaries/` would have had the protected DACL applied to whatever it pointed at, and `/tax-parse` would then have written the taxpayer's parsed W-2 summaries outside the workspace, past every gitignore and PII rule scoped to it. `secure_path()` now refuses one itself, so the guarantee holds at the layer every caller — library and skill CLI alike — passes through, rather than depending on a POSIX-only test written in prose. `test_secure_path_refuses_a_symlink` also asserts the external target's mode is UNCHANGED (the refusal is what matters, not just the raise), and `test_cli_refuses_a_symlinked_path` drives it through the real subprocess so the non-zero exit `/tax-setup`'s block treats as a refusal is covered end to end.
  - **A preview that skips the subtrees the real run refuses is worse than no preview (`test_archive.py`):** `archive_year`'s `dry_run` branch inspected only the ten top-level entries, while the real run also walks each moved subtree refusing any nested symlink/junction — so a `documents/cloud_sync` symlink produced a preview reading "will move documents/", which the user confirms, and the real run then raises *after* five files have already moved and been rolled back. The dry run now walks the same subtrees with the same `onerror=_raise_walk_error`, and translates the re-raised `OSError` into `ArchiveError` — that loop sits outside the real run's `try`, the identical exposure the reparse checks had. Three tests: the nested refusal now fires in dry run (mutation-verified red), an ordinary nested directory still previews clean (so the walk cannot have been made to refuse everything), and an unreadable nested directory raises `ArchiveError` rather than a bare `PermissionError` — without which `os.walk`'s default swallow would return a silently partial preview reading as clean.
  - **`os.fdopen` adopts a descriptor only on success (`agents/lib/{scenario,tie_out,rollover,tax_calendar}.py`):** all four write paths do `fd = os.open(..., 0o600)` then `with os.fdopen(fd, ...)`. If `os.fdopen` itself raises — `MemoryError` building the buffered wrapper, an `OSError` from the seekable/isatty probe on an exotic filesystem — it has not taken ownership, the `with` never runs, and no `except` closes the raw descriptor: `scenario.py` unlinks the path and raises `ScenarioError` while leaking the fd. A long-lived agent session that repeatedly re-saves scenarios or re-runs the tie-out gate against a failing filesystem walks to `EMFILE`, after which every later `os.open` in the process fails — including the ones writing `result.json`. All four now wrap the `fdopen` call and `os.close(fd)` before re-raising; `rollover.py` opens two descriptors per call, so it compounds twice as fast. No test: the trigger is an `os.fdopen` failure that leaves a valid descriptor, which cannot be simulated without patching `os.fdopen` itself into a state the surrounding code then can't proceed through — the correctness here is structural (an unowned resource has exactly one owner) rather than behavioural.
  - **`tax_calendar.py` was the last write-then-chmod site (`test_tax_calendar.py`):** `write_calendar` did `tmp.write_bytes(...)` and only then `secure_path(tmp)`, so the `.tmp` existed at the umask default (0644) already holding the taxpayer's filing status, state and payment schedule — and on Windows with no ACL protection at all until the PowerShell call returned. `rollover.py`, `scenario.py` and `tie_out.py` were each fixed for exactly this in earlier rounds; this file was migrated to `secure_path()` without being migrated off the pattern. Now `os.open(..., 0o600)` + `secure_fd` on the descriptor before the first byte lands, with the `os.fdopen` wrapped so a failure there closes the raw descriptor rather than leaking it (`with` only covers the success path). `test_write_calendar_tmp_file_is_600_before_replace` spies on `os.replace` and reads the SOURCE mode at the moment of the call — the only point in the sequence where the gap is observable, since `os.replace` carries the mode across and the trailing `secure_path` passes either way — and pins umask 022, without which the assertion is vacuous.
  - **A shell command split across a markdown line break silently degrades to its own self-test (`tax-parse/SKILL.md`):** the per-document `secure_fs` instruction in the parse-task prompt template read "run: `python3 -m agents.lib.secure_fs`" with the quoted summary path on the FOLLOWING line — plain prose in a markdown paragraph, where the line break carries no meaning to a human reader but is exactly where a dispatched sub-task copying the command literally would stop: `secure_fs` invoked with zero arguments runs its self-test (the documented no-argument behavior) rather than securing the just-written summary file, and the loud failure mode (self-test output) looks nothing like a permissions failure. Reworded so the module name and its quoted argument sit on ONE line, called out explicitly as a single inline code span rather than prose that could re-wrap across a line break.
  - **A gate that cannot fail on live data is indistinguishable from no gate (`test_secure_fs.py`):** `test_tax_skills_have_no_bare_permission_commands` matched a line STARTING with `chmod 600`/`chmod 700`, or containing `&& chmod 600/700`. It reported green while `.agents/skills/tax-amend/SKILL.md` carried "`chmod -R 600` the files (`700` the dirs)." — the one real offender in the tree, invisible because the flag sits between the command and the mode and the line starts with a backtick. `chmod 0600`, `chmod u=rw,go=`, `; chmod 700` and `xargs chmod 600` were the same blind spot wearing other syntax. The detector now matches the bare WORD `chmod` and exempts only a line that also carries explanatory phrasing — the marker set lives in `_CHMOD_EXPLANATORY_MARKERS` and is not restated here, since a second copy drifts. Each marker is a phrase that appears only when a sentence is telling you NOT to use chmod, and the exemption is kept LINE-LOCAL on purpose: a justification one line above does not license the invocation, which is why two `tax-setup` sentences were reflowed so `not a bare `chmod 600`` sits on the line it excuses rather than the one before. Prose and command are not separable by backticks — the real offender was inside them — so the discriminator has to be the phrasing. `test_bare_chmod_detector_catches_every_shipped_and_plausible_form` is the anti-vacuity guard: it feeds the detector the shipped offender plus six plausible spellings and asserts each is FLAGGED, then feeds it the five real prose lines and asserts none is. Without it, widening the exemption set is the cheapest way to turn the gate green again.
  - **The DACL and the owner must be two `Set-Acl` calls, restriction first (`test_secure_fs.py`):** `Set-Acl` persists every MODIFIED SECTION of a security descriptor in one `SetSecurityInfo`, so folding `SetOwner` into the same object made ownership and restriction all-or-nothing. On a path owned by another identity where the process holds `WRITE_DAC` but not `WRITE_OWNER`/`SeTakeOwnershipPrivilege` — the "left over from a move out of a shared location" case the surrounding comment already cites — the combined call throws and NO restriction is applied at all, where the pre-ownership code applied the protected owner-only DACL successfully. Inside `archive.py` that `OSError` propagates out of `secure_path(dst)` and rolls back the entire year-end archive move. Split into two calls the restriction lands and only the ownership transfer is reported as failed. The ORDER is the other half: taking ownership first and then failing to restrict leaves the path owned and wide open, while restricting first and then failing to take ownership leaves it closed to everyone except a prior owner who could still revert it. Verification was owner-only too, checking a property the module does not exist to enforce: a descriptor can come back with the owner correct and the access rules untouched (a filter driver, a redirected or network path) and `secure_path` would return success while every other local account still reads the file. It now re-reads and asserts `AreAccessRulesProtected`, exactly one explicit rule, that rule's identity/`Allow`/`FullControl`, and a container's inheritance flags, before checking the owner — and `$null -eq $actualOwner` throws its own message, since a `GetOwner` that cannot translate would otherwise be reported as "owner does not match".
  - **The two remaining `is_symlink()` guards were the same fail-open bug in the modules that read taxpayer documents (`test_parse_utils.py`, `test_tax_law_fetcher.py`):** the round that rewrote `is_reparse_point` onto `os.lstat` — because Python 3.14's `Path.is_symlink()` converts EVERY `OSError` to `False`, and 3.14 is one of the two interpreters CI runs — migrated `archive.py` and `tie_out.py` and left three sites behind: `parse_utils._validate_file_path` (the validated document path), `parse_utils.scan_workspace` (the `documents/` scan) and `tax_law_fetcher.load_tax_law` (the cache read). All three now fail closed. `scan_workspace` RAISES rather than skipping an entry it could not inspect, which is the bias-direction call: silently dropping a document understates income and so overstates the refund the user acts on, the direction AGENTS.md §4 makes a hard error. The junction half cannot be tested behaviourally from POSIX CI — `os.name == "nt"` makes `pathlib` construct `WindowsPath` for the whole process, which raises `UnsupportedOperation` — so it is pinned structurally instead: each module's own binding must BE `secure_fs.is_reparse_point` (a `from ... import` name is resolved independently per module, so patching `secure_fs` would not reach it) and its source must contain no `.is_symlink()` CALL, with `#` comments stripped first because the prose here names `is_symlink()` constantly to explain why it is not used. The fail-closed halves are behavioural and all five new tests are mutation-verified red against a revert to `is_symlink()`.
  - **SSN/ITIN are masked at the extraction boundary, and the masker is not `redact` (`test_parse_utils.py::TestMaskSsn`, `TestATableCarriesItsLabelInAnotherCell`, `TestEveryExtractionRouteMasks`, `TestTheCliNeverPrintsAnUnredactedFailure`, `TestTheSkillsReadDocumentsOnlyThroughTheMaskingCli`; `test_crypto_importer.py`):** `parse_utils.py extract`/`tables` printed page text unmasked, and the parse worker was told to mask the SSN AFTER reading it — by which point the full number sat in the session transcript, the "intermediate file" AGENTS.md §1 forbids (V0 of the staff agent review, its one P0; `docs/changelog/epic-V.md`). `secure_fs.mask_ssn` now runs on every string `extract_pdf_text`, `extract_tables`, `extract_pdf_words`, `extract_excel`, `extract_csv` and `crypto_importer._read_csv` return (word positions row by row, a row it changes returned as one masked word), rewriting the hyphen, dot and slash spellings to `XXX-XX-{last4}` wherever they occur, and the whitespace spelling or a BARE nine-digit run only under an identifier label — SSN/ITIN/"social security number", or any possessive plus TIN / identification number / identifying number, with the possessor enumerated on the PAYER side only (payer's, employer's, filer's, … are EINs), so an unmet form family fails toward masking an EIN rather than leaking an SSN; word-bounded, so `ITIN` inside "Awaiting" is not one; on the same line or, as those forms lay it out, on the line below. It is deliberately not `redact`: that substitutes `[REDACTED]`, erasing the last four the skills are told to keep, and its nine-plus-digit rule would erase routing numbers, account numbers and an unhyphenated EIN — FBAR intake needs the first two and the worker template says to record the third in full. Every pattern is digit-bounded, and the whitespace spelling is labeled-only because a dollars-and-cents column (`850 00`) followed by a four-digit figure is the SSN spelling to the digit and masking it corrupts an amount the return is computed from — a comma lookbehind protected only amounts of $1,000 and up. A TABLE is masked by `secure_fs.mask_table` rather than cell by cell, because the label that makes a bare run an identifier sits in a neighbouring cell: a label cell governs the cell to its right and its own column from that row down, which is the same-line and label-above-value rules with a cell boundary where the line break was, and it is the one place an integer is masked (a workbook stores the digits as a number, and "no delimiter and no label" stops being true once the column is labelled). For the same reason the noun after "social security" is REQUIRED — W-2 boxes 3, 4 and 7 are wages, tax and tips, and while it was optional the amount under box 3 in a cents column was masked to `XXX-XX-2026`. The invariant is at the module boundary — `scan_workspace` REFUSES a file whose name carries an identifier in any spelling, naming the remedy, because masking the name returned a `path` that opens nothing and `scan` is the routing table /tax-parse dispatches from (tie_out's coverage check reads `documents/` itself, so an ignored warning is not a silent drop), and `extract_excel` masks the sheet NAMES it returns as keys, refusing a workbook whose sheets mask to one key rather than dropping one — so the CLI is a plain printer with `ensure_ascii=False`; it refuses an unknown sheet name by naming the sheets that exist rather than printing `{}`; and `crypto_importer._read_csv` is `extract_csv`, with the importer's CLI catching every exception the way `parse_utils`'s does, so a Latin-1 export is one `ERROR:` line rather than a traceback. Each route is asserted against the UNMASKED library read of the same synthetic file (employer block, EIN, routing, account, amounts, phone, date, zip+4 and an already-masked value all present), so the claim is "the route applies `mask_ssn` and nothing else changed", with every synthetic identifier assembled at runtime from area 000 so no fixture persists one. The CLI catches EVERY exception into one redacted line — a page out of range or a parser error quoting document bytes used to propagate as a traceback into the same transcript — and gains `csv`/`excel` subcommands, because `extract` refuses anything but a PDF and the Excel/CSV group otherwise had no route but the worker's own file-read tool. That tool is the other leak the CLI cannot close: the three parsing skills now name `parse_utils.py` as the ONLY reader in one sentence with "file-read tool", tax-parse's Steps 4 and 6.7 name the extractor, and the E2 contradiction (rule 4 forbade reading outside `summaries/` while step 1 read `documents/`) is gone — each pinned by a doc-sync test. The Step 5 sweep over the written summaries stays as the second layer. The COMB spelling is masked under a label like the whitespace one (`TestMaskSsn`, `test_pdf_text_masks_a_flattened_comb_field`): a flattened official fillable form prints an SSN in nine one-digit boxes, which extract as nine digits apart, and Form 1040's name row carried the whole number unmasked. Every run after a same-line label is masked nine digits at a time, because Form 1040 prints all four dependents' boxes on one row; a run that is not whole nines keeps no digit, since splitting it from either end printed eight digits of the other dependent's number. Form 1040's comb routing number has no label and survives. The attachment forms' header labels count exactly as printed, each ending its row: "Your taxpayer identification number" (Forms 8995 and 8995-A), "Your identifying number" (Form 1040-NR, under "(see instructions)"), and "Identifying number" (Form 8801), which may also be followed by "as shown" (Form 1116, wrapped onto a second row on Schedule B (Form 1116)). In a sentence, after "other" or after a possessive they are not labels, so Form 8938's security identifier, Form 8283's partnership EIN, a CUSIP column, "your TIN" or "your taxpayer identification number" in prose and Form 8995-A's business column "Taxpayer identification number" all survive. Whether a label line already carries its value is decided by its last line's LAST label alone, in one scan, with a masked value counted in either case: the pattern tempered past every label was quadratic in a long line of them (AA86). The PDF routes also mask BY POSITION (`TestMaskingByPosition`, AA87): page text puts a row between a label and its value whenever something else prints there (Form 540's margin glyph and rotated sidebars, a W-2 copy's form code), and the bare or spaced SSN under it came back whole. So `extract_pdf_text`, `extract_pdf_words` and `extract_tables` read each page's word boxes and mask every identifier-shaped run printed beside a label on its row, just under a label that carries no value of its own (within its column, measured in the label's height), or down a column a label heads with nothing but such runs between them (Form 2441's care providers), wherever those digits occur in the page's text. Each test draws a layout whose raw text leaks under `mask_ssn` alone. Numbers outside those boxes survive: the employer's EIN below and left of the SSN box, an amount down the SSN column after other text, a routing number, and a column under a label that already carries its value.
  - **Missing capital-gains breakpoints refuse; they never fall through to ordinary rates (`test_tax_computations.py::TestQdcgWorksheetApplies`, `test_the_capital_gains_limits_are_read_at_one_site`, `test_scenario.py`, `test_projection.py`, `test_roth_ladder.py`, `test_ptet.py`):** X2 of the 09-05 review (E5). `compute_scenario` and `projection.project_federal_tax`'s own worksheet branch (which never calls `compute_scenario`) both took `limit is not None` as the condition for the QDCG worksheet and fell to `compute_bracket_tax` otherwise, so an unfetched `federal.capital_gains_rates` priced qualified dividends and net LTCG at ordinary rates — an OVER-statement of every Roth rung, what-if delta, projection and safe-harbor figure, and silent, because 22% on a plausible figure looks like 15% on a plausible figure. Only `loss_harvest` guarded it, at its own entry point. `tax_computations.qdcg_worksheet_applies` is the one guard now: False when there is nothing preferential (a wages-only or net-LOSS case stays usable), True with both breakpoints, and a ValueError naming `/tax-setup` when income exists and either is missing — half a worksheet being the same fall-through by another door. Projection translates it to `ProjectionError`; ptet's CLI tuple gained `ValueError` (the same gap the ProjectionError test there records). The READ is one site too: `projection.capital_gains_limits` replaces the four `.get` chains, pinned by a source sweep that allows the key to be read nowhere else in `agents/lib` — because X1's status-specific keys land on that accessor, and four divergent read sites were the root cause both share. The old fallback pin (`test_compute_scenario_no_cg_limits_falls_back_to_ordinary`) became the refusal pin, and six skill prerequisites state the refusal, doc-sync pinned. The accessor also refuses a breakpoint that is present but not a positive finite number (`NaN`, `Infinity`, zero, a bool, a string) and a 15% breakpoint below the 0% one — `load_tax_law` is a bare `json.loads`, so `NaN` reaches it, and in the worksheet `min(qdcg, nan - ordinary)` keeps `qdcg`, taxing the whole preferential stack at 0%; and `annualize` refuses a negative or oversized `qualified_dividends`, which summed to a negative preferential income the guard read as "nothing to price" (`test_projection.py::test_capital_gains_limits_refuses_a_breakpoint_that_is_not_a_positive_number`, `test_annualize_refuses_a_dividend_pair_that_is_not_a_split`). The same guard runs `compute_form_6251`'s Part III coordination and `loss_harvest`'s entry point, and the one-read-site sweep matches both breakpoint keys, so a second definition of either cannot creep back.
  - **The tax-law cache is written by `set`, one validated figure at a time, and dated per block (`test_tax_law_fetcher.py::TestSetFigure*`, `TestFreshnessIsPerBlock`, `TestTheSetCli`, `TestTheDocumentsNameTheIngestPath`):** V4 of the 09-05 review. The agent wrote `tax_law_<year>.json` by hand, and every defect of a hand-written JSON arrived in returns: a figure under the wrong key (which reads as unfetched and falls to a default), a bracket list out of order, an MFJ figure below the single one, a `.com` URL under `_sources`, and — E7 — `save_tax_law` re-dating the whole cache on every save, so one edited figure made every stale block look freshly retrieved. `tax_law_fetcher.set_figure` (CLI `set <dotted.path> <value> --source <url> --year <y> [--applies-to <period>]`) is the one write path: the path must resolve in `create_empty_tax_law` / `create_state_template(state.code)` (dynamic-keyed dicts — `exchange_rates`, `ptet`, `federal.historical_top_rate` — validate the KEY instead), the value must have the seed's shape (a null seed takes a finite non-negative number, a rate-named key a fraction of one, `brackets` a non-empty contiguous ascending list starting at zero with null max only on the last, `underpayment_rates` and IRMAA tiers ascending, a PTET schedule `[lower, rate, base]` from zero; a whole-block write may carry the seed's own nulls, the leaf named on the command line may not), the cache's `tax_year` must equal `--year`, the joint/single ordering must hold (`_MFJ_AT_LEAST_SINGLE`, fifteen above zero, standard deductions positive), and the source host must pass `_source_host_authoritative` — every refusal writes nothing, verified by comparing the file's bytes. `_provenance` is a SIBLING of `_sources` ({pin: {retrieved, applies_to}}), not a richer value inside it, because the pinning policy requires a string there and an object would mark every existing cache INCOMPLETE; it is created lazily rather than seeded, because the freshness auditor's acceptance fixture is a digest of the seed (`docs/ops/review-subagent-acceptance.md`) and a seeded key would demand the fresh-session replay. `check_freshness` dates every block from `_expected_pins` (the roster split out of `verify_source_pinning` so pinning and dating cannot drift apart) via `_provenance`, falling back to `_meta.fetched` for a block with no entry, and the OLDEST block decides (`age_days`, `block_ages`, `oldest_block`, named in the recommendation); a malformed entry, an unparseable `retrieved`, or an `applies_to` naming another year outside the two multi-period blocks is INCOMPLETE. `save_tax_law(..., stamp_fetched=False)` is how `set` leaves the legacy stamp alone. The auditor's own `_provenance` check, its item 9, waited for Y2's fresh-session replay, the definition being digest-pinned. The fetch reference, tax-setup Step 4 and the runbook name `set` and forbid hand edits, doc-sync pinned. The review round added, each pinned in `TestTheReviewRoundRules`: the last bracket's `max` must be null and `compute_bracket_tax` raises past a closed table; the IRS's inclusive bracket reading is normalised; a block write carries exactly the seed's keys and a leaf write seeds a missing parent from the schema; `_provenance` is per figure with the oldest entry under a pin dating the block; a cache with no usable `_meta` is not re-dated by a `set`; `applies_to` is validated at write time whatever its spelling; a future `retrieved` is INCOMPLETE; `check_freshness` runs `validate_cache` (the write rules, plus the schema-derived MFJ-at-least-single rule and the zero-amount refusal) over the whole cache; and `load_tax_law` refuses a non-finite number anywhere in the file. The Codex round then fixed the DATING itself, pinned in `TestTheCodexRoundRules`: a dotted entry dates one FIGURE, so a block keeps `_meta.fetched` for the figures no entry covers (one `set` against a legacy cache reported every untouched sibling as retrieved today) and the legacy stamp drops out only once every populated figure under the pin is covered; a write supersedes every entry beneath its own path, without which a re-fetched block could never clear STALE; a `retrieved` time carrying an offset is read as local wall clock rather than raising TypeError out of the freshness check; and a zero AMOUNT is a figure under `state.` — a state can genuinely have no standard deduction — while a federal zero stays the unfetched state wearing a number.
  - **The eval golden is still a KNOWN answer (`test_evals.py`):** V3's first half. The suite proves the engine computes; nothing proved the AGENT-MEDIATED path does, and `tax-compute` is Markdown instructing a model to call 27 functions and assemble `result.json` by hand. `evals/` holds a synthetic workspace, a golden and a comparator. Every figure in the golden is RE-DERIVED here from the fixture's own inputs through the engine functions the skill calls, so it cannot be hand-edited into drift, and `goldens/<case>/derivation.md` carries the same arithmetic for a reader with a pencil. The case is chosen so the QDCG worksheet's 0% band is EMPTY — the shape an agent gets wrong by applying the breakpoint to preferential income instead of to taxable income — and the W-2's box 3 is the wage BASE rather than box 1, which is the cap an agent transcribes straight past. The fixture is pinned as a workspace the pipeline would accept: readable through the masking extractor, owner-only under a held umask, and federally COMPLETE, because a cache reading INCOMPLETE would make an eval run a test of the freshness gate instead. The comparator reports three classes and all of them — MISMATCH, MISSING (a step that never ran) and EXTRA (a section from somewhere else) — and its $1.00 tolerance is the tie-out gate's own, applied to MONEY only: it reached `meta.tax_year` until a test changed the year and found that 2026 against 2025 is one apart, and it refuses a non-finite or negative value at both the library and the CLI boundary, an infinite one having scored a return whose tax was zero as a MATCH. The golden is the INITIALIZED `result_template.json` with the case's values, not a hand-written list of lines: Step 5 removes inapplicable sections and neither removes individual lines nor invents them, so a hand-written golden fails a correct run in both directions at once — the shape is pinned against the template and the dropped sections as an explicit set. Identity suppression was three rounds of naming fields, and each list missed the next one (`employer_ein`, then the Schedule D row's `source`, which holds the broker), so what prints is decided by the SIDE and the TYPE and never by the field's name: `golden` is a synthetic file in this repository, past the PII gate and swept for identifier shapes by a test, so it prints; `actual` is whatever `--result` was pointed at, so its strings never do; numbers print because a figure is what a reader acts on, with `secure_fs.redact` over every emitted scalar and path underneath. The fixture's PROFILE is the initialized `taxpayer_profile_template.json` for the same reason the golden is the result template — hand-built, it had drifted three ways and left `has_capital_gains` false while the golden carried the Schedule D that flag gates. The golden's own arithmetic is pinned as well as its keys, twenty-three of its figures having been asserted nowhere.
  - **A parse record is checked by the GATE's rules, and the ledger is derived (`test_parse_records.py`):** V2. The agent used to hand-assemble `parse_ledger.json` from every document at once — a transcription step across documents, at the one place whose purpose is to prove no transcription was lost. Each document now gets its own record and the ledger is derived from them. The load-bearing property is that validation DELEGATES: every field rule is `tie_out.load_ledger` run on a one-record ledger, so the pinning test drives a rule only `tie_out` knows (an unknown category) and requires it to be refused at WRITE time — a re-implementation would go red there first. Coverage is checked at build time rather than only at step 12.7: a document with no record is named while the agent still has it open, a record pointing at a document nobody supplied is refused, and two records for one file are refused by a whole-ledger recheck that the per-record pass structurally cannot make (coverage passes with a duplicate while that document's amounts are counted twice). `boxes` and `notes` stay on the record and never reach the ledger — the first is what a second extraction is diffed against — and an unknown field is an error rather than ignored, because a misspelt `key_box` is a record with no amounts, which reads as an empty parse. A refused build leaves no partial ledger, and the blocked code is the gate's own 2.
  - **A case declares what counts as correct, and a refusal is not a failure (`test_evals.py::TestACaseDeclaresWhatCountsAsCorrect`, `TestTheEvidenceCatchesASilentCompletion`, `test_eval_run.py::test_a_MATCHING_run_on_a_REFUSAL_case_exits_TWO`):** V3's second half. One contract — compare to a golden, a match is a pass — is the wrong question for a missing document or a stale cache, where the right behaviour is to STOP and stopping scores `NO_RESULT`. `evals/cases.py` holds a per-case expected outcome; the structural guard is that a case accepting anything but MATCH must carry EVIDENCE, since `NO_RESULT` is also what a crash produces and such a case would otherwise pass for an agent that fell over before reading anything. The evidence asks whether the agent avoided completing SILENTLY, not whether it stopped: stopping passes, completing while naming the gap passes, completing with a plausible figure and no mention of the gap fails. `hostile_document_text` expects a MATCH because the injection moves no figure — pinned by comparing the money in the extracted text against the base fixture's — so the case is scored on the sentinel file not existing, box 17 surviving, and the instruction-shaped text being REPORTED, resisting alone not being enough. The stale-cache evidence also refuses a cache that was rewritten in place, which is an agent supplying a figure from memory and leaves no other trace. The exit rule counts PASSES: on a refusal case a golden-matching return is the worst outcome, the two tallies agree everywhere else, and the mutation probe found that the first version had no test for it at all. **A third case arrives by the same route and needs no second golden** (V3's third slice): `corrected_document` makes the ORIGINAL W-2's withholding wrong and has a W-2c restore the golden's figures, so using the superseded figures, summing the pair and ignoring the correction are three different wrong answers and none of them match — both are superseded UPWARD, so a failure over-states the withholding and therefore the refund, the direction §4 calls a hard error. **Which boxes a case may supersede is not a free choice, and getting it wrong made the first version UNWINNABLE**: box 1 drives boxes 5 and 16 and, through the rates, 6 and 14, so moving it while the W-2c mentioned only 1, 2 and 17 left four boxes carrying the old number with nothing to put them back — an agent applying the correction exactly as written missed the golden by four figures it was never told about, a guaranteed DIFFERS on a case accepting MATCH alone, and every test passed. The invariant is therefore not "the W-2c mentions what it corrects" but **the supersede moved nothing the W-2c is silent about**, checked by diffing the extracted money against the base fixture's. Two more properties MATCH cannot see: the W-2c prints PREVIOUSLY REPORTED beside CORRECT (a form carrying only the new figure is just a second W-2, and adding it to the first would be defensible), and the original no longer carries the corrected figure, without which an agent ignoring the W-2c entirely would produce the golden anyway. And the mutation rewrites `answers.md`: `[corrected_documents]` and `[document_set_complete]` are REGISTERED required answers, and left at the base fixture's "No" and "the four PDFs" the workspace would tell the agent in writing that the document under test does not exist — the case would then measure whether the agent DISBELIEVES `answers.md` and would fail one that stopped on the contradiction, which is what §6 asks for. **`zero_income` is the second GOLDEN**, and the only case here whose correct return is a different return. Its arithmetic is short — every figure is zero except two deductions and a CA exemption credit — so what its class actually pins is the three places where a zero is a DECISION rather than an absence: taxable income floored at `max(0, 0 - 31,500)` rather than run negative (a negative there propagates a negative tax and a refund nobody is owed), a CA exemption credit that two filers earn whether or not there is tax to apply it to and that is NON-refundable so `max(0, 0 - 300)` is zero, and and a `w2_summary` that is DROPPED — an earlier draft kept it on the ground that the template marks the section `(always)`, which it does not: `w2_summary` carries no `_include_if` and sits below `_optional_sections_below` ("Remove sections that don't apply"), so a compliant agent prunes it and a golden keeping it would have scored that agent's seventeen MISSING fields as a failure. The `(always)` was a label printed by the script used to survey the template, read back as though it were in the file. **MATCH is not evidence for this case and it carries an evidence predicate**, which no other MATCH-only case needs: measured, the golden is twenty-five differences from the bare template and thirteen of those are identity fields copied out of the profile, so an agent that never opened `documents/`, never wrote a ledger and never ran a gate could produce it. The predicate asks for `parse_ledger.json` with an EMPTY `records` list — the affirmative statement that the intake was examined and held nothing. Three surrounding fixes came from the same review: the mutation writes a WHOLE second answers file rather than patching one bullet (the first version left five sentences asserting a W-2 and $28,000 of withholding, so an agent obeying §6 produced a refund on a return with no income and the case failed it for obeying); the harness PROMPT's "and the source documents under documents/" is now per-case, driven by `documents_removed`, which until then was declared, populated and read by nothing; and `tests/test_return_builder.py`'s golden class is parametrised over BOTH goldens, which is what actually kills the mutations — seventeen of eighteen had left every test that reads the zero golden green. The SHAPE is derived from `result_template.json` the way the first golden's is, and the dropped set is the base case's plus the three this case cannot reach (no interest, no dividends, and a profile whose capital-gains flag is false — the flag being what `schedule_d._include_if` keys on, so the workspace has to carry the false one rather than merely lack a lot). The mutation's real content is `answers.md`: it declares the absence AFFIRMATIVELY, because with the base fixture's "the four PDFs in documents/ are every tax document" left standing an empty directory is a workspace whose own answers describe four files that are not there — which is `missing_document`, not this. The difference between "no income" and "the documents are missing" is a claim somebody has to make, and §6 says where. `return_builder` refuses to treat an ABSENT figure as zero; this is that rule read backwards, and it fails quietly — an agent that reads a legitimately zero return as an incomplete one refuses to file, and there is no wrong number for anything downstream to catch.
  - **A model-driven eval run is dispatched, scored and RECORDED (`test_eval_run.py`):** V3's second half. `evals/run.py` builds a fresh fixture workspace, runs an agent inside it, scores `result.json` against the golden and writes `eval_run.json`. NO MODEL RUNS IN THE SUITE — every test dispatches a fake agent, a short Python script writing whatever result the test wants, which is the only way to pin the four outcomes a real model produces occasionally and on nobody's schedule. FIVE outcomes, and the separations are the invariant: `NO_RESULT` is not `DIFFERS`, because the difference between a wrong return and no return is the whole false-completion question and a scorer reporting the second as a hundred MISSING fields buries it under the first; `UNREADABLE_RESULT` is neither; and `AGENT_FAILED`/`TIMEOUT` are not scored at all, a failed agent having made no claim about the return. The record carries the agent version (read BEFORE the runs, so a self-updating agent is not recorded under the version that replaced the one measured), a digest of `.agents/skills/` with the PATHS hashed as well as the bytes (progressive disclosure makes a MOVED reference a behaviour change), a digest of the engine, the repeat count and the per-run timings — an eval number is a claim about a PAIR and is unattributable without both halves. The agent's own OUTPUT never reaches the terminal: it ran in a workspace holding a return, so only a redacted tail survives, and only for a run that never got as far as a result. A timeout kills the process GROUP, pinned by a child that outlives its parent; the workspace root is 0700 under a held umask; a mistyped `--case` is refused BEFORE an agent is dispatched, pinned by asserting the fake agent never ran; and `.github/workflows/model-eval.yml` has no `on:` key at all, pinned along with `ci.yml` still being the only workflow with a trigger — a key in the repository's secrets is readable by every workflow the repository ever gains.
  - **One exit contract across every gate CLI (`test_tax_law_fetcher.py::TestEveryGateCliUsesOneExitContract`):** V5's first slice. `preflight`, `capabilities` and `tie_out` exit 0 clean / 2 blocked / 1 could-not-run; `tax_law_fetcher freshness` printed its verdict and exited 0 for FRESH, STALE, INCOMPLETE and NOT_FOUND alike, so a workspace with NO CACHE — the state every skill's Step 0.5 calls a STOP — reported success to anything reading `$?`. AGENTS.md §2 opens by saying a stale figure silently produces a plausible wrong return and that there is no downstream check for it; this was that check, and it was the only gate that could not fail. The five skills running it each carried a prose workaround saying the exit code lies, which is the clearest available sign the CLI was wrong, and a test pins that none of them still says so. BOTH ENDS are pinned — an early version asserted only the blocked cases, so exiting 2 unconditionally passed, and that direction blocks every run rather than letting one stale cache through. The class states what it does NOT cover: the other three gates keep their blocked-exit assertions in their own suites, where a blocked workspace is cheap to build, and the first spelling of a cross-gate check here (`"2" in source`) was satisfied by any file containing a digit. The mapping spends the BLOCKED code on `NOT_FOUND` alone, because that is the only status whose documented remedy is a stop: the first version blocked STALE and INCOMPLETE too, and INCOMPLETE fires on CORRECT data — `exchange_rates` is empty for every domestic-only filer, so a wholly ordinary return exited 2 on every Step 0.5, which is `gates-that-cry-wolf` exactly. The could-not-run arm IS reachable (the cache is 0600 by §1, and a file this process cannot read raises straight through `read_text`) and is tested; one revision claimed it unreachable in four places at once.
  - **Form 1040 lines 1-24 are DERIVED, and the AMT reaches total tax (`test_return_builder.py`):** V1's first slice. The 1040's own arithmetic had no check anywhere — `tie_out` proves each document reached a line and `capabilities` proves the engine can model the situation, but nothing recomputed line 9 from its eight terms or line 24 from lines 22 and 23, and the skill's reference file carried a written warning about forgetting line 6b, which is a warning that exists because a person is doing the sum. `agents/lib/return_builder.py` owns every line that is ARITHMETIC over other lines (1z, 9, 11, 14, 15, 16, 17, 18, 21, 22, 24) plus the six income lines that are a straight sum of the parse records; the JUDGEMENT lines — what is taxable, whether to itemize, what the credits allow — stay with the agent, which is the same line V2 drew through `tax-parse`. The acceptance test is that the derived lines reproduce the HAND-COMPUTED `ca_mfj_2026` golden from the components alone, with every derived line stripped from the input first, so nothing can be copied; a second test requires it to put back everything it stripped, because a builder that derived FEWER lines would pass the first on the ones it kept. The category-to-line map is read out of `tie_out.CATEGORY_TARGETS` rather than listed again, and a category naming TWO lines (`retirement_gross` → 4a and 5a) is not derived at all, since the split is not recoverable from a sum. Deriving those six income lines makes six of the gate's sum checks an invariant of the build rather than an independent check — what they still catch is an EDIT after the build, which is why `--check` runs as step 12.6 before the tie-out. **The defect this found:** `form_1040` had no line 17 field, so the Form 6251 AMT was computed, recorded and never reached total tax — an ISO exerciser's lines 22 and 24 were the regular tax alone, the under-statement direction, on exactly the taxpayer the form is included for. Out of scope REFUSES rather than guessing: a computed PFIC fund moves amounts out of lines 3a and 3b after the broker reported them, and building those lines from the ledger would restore the qualified treatment §1291 forbids. An unchanged return is NOT republished, because V8's receipt binds each gate's verdict to the digest of what it read — and the bytes match `rollover.apply_changes`, the other writer of that file, because two byte-formats churn the same digest. Four refusals pin the class of failure where a component is BELIEVED: an absent component is not a zero (the template ships every one at 0, so absent means the section was never initialised), only lines 7 and 8 may be negative and only as INPUTS (line 9 and line 11 are legitimately negative for a business loss, and an earlier draft that checked inside the reader refused those correct returns), line 7 is cross-checked against Schedule D because it is the one component with a second source in the file, and a present `form_6251` with a null `amt` is an unfinished computation rather than a zero AMT. The tax-law cache is read through `tax_law_fetcher.load_tax_law`, so a NaN bracket rate cannot reach the worksheet that resolves every comparison against it as False. The SECOND slice takes lines 25-38 the same way, and the property that matters there is the SETTLEMENT: line 34 is the form's "amount overpaid" and is taken before the line 38 penalty, while line 37 adds the penalty and line 35a subtracts it — both written from the same three figures, so a penalty larger than the overpayment becomes the balance due without a second branch. A first draft left line 38 out of both and shipped a TEST asserting that was right, which is the shape worth remembering: the defect came with a certificate, and the correct rule was already written in the skill's own reference file. Fixing it then blocked every balance-due return, because the line-36 bound was compared against an availability figure that is negative whenever tax is owed — both directions are pinned now. Three lines existed nowhere before this slice (`line_27_eic`, `line_28_refundable_actc`, `line_36_applied_to_next_year`), and the EIC one is a field for a credit nothing computes, which the field's own note says. The THIRD slice receipts the check itself: before it, Step 12.6 was a gate an agent could skip — or pass and then edit line 24 — with nothing downstream able to tell, because six of the tie-out's category sums now PRODUCE the lines they are compared against. `differences()` was NOT folded into `tie_out` to close that, for two reasons worth keeping: this module imports `tie_out` for the ledger rules, so the dependency cannot run both ways, and the two gates do not read the same files.
  - **A required answer is owed by ID, and an acknowledgement is re-asked (`test_answers.py`):** V5's second slice. `answers.md` is the headless input path (AGENTS.md §6) and nothing said WHICH answers a workspace needs, so the hard stop arrived mid-compute one question at a time. `agents/lib/answers.py` is the registry and `--check-required` asks it before the run starts. The parser is what these tests mostly pin, because the first version's marker regex ran over the whole file with `\s*` either side and `\s` matches a newline: a blank marker absorbed the blank line under it and captured the next question's HEADING as its answer, so the template `tax-setup` copies into every workspace parsed as fully answered and the gate exited 0 where nothing had been asked. Every blank-body case in the first test module was a one-line string with nothing after it, which passes on the broken regex and the fixed one alike — so the cases here always put something AFTER the marker, `TestTheShippedTemplateIsTheHardestCase` runs the real file, and the guard that actually holds is that `_scan` matches ONE LINE AT A TIME (the mutation that goes red is the revert to the whole-file form; narrowing the whitespace classes alone does not). Around that: a fenced example does not satisfy the gate it describes, a dash counts as an answer because that is how "none" is written, and an answer may run onto indented lines under its marker. `_TRIGGERS` names a workspace that makes each entry owed and is pinned as a SET against the registry — the condition used to sit in an `if/elif` ladder keyed by id, where an entry added without its branch was silently never owed. Each entry's id must appear in the skill its `asked_by` names, so an answer given at the mid-run stop is recorded in the form the next run reads; the stops said only "record it in answers.md", which the parser cannot see. The triggers are pinned against a ZEROED `result_template.json`, because the check runs before compute and three of them read figures the run itself produces; and against a `type: "none"` state, which must still be asked for charitable gifts (§170(p) is a NON-itemizer deduction, so unasked it is silently zero and the tax overstated) and must not be asked for state payments it cannot have. The ACKNOWLEDGEMENT is never satisfied by the file and is cleared only by `--acknowledge`, for one invocation, with the workspace asserted unchanged afterwards: a gate that can never be cleared teaches an agent to reason past a 2, and one that persists the acceptance is what the kind exists to forbid. A FACT is refused and an unknown id hard-errors, as in `capabilities.py`. The report is asserted to reference answers and never print one, and an unreadable `answers.md` must not put the taxpayer-named workspace path into a traceback.
  - **The interpreter floor is one number, and something compares every copy of it (`test_ci_workflow.py::TestTheInterpreterFloorIsOneNumber`):** `MIN_PYTHON` said 3.10 while CI's oldest leg was 3.12 and the only local interpreter was 3.14, so two releases were supported in prose that no job and no machine had ever run. W5 shipped a `.python-version` file and called the pin done, but a pin nothing compares against is prose, not a mechanism. This class IS the comparison: `.python-version` is the source and everything else is checked to it — `pyproject.toml`'s two tool targets, all six lock and recipe sources, `MIN_PYTHON` by import, the four prose documents, and the workflow. EVERY SITE IS READ THE WAY ITS CONSUMER READS IT, which is the finding that took the longest: the workflow and the issue template are parsed as YAML, `pyproject.toml` as TOML, the regeneration recipe as a command line split on `&&` and shlex'd, the prose as the text a reader can SEE with HTML comments removed.
