Regexes stood at every one of those and **thirty-one mutations passed them** — a setup step spelled `${{ matrix.python-version == '3.13' && '3.14' || matrix.python-version }}`, which contains the substring and resolves to the same interpreter on both legs; `if: false` on the JOB, where only step conditions were read; `MIN_PYTHON = (3, 12)` beside `MIN_PYTHON_STR = "3.13"`, which left both the floor test and the refusal test self-consistent at a version the locks are not resolved for. Four small tables carry what cannot be derived — which leg each named step belongs on, which sentences state the floor, which state the matrix, and the four documents that may name NO interpreter version but the floor and the legs. That last one is deliberately spelling-independent rather than pattern-widened: four contradictory second claims (`3.12 or later`, `Requires **Python 3.12**+`, the same sentence broken over a soft wrap) each passed a widened pattern, and widening buys one round.
  - **Every CI trigger has a scan range, and Windows is exercised rather than assumed (`test_ci_workflow.py`, `test_secure_fs.py::TestTheWindowsAclIsActuallyRestrictive`):** W6. `github.event.pull_request.base.sha` is EMPTY on every event but one, so the PII gate's inlined range would have become `...HEAD` the moment CI gained a push or a schedule; the range comes from `scripts/ci-pii-range.sh` now, and the load-bearing test is bidirectional — every trigger the workflow declares must have an arm in the script, because nothing else connects the two files. The script's contract is the gates' own: 0 with a range, 3 for "this event introduces no commits", 1 for "cannot be determined", never a fallback range. Its exit-code mapping is RUN against a stub rather than substring-matched, because the substring form passed against a `case` that mapped 3 to `exit 1`. THE LOCKS are three and the split is structural: resolving pytest beside pip-audit landed `charset-normalizer` past the runtime pin and `parse_utils` refused to parse, so a test pins that the pair a test job installs share NO package at all — not merely that they agree about a shared one, which an empty intersection makes unfailable and which agreement today does not survive a transitive bump — and another pins that every hashed lock is in the PII gate's digest exemption — that exemption is by exact PATH, so a sibling lock blocks its own commit and the documented way out is `--no-verify`. ON WINDOWS: thirty tests carry an explicit `posix_only` skip and the ACL is asserted by reading it back with `icacls`, a different tool from the one that wrote it — every previous Windows assertion either stubbed `subprocess.run`, proving the script was ASSEMBLED rather than effective, or read back a POSIX mode, so a green job would have proved PowerShell was invoked and nothing else. Two shipped defects came out of the first runs and are pinned: text I/O in `agents/` and `evals/` must name `encoding="utf-8"` (the platform default is cp1252 and this repo's own files are not), and the DACL must be set through `GetAccessControl`/`SetAccessControl` with the execution policy bypassed — that last guard sits OUTSIDE the Windows-only class on purpose, because its absence is invisible on POSIX and fatal on Windows.
  - **The parse boundary is drawn by ABSENCE, and every CLI read is confined (`test_claude_agents.py::TestTheParseWorkerBoundaryIsDrawnByAbsence`, `test_parse_utils.py::TestEveryCliReadIsConfinedToAWorkspace`):** V6. AGENTS.md §1's "no network, write nothing outside `summaries/`" for a document-parsing sub-task was six numbered rules in a prompt — a request to the one party an injected document is built to subvert. Measured on the installed build: the `tools:` allowlist IS enforced (a sub-agent without a write or network tool reported having none rather than declining), `Read` is NOT directory-confined (the same agent read `/etc/hosts` and a file above its workspace, silently), `PreToolUse` hooks fail OPEN, and `permissions` rules are session-wide. One mechanism fails closed — not granting the capability — so `tax-parse-worker` holds nothing that reads, writes, runs or fetches, the parent extracts and hands over the masked text, and the grant is pinned as a CLOSED set rather than another forbidden list. The half no grant could supply is the COMMAND: `parse_utils`'s CLI passed `None` for the `workspace=` its library functions have always taken, so "run only parse_utils" was permission to read the whole disk one suffix at a time — `csv ~/other-return/documents/bank.csv` printed another taxpayer's statement and `scan /` mapped every tax document on the machine. Every route is confined now, defaulting to the working directory, with no way to switch it off, and one test per route because four of five being right is a hole. `.claude/agents/` gains a second acceptance REGIME with a test requiring every definition to be in exactly one: a reviewer's judgement can only be checked by planting a defect, while a worker's boundary is a property of its grant and is therefore checked on every run.
  - **The workspace is one directory, named in one place (`test_workspace.py`):** Y4. Every CLI took a `--workspace`, every skill passed `.`, and `run.sh` resolves from the repository — so the workspace WAS the checkout, and a development clone and a live return shared a directory that three separate mechanisms (the `.gitignore` list, `install.sh`'s `IGNORE_PATTERNS`, the pre-commit PII scan) existed to keep apart. `TAX_WORKSPACE` names it instead; the precedence is explicit flag, then the variable, then `.`, and that last step is the whole of the backwards compatibility. The CWD stays the CLONE — `run.sh` is a relative path and the engine's venv lives there — so the variable moves the data, never the code. Two sweeps are pinned STRUCTURALLY rather than case by case, because both failures are invisible from outside: a CLI that goes back to `Path(args.workspace)` still works through the flag and silently loses the variable, and a path ARGUMENT (`--result result.json`) resolved against the CWD names a file in the clone, which `_validate_subpath` then refuses for being outside the workspace — a confusing way to say "you are in the wrong directory". That second rule already existed as `_resolve_under` in `scenario.py`, known to two planning modules and unknown to the six others that needed it. The one flag that is deliberately NOT the taxpayer workspace is `preflight --workspace`, which names the CODE root it resolves `.venv` against; pointed at the variable it would certify a run against an interpreter the engine is not installed in, and the distinction is asserted from both sides. `WorkspaceError` is its own class and not `FileNotFoundError`: the builtin is an `OSError`, which half these CLIs already catch, and widening the other half would have turned every unrelated IO fault into a one-line message instead of a traceback.
  - **The instruction files carry the alarms, not the answers (`test_portability.py::TestInstructionFiles`):** Y5. AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md are 39 KB loaded into every session before any skill, and the only control on that was a byte cap sitting 10 bytes above the file — so a five-line addition for an unrelated change had to evict three passages of live content, and which safeguard died was decided by whoever was editing, at the end of a task about something else. A cap can only force a choice; these tests are what make the right choice the cheap one. `AGENTS_MAX_BYTES` is now a BUDGET rather than the cliff — `test_the_agents_cap_reserves_room_under_the_truncation_limit` pins that the cap leaves at least an eighth of Codex's 32 KiB chain limit unspent, because the chain also carries a nested AGENTS.md and the user's own global instructions, which nobody here writes and which a cap set AT the limit would let spend the whole budget silently. CLAUDE.md gained a BYTE cap beside its line cap, the line cap alone being gameable by exactly the edit most likely to try it: every line is wrapped prose, so a section re-flowed onto fewer, longer lines sheds no weight while the count drops. The other three are about REACHABILITY, which is the half of a relocation that is easy to lose — moving text out is one edit and linking it is another, and only the first is visible in the diff you were reading. `test_the_contributing_index_is_complete` iterates the DIRECTORY, not a hand-kept list, and requires every file in `docs/contributing/` to appear in AGENTS.md §9's index; it is scoped to §9 by string partition, and that scoping is the whole test, because the first version searched the entire file, found `pii-gate.md` linked from §1's own bullet, and read a deleted index row as present — a mutation probe that came back VACUOUS. `test_every_relocation_pointer_resolves` derives its roster by regex over both instruction files and checks each target exists, with `RELOCATED_BEFORE_Y5` as a FLOOR under it: a derived roster fails by SHRINKING invisibly, so a regex that stops matching must be a failure rather than a clean pass over nothing. And `test_each_relocation_is_linked_from_the_file_it_left` covers what the index does not — an agent editing a hook reads the Hooks section, not the documentation index, so the section a file LEFT has to point at where it went.
  - **The parser's budgets bound what the file size does not (`test_parse_utils.py::TestTheParserBudgets`):** V11. The 100 MB cap bounds the FILE; a `.xlsx` is a ZIP whose XML can be orders of magnitude larger, and openpyxl decompresses the whole archive into memory before this module sees a cell. The uncompressed size AND the ratio are checked from the central directory before `load_workbook` (a bomb is small on disk, which is the trick, so an absolute cap alone lets a 100 KB file through); extracted characters and elapsed time are charged incrementally per page, per row and per cell, because a check that only runs at the end has already paid the cost it exists to avoid; and `MAX_PAGES` bounds what ONE CALL extracts rather than what the file holds, since /tax-parse splits a long document into per-page sub-tasks and a file-level cap would refuse every slice. Every budget REFUSES rather than truncating and returns nothing on refusal — a partial extraction that looks complete is what the tie-out gate structurally cannot see — and `ParseBudgetError` subclasses `ValueError` so the handlers that already cover the size cap cover it too. The residual is stated in the code: a crafted archive can understate its own sizes, and the output budget is what catches that.\n  - **One writer for every runtime artifact (`test_secure_fs.py::TestTheOneWriter`):** V10's first half. Five modules had hand-rolled a temp-then-rename and two wrote IN PLACE — `scenario.py` and `tie_out.py` opened the real target with `O_TRUNC`, so an interrupted write left a truncated scenario, or a truncated tie-out report, under the name the next reader trusts (and that report is what V8's receipt binds a filing package to). `secure_fs.atomic_path`/`atomic_write`/`write_secure` keep, once, what the copies had earned separately: 0600 BEFORE the first byte via `secure_fd` on the descriptor (a file created at the umask default holds its contents before anything tightens it, and on Windows has no ACL until the call returns) — pinned by reading the mode at the moment of the RENAME, which is the only point where the pre-fix window is observable, with umask 022 held for the duration or the assertion is vacuous; a unique temp in the TARGET's directory, so the rename is atomic on one filesystem and two runs cannot collide; and the temp removed on ANY failure, so a half-written file never survives under a name something else reads. `rollover.apply_changes` WAS the exception — two files as one transaction with a rollback, which a single-file helper cannot express — and V10's second half closed it with `secure_fs.atomic_paths` (`test_secure_fs.py::TestTheOneWriterCoversTheTwoFileTransaction`). Routing it fixed three defects the local copy had: a FIXED `.tmp` name beside the target, so two applies running at once wrote the same scratch file and one published the other's bytes; a rollback that used `write_bytes`, which is not atomic, so a crash during the restore truncated the file the restore existed to protect (it renames back a hardlink taken before the first publish now); and a handler that read the target back to decide whether to restore, so a read that itself failed raised out of the handler and skipped the rest of the cleanup. The residual is named in the helper rather than implied: POSIX has no multi-file atomic rename, so between the first rename and the last there is a window a CRASH leaves half-applied. An EXCEPTION in that window is handled; nothing in user space closes the other, and what covers it in practice is that the caller re-reads both files and re-derives.
  - **The summary PDF is rendered by tracked, tested code (`test_pdf_render.py`):** V9 of the 09-05 review. `/tax-generate` used to instruct the agent to WRITE a `generate_tax_pdf.py` per workspace and run it — gitignored, blocked by name at the PII gate because it carried the taxpayer's name in its own source, and the one artifact in the pipeline with no test (AGENTS.md §8 said to check it by hand). `agents/lib/pdf_render.py` replaces it, and the three rules the skill could only ASK for are now assertions: the shipped all-null `result_template.json` RENDERS (a renderer that reads null as a present value crashes there and on any partly-populated return); the filer's name is read from the result, proved by rendering two names and sweeping the module's own source for both; and no assistant or vendor appears in the PDF metadata or on the page, which every generated return inherits. Page 1 carries `meta.limitations` before any dollar figure, and a `--draft` render stamps NOT VALIDATED in the same box — the CLI refuses (exit 2) on a stale V8 receipt and writes no file, so generation and validation consume the same input generation. A number is an amount unless its KEY says otherwise (years, codes, counts, ratios and rates are keyed out — "Tax year $2,026.00" was the first thing it printed), and an `_include_if` section whose every figure is zero is skipped while a section without that marker prints its zeros, because a $0.00 refund is an answer. The output is owner-only, pinned with umask 022 held for the duration or the assertion is vacuous.
  - **A gate's verdict is bound to the files that gate read (`test_receipt.py`, `test_filing_package.py`):** V8 of the 09-05 review. The capability gate (Step 0.7) and the tie-out gate (Step 12.7) each PASS against the workspace as it stood when they ran, and nothing bound the pass to those inputs — a document arriving afterwards, an edited figure or a re-fetched cache left `/tax-generate --filing-package` assembling a package on gates that never saw what it was packaging. `receipt.record` writes each gate's outcome plus the digest of every file that gate read; `receipt.verify` RE-DERIVES the input map from disk rather than re-hashing the recorded paths, which is the only way an ADDED document is visible, and it reports every reason rather than the first. Per GATE, not per workspace: the capability gate reads the profile and runs before result.json exists, so a result edit does not invalidate it. The invalidation set is the input set and NOTHING WIDER — `answers.md`, `summaries/` and `reviews/` are outside it because /tax-review writes them after the return is computed, and a receipt that fires on that is the cry-wolf gate PR #69 paid for. The engine's identity is a DIGEST over `agents/lib/*.py`, not a version constant nobody remembers to bump. `filing_package` exits 2 on a stale receipt naming each reason, with `--draft` as the banner-marked escape hatch; both gates record AFTER printing their verdict and a receipt-write failure never lowers a verdict's exit code, so a permissions fault cannot mask a tax-correctness one. A FAILED or BLOCKED gate is RECORDED as such — "this gate failed" and "this gate never ran" are both refusals and the user is told which. The THIRD gate is V1's derived-lines check (Step 12.6), added when that slice was receipted: only `--check` records, because the build mode rewrites the very file the receipt binds to, and its input set is the four workspace files it reads — the SOURCE DOCUMENTS belong to the tie-out alone, since an unparsed document cannot have moved a derived line and invalidating on one would fire on correct work. Nothing is lost by that split, because a filing package requires both receipts. The third gate is also the only one with a second acceptable outcome: a return whose lines 1-38 it does not govern leaves it nothing to assert, and recording that as a failure would refuse a filing package for a correct return while recording it as a PASS would claim a check that never happened. It is `OUT OF SCOPE`, accepted for that gate alone — and a pass that verified nothing is carried out loud, as a NOTE from `verify`, into the page-1 limitations box and a NOT FULLY VERIFIED heading on the package. Two decisions that were free while it exited 2 had to change with it: the scope test moved to the END of `check()`, because deciding it first waived every other refusal on that return, and the §1291 predicate reads the whole `form_8621_pfic` section rather than `funds` alone, because the reclassification totals are its SIBLINGS. `receipt.record_after_verdict` holds the rule all three gates had hand-rolled — a receipt that cannot be written never lowers a verdict already printed — and `receipt._tax_year` falls back to the profile, because `result_template.json` ships `meta.tax_year` null and the cache that PRICES LINE 16 was being left out of the input map.
  - **A capital-loss carryforward keeps its character, and rollover's JSON boundary refuses what no transform should see (`test_rollover.py`):** X3 of the 09-05 review (E6). The profile carried ONE combined `prior_year.capital_loss_carryforward` and the compute skill had nowhere to put it — Schedule D line 6 is short-term, line 14 long-term, and short-term loss offsets ordinary income first, so a combined figure cannot be placed without a split nobody can guess. The profile template gains `capital_loss_carryforward_short_term` / `_long_term` (the legacy combined field stays, documented as such), the result template gains `line_6_prior_year_carryover` / `line_14_prior_year_carryover`, and `rollover._capital_loss_carryforward` returns the split when the archive carries the Capital Loss Carryover Worksheet block — `_capital_loss_changes` then writes both split fields and retires a non-zero legacy value — or a combined LOWER BOUND (abs(line 16) − the prior status's cap, $1,500 MFS) as an `ask` on the legacy field when it does not. An explicit zero is distinguished from an absent block: a zero block beside a loss inside the cap is a computed zero (no change), a zero block beside a loss BEYOND the cap is a contradiction (the template ships the block at zero) and is offered for resolution, an absent block is the legacy derivation. `_read_json` walks every parsed document and refuses a non-finite float (`NaN`/`Infinity`/`-Infinity`, and `1e999`, which `json.loads` parses to `inf`) — previously `+inf` passed the explicit block's `v > 0` and a NaN fell to the legacy path where NaN and −inf passed `raw >= 0`, writing `nan`/`inf` carryforwards; `apply_changes` reads its targets through the same boundary. Every transform goes through `_amount`, so a JSON `true` is 0, not `abs(True) == 1`. The compute reference names both split fields and STOPs on a combined-only figure, the setup interview asks for the split, and the rollover skill names the `ask` — doc-sync pinned. The review round added: a gain year is an answer (zeros) that retires a stale split as an `ask`, never silence; a joint-year carryforward is `ask`-only after an MFJ → anything transition (Reg. §1.1212-1(c), Rev. Rul. 74-175); the combined bound asks to zero a split that would disarm /tax-compute's STOP; the contradiction test is a total (a block below abs(line 16) − cap is offered for resolution); the block is bounded above as well as below (a block above abs(line 16), or beside a gain, is a contradiction); a loss inside the cap with no block is a zero bound offered loud; the cap is the engine's constant and an unreadable status keeps the $3,000 cap, the smaller bound, because the bound lands; the joint-to-separate guard fails closed on a null status; the CLI prints a workspace non-finite refusal as one line; and the non-finite walk is `secure_fs.first_non_finite`, shared with the tax-law cache reader. The Codex round then closed the two places an ABSENCE read as an answer: a worksheet block whose amounts were never written (`{}`, or one side only) was taken for a computed zero and retired a live carryforward, so both sides must now be present and numeric for the block to be an answer; and a bound of ZERO could LAND — `apply_changes` writes an `ask`'s prior_value — writing "no carryforward remains" against an archive whose own reason says one may remain, and zeroing a split keyed from the paper worksheet, so a zero bound now carries the profile's own value and proposes no split write at all.
  - **The capital-gains breakpoints are status-specific, read by the full status key (`test_projection.py::test_capital_gains_limits_accessor_reads_the_full_status_key`, `test_project_federal_prices_hoh_on_its_own_breakpoints_or_refuses`, `test_tax_law_fetcher.py::TestTheCapitalGainsBreakpointsAreStatusSpecific`):** X1 of the 09-05 review. §1(h) gives head of household its own 0%/15% breakpoints (Rev. Proc. §1(h) table; TY2025 $64,750 / $566,700 against single's $48,350 / $533,400) and married filing separately half of joint (15% breakpoint $300,000 — far BELOW single's). The schema keyed `zero_pct_limit` / `fifteen_pct_limit` single/mfj only and every reader collapsed HoH and MFS onto `single` through `_binary_status_key`, under-stating HoH's 0% band and over-stating MFS's 15% band — a directional error in both directions. The seed carries all four keys, `projection.capital_gains_limits` (the one read site X2 made) reads `_full_status_key` (QSS → mfj), the `set` sanity table orders them (fifteen above zero per status; HoH between single and joint; MFS never above single), the fetch reference says to populate all four, and AGENTS.md §8's gotcha no longer lists `capital_gains_rates` among the single/mfj-only maps. A legacy cache keyed single/mfj reads `None` for HoH and MFS — never the single figure — and `qdcg_worksheet_applies` refuses to price preferential income on it, naming `/tax-setup`; the five fixture caches gained the two keys so their MFS/HoH cases keep computing. Adding the keys to the seed moved the freshness auditor's acceptance-fixture digest, so both replays were re-run and filed under a new results section in `docs/ops/review-subagent-acceptance.md` (definitions unchanged, which is what makes an in-session dispatch valid for a fixture-only change; the model is recorded, as every post-08-27 verdict must). That replay also caught an unplanted seed defect: `form_8938_thresholds.mfj_us` carried the foreign-resident SINGLE pair ($200K/$300K) instead of the instructions' $100K/$150K, and so did `check_form_8938_required`'s fallback, the FBAR skill and the test fixture — `test_mfj_us_thresholds_are_the_instructions_row_everywhere` now pins all three against a $120K domestic MFJ case that must be required to file. The review round added: §1(h)'s strict and equal relations between the statuses (`TestTheBreakpointRelationsTheStatuteImposes`); the joint ≥ HoH ≥ single ≥ separate chain derived from the schema for every per-status map (`TestTheStatusChainIsDerivedFromTheSchema`); a seed-complete block whose cache leaf differs from the seed reads INCOMPLETE (`TestAStructuralConstantThatDriftedFromTheSeedIsIncomplete` — the doubled Form 8938 pair in a pre-existing cache); and the state standard deduction read by the full status, refusing an unfetched key (`test_state_standard_deduction_reads_the_full_status_and_refuses_an_unfetched_one`). The code review then added: a two-key cache reads INCOMPLETE from `validate_cache` (the walker cannot see an absent key); the state standard deduction takes `single` as the stand-in where a template carries no `hoh` row, never a silent zero; and the FBAR skill's Form 8938 figures are derived from `FORM_8938_THRESHOLDS` by test.
  - **Two `secure_fs` calls in one fenced block, with a file write required between them (`tax-project`, `tax-withholding`, `tax-review`):** the shape was `mkdir -p X && secure_fs X` / `# save the report, then:` / `secure_fs X/report.md`. An agent that runs a fenced block as ONE Bash call reaches the second command before the report exists, `secure_path` hits `FileNotFoundError`, and the block exits 1 — after which the agent either treats securing as broken or writes the report afterwards and never re-runs it, leaving the report at the default mode. Split into two separately-labelled blocks with the reason stated inline, so the failure cannot be reached by following the instructions literally.
  - **A runtime PATH is sensitive data, and the permission layer printed it on every failure (`test_secure_fs.py`):** `secure_fs`'s CLI reported `Could not secure {target}: {e}` with both halves raw. AGENTS.md §1 restricts the runtime DIRECTORIES precisely because an owner-only file inside a listable one still discloses its NAME — under `documents/` and `archive/` those names are a taxpayer's scanned documents — and forbids emitting a full SSN under any circumstance, so a file named after one turned the safeguard into the disclosure. The skill layer invokes that CLI directly and `tax-amend` pipes `find archive/{year}/as_filed` into it, so the message lands in terminal scrollback and in whatever an agent forwards. Redacting the CLI alone would have fixed nothing: `os.chmod`'s own `OSError` stringifies WITH the raw filename, PowerShell quotes the offending path back in its stderr, and `archive.py`/`tie_out.py`/`scenario.py` embed that text verbatim in the `ArchiveError`/`TieOutError`/`ScenarioError` they print. The guarantee is therefore made at the module BOUNDARY — no `OSError` leaves `secure_fs` carrying an unredacted path, which costs the dropped `errno` that no caller reads — and the four caller messages that prepend their own path were redacted too. `redact()` MIRRORS `scripts/pre-commit-pii-scan.sh`'s `PII_PATTERNS` and its deliberately-broad nine-digit sweep; the two cannot share an implementation (that gate is bash at commit time, this is a dependency-free runtime library imported by parse sub-tasks), so the duplication is pinned instead: every SSN spelling that gate blocks is driven through this redactor and must not survive, with `test_an_ordinary_filename_still_prints` as the counterweight — a message that cannot say WHICH path failed is not a message, which is the same trade the shell gate settled on. All four emit sites are mutation-verified: reverting any one to the raw path fails its own test.
  - **`os.lstat()` declines to follow only the FINAL component (`test_secure_fs.py`):** `secure_path()`'s reparse-point refusal inspected the leaf and nothing above it, so a workspace whose `archive/` was a symlink or junction with a REAL `archive/{year}/as_filed` beneath it passed the check and had the chmod / protected DACL applied outside the workspace, past every gitignore and PII rule scoped to it. `/tax-amend` reaches this directly — it passes `archive`, `archive/{year}` and `archive/{year}/as_filed` to the CLI in one call, and pipes `find archive/{year}/as_filed` into a second, where every path `find` yields has a real leaf under a linked ancestor. `_named_components()` now walks every component the CALLER NAMED, outermost first. The scope is deliberate and is the interesting half: a relative path (what every skill passes, from the workspace root) is walked in full from the current directory down, an absolute path the same way when it lies under the current directory, and otherwise the leaf only — walking an unrelated absolute path to the filesystem root refuses correct data, because macOS puts every temporary directory under `/var`, which IS a symlink, and a gate that fires on correct data teaches the caller to route around it (the same lesson `_CHMOD_EXPLANATORY_MARKERS` and the freshness gate above are shaped by). Components are accumulated as WRITTEN, never normalized: `os.path.relpath`/`normpath` collapse `link/../x` to `x`, dropping the very component being inspected, since the kernel resolves a `..` only after traversing what precedes it — `test_a_dotdot_component_is_not_normalized_away` builds a path that escapes the workspace exactly that way and asserts the external directory's mode is untouched. Built on `os.path` string operations rather than `pathlib` because the Windows branch is exercised by patching `os.name`, a SHARED singleton that makes `pathlib` hand back `WindowsPath` and raise `UnsupportedOperation` the moment one is joined on a POSIX host. The CLI also **stops at the first refusal** instead of reporting it and continuing to the next target: continuing is what secured the descendants THROUGH the refused component, and it prints the COUNT of paths not attempted, never their names. The stop is SCOPED to the new `PathRefused` (a component that is a reparse point, or could not be inspected at all) rather than to any `OSError`, and the scope is the interesting half: a refusal says something about the TREE, while a missing file or a denied chmod says nothing about the OTHER targets — stopping there would leave siblings at their default mode over an unrelated error, and `/tax-parse` passes `summaries/*.md result.json`, where an unmatched glob arrives as a literal pattern and `result.json` still has to be secured. `PathRefused` subclasses `OSError` so every library caller that catches and translates is unaffected. Mutation-verified: reverting the walk to `[path_str]` fails four tests, and restoring the continue-on-failure CLI fails two more.
  - **A Windows spelling could switch the ancestor walk off (`test_secure_fs.py::TestAWindowsSpellingCannotSwitchTheWalkOff`):** the containment test that decides whether an absolute path gets the full walk or the leaf-only check was one raw `path_str.startswith(cwd + sep)`, which is case- and separator-SENSITIVE. Windows is neither: from `C:\Work`, `c:/Work/archive/2026/as_filed/W2.pdf` names exactly the path the walk was built for and was judged to be OUTSIDE the current directory, so only its leaf was inspected — `lstat` sees the real file past the junction and `Set-Acl` applies through it. The safeguard turned off by a spelling, on the one platform whose junctions it exists for. `_parts_below()` now compares part-by-part under `_normcase()` (Windows: lowercase + `/`→`\`; POSIX: identity, because `Archive` and `archive` really are two directories there). PART-wise rather than on a normalized prefix STRING, and that is not style: `.lower()` is not length-preserving for every Unicode character, so a normalized prefix's length cannot be used to slice the original. `.` parts are dropped from both sides, `..` is kept and accumulated as written for the reason the bullet above gives. `_normcase`/`_split_path` are keyed on `os.name` rather than on `os.path.normcase`/`os.altsep` — equivalent on both real platforms, but only the former MOVES when a test simulates the other one, which is the difference between a covered branch and a comment. Mutation-verified: restoring the `startswith` prefix test fails three tests. The same class of fix removed `is_reparse_point()`'s `Path(path)` wrap — `os.lstat` takes a string, so the wrap bought nothing and cost the module its last `pathlib` hazard: under a simulated `os.name` a plain string arrived as a `WindowsPath`, reached `lstat` rendered with backslashes, was reported ABSENT, and the caller was certified safe by a lookup that never found anything.
  - **`is_reparse_point()` failed closed LOUDLY, naming the path in the traceback (`test_secure_fs.py::TestNoUninspectablePathNamesItselfInATraceback`):** `os.lstat`'s `OSError` carries its operand in `.filename`, so the propagated text reads `[Errno 13] Permission denied: '/documents/W2_000-00-0000.pdf'`. `secure_path()` translated it; the other three public call sites did not — `parse_utils._validate_file_path`, `parse_utils.scan_workspace` and `tax_law_fetcher.load_tax_law` all call it outside any translating `try`, and the first two run under CLIs that catch nothing. A scanned document's NAME is the disclosure AGENTS.md §1 restricts `documents/` to prevent, and an SSN in a filename is the absolute case. Fixed at the SOURCE rather than at the three reported sites, so the boundary guarantee `secure_path()` already made ("no `OSError` leaves this module carrying an unredacted path") holds for every caller: `_without_the_raw_path()` rebuilds the exception from `(errno, redact(strerror))`, preserving the CLASS so callers that distinguish a `PermissionError` still can. Rebuilding rather than clearing `.filename` in place is required — `OSError.__str__` tests that member for NULL, not for `None`, so assigning `None` renders a literal `: None` and leaves the object claiming a filename it does not have. The structural gate in `TestNoRuntimePathReachesAMessageUnredacted` structurally could NOT catch this one: it reads `raise`/`print` statements for interpolated paths, and here nothing was interpolated — the message was built by the OS and the statement was a bare propagation. Mutation-verified: dropping the `except OSError` clause fails five tests.
  - **A skill that writes taxpayer data without securing it was invisible to a per-CALL pin (`test_secure_fs.py::test_every_tax_skill_is_classified_as_writing_or_not_writing_pii`):** `SENSITIVE_SKILL_SECURITY_CALLS` asserts the exact `secure_fs` invocations a skill must contain, and can only fail on a skill someone listed. `/tax-extend` (`taxpayer_profile.json` + `answers.md`), `/tax-verify` and `/tax-rollover` (`answers.md`) each wrote taxpayer data with NO securing step anywhere in the file, and `/tax-amend` had three `answers.md` writes with none, while SECURITY.md claimed every documented toolkit write went through `agents.lib.secure_fs`. An in-place edit preserves the mode, which is why it went unnoticed; an editor that writes a replacement and renames it over the target does not, and owner-only is not inherited from the file being overwritten. `/tax-harvest` had only the blanket "restrict every manually written file" line three screens below the step it governed, and `/tax-compute`'s Step 0.7 blocked branch writes both files on a path that can END the run before `references/finalize.md`'s securing step is reached. All fixed inline at the write site; the classification is now TOTAL — every `tax-*` skill is either pinned to the calls it must make or named in `SKILLS_THAT_WRITE_NOTHING_SENSITIVE` with the reason, so a new skill cannot be added without answering the question, and a skill in the exemption that invokes `secure_fs` fails too rather than sitting there with a call nobody asserts.
  - **The redactor mirrored two of the gate's three detected families (`test_secure_fs.py`):** `secure_fs.redact()` carried the SSN and prefixed-credential patterns but not `AWS_SECRET_PATTERN`, the keyword-anchored 40-character AWS secret — so a runtime path or a PowerShell diagnostic holding one was BLOCKED at commit time and PRINTED at runtime. Detected-but-not-redacted is the worse half of the pair: it turns a safeguard into the disclosure channel. The parity claim was a comment, which is what let the divergence ship, so it is now a test — `test_the_sample_set_covers_every_family_the_gate_detects` reads the `PII_PATTERNS` array out of `scripts/pre-commit-pii-scan.sh` and fails when a shell variable appears there that this module has no sample (and therefore no rule) for. The bare-nine-digit sweep is deliberately absent from that set: it is token-based rather than a `PII_PATTERNS` member and has its own tests. Substitution ORDER is load-bearing and pinned separately — the AWS value is 40 arbitrary base64 characters, so a digit run inside it matches the SSN or nine-digit rule first, and a value fragmented by an earlier substitution no longer matches the keyword+value form at all, leaving most of the credential in the message. Widest match first — and that rule has TWO instances, not one: `sk-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,}` accepts hyphens, so an API key carrying an SSN-shaped run (`sk-000-00-0000…`) was fragmented by the SSN rule into `sk-[REDACTED]` plus twenty unmasked characters of the credential. Both credential families therefore run before both PII-shape families, which cannot cost anything in the other direction: no SSN spelling contains an `AKIA`/`sk-`/`ghp_`/`github_pat_` prefix, so moving them forward cannot fragment an SSN. Each ordering is pinned by its own test, and the pair is why the rule is stated as a rule rather than as a fact about the AWS pattern. The fixture keyword and its 40-character value are composed at USE, never adjacent on a tracked line, or this file would — correctly — block itself; the `sk-` sample is split for the same reason.
  - **`archive.py` reported the one name it must never print (`test_archive.py`):** every refusal in that module interpolated its path raw — `_refuse_symlink` embedded `src.name`, the destination and dry-run checks their full paths, and the rollback its underlying `shutil.move`/`os.lstat` error text — and the `/tax-archive` CLI prints all of them. An entry under `documents/` (or `archive/{year}/documents/` after the move) is a taxpayer's scanned document whose NAME is the disclosure AGENTS.md §1 restricts those directories to prevent, and the name is untrusted input besides: whatever the taxpayer saved the file as, up to and including an SSN. The OS half of each message is the half that matters — redacting only the interpolated `Path` leaves the raw filename disclosed by the `OSError` text appended after it — so `redact()` wraps both, at every site including the two argv echoes. `test_an_ordinary_name_still_prints` is the counterweight: a refusal that will not say WHICH entry it refused is not actionable. Mutation-verified red by removing `redact` from the module.
  - **A gate that covers seven of twenty-one modules is a gate with a hole next to it (`test_secure_fs.py::TestNoRuntimePathReachesAMessageUnredacted`):** the first version of the walk above enumerated the modules the change happened to touch, and a review pass found `projection` naming a raw workspace path, `tax_law_fetcher.py` naming the cache, and `crypto_importer.py`, `retirement_dashboard.py`, `capabilities.py` and `amend.py` doing the same — forty-eight sites, every one of them the defect the gate was written to prevent, sitting one import away from it. It now derives its module list from `agents/lib/*.py` rather than carrying one. The identifier rule changed with it, twice, and both changes are the point: whole names missed `workspace_dir` and `c.json_path`, raw substrings then flagged `profile_year` (which CONTAINS "file" and is an integer), and the rule that survives matches `_`-separated TOKENS. A bare `name` is deliberately NOT a token — `src.name` is a path component and "bracket name" is a label, indistinguishable by identifier — so the attribute rule catches `<path-ish>.name` and the few bare ones that carry user input are redacted by hand. Enforcing it mechanically would have meant redacting every parameter name in the math engine's validation errors.
  - **SECURITY.md's strongest promise was the one nobody could check (`test_secure_fs.py::TestTheNoNetworkClaimIsTrue`):** the page told a taxpayer that "the only outbound request the code makes is fetching current-year tax law from IRS.gov" — and this code makes NO outbound request at all. `tax_law_fetcher` imports `urllib.parse`, not `urllib.request`: it PARSES the source URLs the agent brings back to decide whether the host is authoritative, and the fetch itself is the AGENT's, following the skill. Getting that backwards misplaces the network boundary in the reader's head, and the boundary is exactly what the next bullet of that page asks them to reason about. The corrected claim is stronger and is now machine-checked — an `ast` walk asserts no module under `agents/` imports a network client, and a text scan asserts no shipped script shells out to `curl`/`wget`/`nc`/`ssh`, with `urllib.parse` explicitly NOT in the banned set and a counterweight test pinning that (a rule that banned it would fire on the source-pinning check the claim depends on).
  - **`/tax-ptet` before `/tax-compute` exited with a traceback (`test_ptet.py`):** `_main` caught `(PtetError, ScenarioError, RolloverError)`, but the `_read_json` it calls is `projection`'s and raises `ProjectionError` — a fourth type, and the one a missing `result.json` produces, which is the single most ordinary way to run the skill wrong. The agent saw a Python traceback instead of the `Error: …` line the skill instructs it to read, so a satisfiable prerequisite looked like a crash. Every other planning CLI was checked against an empty workspace in the same pass; `ptet` was the only one. Two tests pin it — the missing result and the missing tax-law cache — both mutation-verified red against the three-type tuple.
  - **"Every module self-tests when run directly" was true of four modules out of twenty-one (`test_portability.py::TestSelfTestClaim`):** AGENTS.md §5 and the new CONTRIBUTING.md both said every one did; the other seventeen argparse-error or print usage, and a contributor following that sentence cannot tell a broken module from a wrong instruction. Both documents now name the four (`crypto_importer`, `irs_dates`, `secure_fs`, `tax_computations`) and a test runs each one to confirm it really exits 0. Only that direction is checked: catching a module that quietly GAINS a self-test needs a glob over `agents/lib/`, which would make every edit to any of the twenty-one modules select the whole portability suite in the PostToolUse hook — a cost paid on every engine edit, against a stale-but-short list that costs a contributor nothing. `CONTRIBUTING.md` joined `WATCHED_ROOTS` and the hook's `_ARMS` together, which is the pairing that file's own comment demands.
  - **The reported sites were three; the defect was a class (`test_secure_fs.py::TestNoRuntimePathReachesAMessageUnredacted`):** `secure_fs` redacts at its own boundary, but every module that names a runtime path in an error had the same hole — `archive.py` printed a refused entry's NAME, `parse_utils.py` a skipped document's, `tie_out.py` the `summaries/` path, and `rollover.py`/`tax_calendar.py`/`scenario.py` their own — and each of those is a taxpayer's scanned document, whose name is the disclosure AGENTS.md §1 restricts those directories to prevent. Fixing only what was reported is how the gap survived the round that introduced `redact()`, so the invariant is now structural: an `ast` walk over the seven path-handling modules asserts that no `raise` or `print` interpolates a path-shaped name (or a path-shaped attribute of one) except through `redact()`. The EXCEPTION half is not padding — `os.chmod`, `os.lstat` and `shutil.move` each name their operand in their own message text, so a dutifully-redacted `{path}` sitting next to a raw `{e}` discloses the path anyway, by the half of the sentence that came from the OS. Scalars are deliberately outside the rule (`{p.suffix}`, `{type(e).__name__}`): a detector that fires on correct code gets exemptions added until it means nothing. Two guards keep the walk honest in both directions — one module written to offend in both recognised shapes must be flagged, one written to redact must not be — and it is mutation-verified against live code by removing a single `redact()` from `parse_utils.py`.
  - **`os.walk` silently swallows a `scandir` failure unless told not to (`test_archive.py`):** the nested-directory hardening loop in `archive_year` walks a moved subtree (`archive/{year}/documents/`, etc.) with `os.walk(dst, followlinks=False)` to chmod every entry and refuse any symlink found inside it — but `os.walk` defaults to silently skipping a directory it cannot `scandir` (permission denied, a transient NFS/I/O error) rather than raising, so a failure partway through the walk would leave the rest of that subtree at whatever mode it arrived with and any symlink inside it unchecked, while `archive_year` still returns a normal, successful report. Fixed with `onerror=_raise_walk_error`, a one-line callback that re-raises the `OSError` it's handed so it lands in the SAME `except Exception` block that already rolls back a failed move or chmod — no new failure path, just closing the one `os.walk` opens by default. `test_scandir_failure_during_nested_walk_rolls_back` monkeypatches `os.scandir` (the same technique the existing chmod-failure test uses on `os.chmod`, since `archive.py`'s `os` name is the real module object and a module-level function resolves its own globals dynamically) to fail on the specific moved directory, and is mutation-verified red without `onerror`: absent it, `os.walk` finishes normally, `archive_year` returns instead of raising, and the test's `pytest.raises` sees nothing.
  - **The one subtree `archive_year` does not move is the one nothing was securing (`test_archive.py::TestTheRetainedSnapshotIsHardenedToo`):** `archive/{year}/as_filed/` is the `/tax-amend` snapshot the year-end cutover MERGES alongside rather than refusing — and every hardening path in the function is keyed on movement. The two `secure_path` calls on the destination stop at `archive/{year}` itself, and the walk that refuses links and restricts entries runs per moved directory, so a snapshot already in place kept whatever modes it was written with: 0755 directories listing a taxpayer's as-filed document NAMES under a normal umask, and on Windows a snapshot written before the ACL work whose "chmod" only flipped the read-only DOS attribute. `archive_year` then returned a successful report over it — an owner-only file's name remains visible when its enclosing directory is listable, which is why AGENTS.md §1 restricts the directories at all. The retained entries are now refused and secured on the same terms as a moved subtree, and the DRY RUN inspects them too: the real run refuses a link inside the snapshot and rolls the whole archive back, so a preview that skipped it would report a clean archive for a tree the real run stops on — the user confirming a preview that then fails partway through, which is the exact gap the moved-subtree preview walk exists to close. Four behavioral tests, each mutation-verified red (nested modes, a link inside the snapshot, `as_filed` itself being a link — the tolerating guard matches on the NAME, so a link wearing it was tolerated and then chmod'd through — and the dry-run refusal), plus two counterweights that pass either way by design: the snapshot's contents survive the hardening, and a clean snapshot still previews as an ordinary merge. The mode assertions take the `permissive_umask` fixture, without which asserting 0700 is vacuous — `mkdir` asks for 0777 and umask 077 subtracts its way there with no chmod involved.
  - **A dependency pin nothing imports is not pinned (`test_pdf_toolchain.py`):** no other test imports `reportlab` or `pdfplumber`, so a bump used to reach `main` on a green CI that had proved only that pip could RESOLVE it — `generate_tax_pdf.py` is an untracked runtime artifact CI cannot see, and pdfplumber is imported lazily inside `parse_utils` where nothing reached it. Surfaced by the reportlab 4.5.1 -> 5.0.0 review: the whole release delta is one INVERTED DEFAULT (`trustedHosts=None` flipped from trust-all to trust-none), a change no signature diff or type checker can see. The build test renders the generator's full flowable + style + onPage-canvas surface; the round-trip test reads that PDF back through `extract_pdf_text` / `extract_tables` / `pdf_page_count`, so the two pins are pinned against EACH OTHER and no binary fixture is committed. Mutation-verified PER TEST rather than in aggregate, because the split is load-bearing: the build test fails on a removed/renamed reportlab symbol and on broken pagination, asserting reportlab's own `doc.page` and NOT a byte-size floor — the full document is ~2.2 KB and a PDF holding one character is ~1.4 KB, so any threshold admitting the first admits the second, which is a content check in appearance only. Silently-lost CONTENT is caught by the round-trip test alone, since an empty story still renders a well-formed two-page PDF. Neither catches a renamed TableStyle command, which reportlab ignores in silence — that stays a manual /tax-generate check. The third test walks the generator's imports with `ast` — BOTH statement forms, because a guard blind to plain `import reportlab.x` misses exactly how the graphics/renderPDF modules are conventionally imported, and the non-empty sanity check cannot catch that since the other lines keep the set populated. It fails unless `_build_reference_pdf` both IMPORTS and REFERENCES each one. The covered set is DERIVED from that function, never a hand-maintained list: a list makes appending a name the cheapest way to turn the guard green, silencing the alarm while creating the exact untested surface it exists to prevent — and importing without using is the same bypass one step quieter, which is why usage is checked too. Both bypasses are mutation-verified to fail, and a genuinely-updated mirror is verified to pass so the guard cannot cry wolf. It SKIPS on a fresh clone and in CI by design, firing only where the generator is actually edited (so CI reports the count above as one SKIP plus the rest passed, never as all-passed — stated without repeating the number, because the restatement that used to live here went stale the first time the total moved).
  - **A freshness gate must not fire on correct data (`test_tax_law_fetcher.py::TestScalarByDesignThresholds`):** `_EXPECT_STRUCTURED` matches on the KEY NAME, and `threshold` is a per-filing-status dict nearly everywhere it appears. CA's mental-health services surcharge is the exception — Prop 63 / R&TC §17043 set a flat $1,000,000 threshold and never indexed it, so `create_state_template` hardcodes a bare int. The key-name rule flagged the toolkit's OWN canonical CA template, so **every California return reported INCOMPLETE at `/tax-compute` Step 0.5 forever** — the sdi_wage_limit / underpayment_rates / phaseout_rate nag a fourth time, on the flagship state. This one is worse than a nag: Step 0.5 says INCOMPLETE plus no live web means STOP, so a gate that fires on correct data teaches the agent to reason its way PAST the freshness gate. Observed end to end in a naive-agent probe — the agent hit the false positive, correctly diagnosed it, then reclassified the cache FRESH and computed a full return on placeholder brackets. The exemption is keyed by exact PATH, never by key name; the paired leak guard pins that a scalar `federal.niit.threshold` is still corruption, and a third pins that a NULL at the exempt path still flags — as a plain missing field, not a misleading "expected dict/list". The fourth is the one that holds the LINE rather than the instance: it walks every state template (CA/NY/NJ/FL/TX/WA) and fails if any of them provokes a SHAPE complaint about its own canonical data, so the fifth field of this kind fails at the point the template is added rather than reaching a taxpayer. Pinning individual paths is what let this recur four times.
  - **Claude Code hooks are shipped configuration nothing else exercises (`test_claude_hooks.py`):** no import graph reaches `.claude/hooks/*.sh` and no type checker reads them, so a test inspecting the source TEXT would be satisfied by a script that cannot run at all — every assertion drives the real script with a real payload. The failure paths are the load-bearing half, because a hook that always exits 0 and a hook that never fires are indistinguishable from outside: the tempting `pytest ... | tail -20 || exit 2` was MEASURED returning 0 on a red suite (a pipeline's status is its last command's unless `pipefail` is set, which makes that form correct only by a side effect of a line elsewhere), and mutating the script back to it turns two assertions red. `.venv/bin/python` is shimmed rather than really run, which is what makes the failure path both deterministic and fast. The degrades are pinned as behaviour, not prose — no `jq`, no `.venv`, a non-skill path, a nonexistent project dir — each exiting 0 SILENTLY, since a hook that narrates every successful edit is noise the user learns to ignore. On the permissions side the counterweight is the one that holds the line: `tax_law_<year>.json` must stay 644, because it holds published rates and no PII, and without that assertion the protected list could quietly grow to everything and the hook's claim about WHAT it protects would stop being checkable. `generate_tax_pdf.py` IS in the list — a runtime artifact by extension only, carrying the taxpayer's name in its section headers, and the one runtime file the commit-time scan structurally cannot vet since that scan matches SSN and credential SHAPES. The matcher test pins `Bash`, because `result.json` is written by RUNNING a script and a `Write|Edit`-only matcher would miss exactly the files the hook exists for. **The payload fast path is pinned on ORDER, not outcome**, because the later tree walk hardens the same files anyway — so an end-state assertion passes whether the fast path ran or not, and what the fast path buys is precisely surviving a walk that times out. One shared helper pauses a chosen walk and asserts the target is already 0600 while the hook is still blocked in it: it covers the exact runtime-tree target, a generated PDF OUTSIDE every runtime tree, and a payload path reported in its PHYSICALLY-RESOLVED form while `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` names the workspace through a symlink — that last one had been classified as external, making the whole fast path inert on such a host with the hook still exiting 0. **Which walk is paused is itself load-bearing**, and is the one knob that quietly turns these into tests that cannot fail: pausing a walk that runs BEFORE the behaviour under test leaves the assertion satisfied by that walk, so the artifact case pauses `harden_workspace_artifacts` itself (`find .`) rather than a runtime tree — that holds regardless of which of the two post-payload walks runs first, since the fast path precedes both. **The Bash route has no fast path at all** — `python generate_tax_pdf.py` names no file, so nothing can be hardened from the payload and ORDER between the two remaining walks is the only lever. An earlier version ran `harden_workspace_artifacts` first on the theory that it was "bounded by construction" for having pruned the named runtime trees — wrong, because pruning the KNOWN trees does not bound what is left: any other subtree a workspace happens to contain (a nested checkout, a build output) is walked in full, so that sweep is unbounded in the general case while `documents/` is bounded by something real, what the taxpayer actually scanned in, and is the directory AGENTS.md singles out as needing its own rule. The runtime-tree loop now runs BEFORE `harden_workspace_artifacts`, so a slow generic sweep cannot starve the named trees; the accepted residual gap is the reverse — an opaque-Bash-written file outside every named tree AND outside the workspace root, colliding with a slow named-tree walk — which needs three things to compound and does not cover the documented generation flow, since that writes to the workspace root and is hardened at pass start independent of this order. Both halves are mutation-pinned independently — undoing the (corrected) order reddens only the Bash-written-document test, deleting the fast path's artifact arm reddens only the Write test. The generated-artifact extension set is declared ONCE, in `GENERATED_ARTIFACT_EXTENSIONS`, and read by three consumers that each need their own loop over it — find's case-insensitive predicate, the fast path's `case`-arm check (it runs before any walk), and the root-level glob loop (it runs before `.claude/` or a walk exist at all) — because a comment once claimed the set was "stated twice and only twice" while a third, untested copy sat in that root-level loop the whole time; `test_the_generated_artifact_extension_set_is_declared_once` pins all three against the one array instead of trusting a comment's count. The counterweight is `test_the_payload_fast_path_reaches_no_further_than_the_walk`: the fast path must be EARLIER, not WIDER, so `.git` and `.venv` are pruned on both routes. More chmods is not automatically safer — a permissions hook that reaches into the object store is one a contributor switches off. That prune arm was itself CASE-SENSITIVE, and a default case-insensitive macOS host resolves `.VENV/tool.tmp` and `.venv/tool.tmp` to the same file, so a payload spelling the alias missed the arm, fell to the generic artifact-name match, and got chmodded anyway — the classification now lowers only the first path segment before comparing (not the whole path, so a case-sensitive host's unrelated `.gitignore` cannot fold into `.git`). `test_a_case_aliased_venv_path_is_still_excluded_from_hardening` pins it end-to-end with a runtime case-insensitivity PROBE rather than an OS check, and skips where it cannot fire: CI runs Linux, where `.VENV` is an ordinary unrelated directory and the alias this guards cannot exist. That case-folding fix was itself too blunt the first time: lowering `edited_top` unconditionally excludes `.VENV` on a case-SENSITIVE host too, where it is not an alias at all but an ordinary directory the find walk never prunes — stranding a file inside it at whatever mode it arrived with if the later workspace scan times out. The classification now confirms the alias with `-ef` (same device and inode; a bash test builtin, not `stat`, whose flags this project already avoids for the BSD/GNU reason above) before excluding, so a same-named-but-distinct directory falls through to the generic artifact-name arm instead. `test_a_distinct_uppercase_directory_is_still_hardened` pins the inverse of the alias test with the same runtime PROBE, inverted, and can only fire on a case-sensitive host — it skips on this project's macOS dev boxes and runs for real only on CI's Linux. Separately, `harden_one`'s own chmod call inside the fast path was missing the `./` prefix the root-level PDF/backup loop already uses for exactly this reason: an absolute payload path like `/repo/-return.pdf` strips to the root-relative `-return.pdf`, and GNU chmod's argv-permuting option parser reads a leading `-` as an unrecognized flag, `warn`s, and blocks stamp advancement on an otherwise-successful Write — BSD chmod does not permute argv the same way and cannot reproduce it, so `test_a_dash_prefixed_root_artifact_reaches_the_fast_path_cleanly` is gated on a `chmod --version` GNU/BSD probe rather than an OS check, for the same reason the two case tests probe the filesystem instead of `uname`. That fix was itself later centralized: the `./` prefix now lives INSIDE `harden_one` (applied only when the target actually starts with `-`, so an already-prefixed caller is unaffected), so a future caller threading a raw externally-supplied path into it does not have to independently rediscover the lesson at its own call site. The alias-confirming `-ef` check that rescued `.venv` from over-exclusion had a mirror-image gap: it covered only the EXCLUDED `.git`/`.venv` arms, while the INCLUDED runtime trees (`documents/`, `summaries/`, ...) still matched by exact case only, so a case-varied `Documents/scan.png` on a case-insensitive host skipped the fast path entirely and was left at whatever mode the Write left it in — the same exposure window the fast path exists to close, just on the inclusion side instead of the exclusion side. `RUNTIME_ARTIFACT_TREES` and `PRUNED_HOME_TREES` now name both lists ONCE, feeding both `classify_edited_top_tree` (the fast path's classification, tried by exact spelling across all ten names before EVER forking a `tr` process, so the dominant case — an exact top-level spelling, the only one any skill writes — pays no subshell at all) and `harden_workspace_artifacts`'s find-predicate construction, closing the gap a hardcoded triple copy could not be tested against; `test_the_runtime_and_pruned_tree_names_are_declared_once` pins the declaration and both consumers, and `test_a_case_varied_sensitive_tree_path_is_still_hardened` reproduces the inclusion-side alias end-to-end the same way `test_a_case_aliased_venv_path_is_still_excluded_from_hardening` already did for exclusion. The case-folding itself was a second, separate hazard: `tr` can raise "Illegal byte sequence" on a byte that is invalid for the shell's locale — the same hazard `scripts/pre-commit-pii-scan.sh` already pins `LC_ALL=C` to avoid — and a scanner/OCR-produced filename hitting it silently disabled the fast path with no `warn()`. `lower_ascii()` pins the same fix in the one place both the extension check and the tree classification now fold case through. Two test-only fixes rode along: `_start_behind_a_slow_walk`'s `slow_walk` parameter lost its silent `"documents"` default — every caller now states explicitly which walk it pauses, since a caller that forgot to override a wrong default is exactly how one of these tests would stop discriminating without anyone noticing — and the case-insensitivity probe, duplicated verbatim across what was about to become three tests, is now the one shared `_is_case_insensitive_filesystem()` helper. **Both new case tests were then found VACUOUS for the reason the paragraph above already names, and the rule generalizes: in this class an assertion that a file IS hardened is satisfied by SOME later walk no matter what the fast path did, so every such assertion must sit behind a pause — running the hook to completion pins nothing.** Each had done exactly that. `test_a_case_varied_sensitive_tree_path_is_still_hardened` asserted after the whole hook, and `harden_changed_tree documents` reaches `Documents/scan.png` through the OTHER SPELLING OF ITS OWN INODE — the aliasing that makes the test a test is also what makes the walk cover it — so it now pauses `documents`, the same directory. `test_a_distinct_uppercase_directory_is_still_hardened` was satisfied by `harden_workspace_artifacts`, which prunes the literal `./.venv`, therefore descends into the distinct `./.VENV`, and matches `return.pdf` on `-iname '*.pdf'`; it now pauses that walk (`find .`) — the very scan whose timeout the test exists to survive. Both were verified in BOTH directions against a mutated hook, the pre-fix versions passing green where the fixed ones go red, the uppercase one on a real case-sensitive volume rather than by reasoning. The third case test needs no pause and is the counterweight explaining why: `test_a_case_aliased_venv_path_is_still_excluded_from_hardening` asserts a file is NOT hardened, and no later walk can produce that failure, since every walk prunes `.venv`.
  - **A subagent prompt is prose that nothing imports (`test_claude_agents.py`):** `.claude/agents/*.md` rots in complete silence — a key renamed in `create_empty_tax_law` leaves the whole suite green while the freshness auditor hunts for a field that no longer exists and reports the absence as a finding. Every backticked fully-qualified `federal.*` / `state.*` path in the auditor is RESOLVED against the real schema builders (mutation-verified: `federal.salt_capp` fails it), and the scope limit is stated rather than papered over — an unqualified mention is not covered, which is why the agent doc spells the paths out. The auditor normally requires a source to name the filing year, but the schema's two multi-period blocks are pinned to their actual periods: each `federal.underpayment_rates` entry to the calendar quarter containing `effective_from` (including TY+1 Form 2210 coverage), and each `federal.historical_top_rate` entry to its prior-year key. Treating either as a filing-year value makes a correctly populated cache impossible to certify. Two grant invariants: no review agent may hold `Write`/`Edit`/`NotebookEdit`, since an auditor that can resolve its own findings cannot be trusted about them; and an agent holding `WebSearch`/`WebFetch` may not also hold `Bash`, because a shell and an outbound connection in one context inside a workspace holding a real return is the combination AGENTS.md §1 exists to prevent. Tool names are checked against a CLOSED set — a misspelt grant is silent, the agent simply lacking the capability its prompt assumes. **The self-review found the hole that made all three grant checks vacuous:** an agent definition with NO `tools:` line inherits EVERY tool, `Write` included, and each grant check works by intersecting the declared set with a forbidden one — so deleting that line turned all of them into no-ops at exactly the moment the grant became dangerous. `test_every_agent_declares_an_explicit_tool_allowlist` forbids the permissive default and is mutation-verified against a deleted `tools:` line; the agent set is named explicitly rather than globbed, since an empty `.claude/agents/` satisfies every other assertion here by having nothing to check.
  - **A recorded acceptance is a claim that goes stale (`test_claude_agents.py`, `docs/ops/review-subagent-acceptance.md`):** CLAUDE.md advertises that both review subagents passed a replay on a date, and from outside a reviewer that finds nothing and one that cannot find anything are the same artifact — so the record is executed rather than read. A replay is a function of THREE things and each fails silently. The FIXTURES must still be defective: Replay A's `git show <sha>` is materialised and the pinned `compute_qbi_deduction` must still take no taxable-income argument and still return the full 20%, and Replay B's generator is executed and its cache audited against `ptet._salt_cap`, so a plant that has drifted into being correct goes red here instead of passing over there on an empty finding. They must also be the SAME ones: `6c8804f` carries the QBI defect too, so a pin moved one commit back leaves every corruption check green while the PASS describes a report on a file nobody dispatched, and each verdict therefore pins a sha256 of its input as well as of the definition it judged — Replay B's being a function of the engine, which turns "re-run after a schema change" from prose into a failing test. The third is the MODEL, which no digest reaches: the definitions name none and a dispatch takes the session's, so it is recorded as a field, with the one pre-existing date grandfathered by name in `MODEL_UNRECORDED_DATES` rather than by default. Every predicate has a control that drives it over a doctored record, because an audit that is green on a correct record and green on a broken audit looks identical from outside — which is the same finding as the fixture's, one level up.
  - **A skill is instructions to RUN, and an angle-bracket placeholder is a redirect (`test_portability.py::test_every_bash_block_in_every_skill_parses`):** `PR=<number>` does not read as "fill this in" to a shell — it opens a file called `number` and aborts the line. `bash -n` parses without executing, so the check costs nothing and runs no command; it is a SYNTAX check only and cannot tell whether a command does the right thing. Two of 44 blocks failed when it was written, and the pre-existing one was in `tax-rollover`'s apply step, the single command that writes carryforwards into the new year's workspace — a paste-time syntax error naming a token the document never mentions. Brace and quoted placeholders pass, which is what makes them the convention.
  - **A code review of the hooks/agents work found fourteen more, and three are worth recording as rules.** (1) **A shell is a write tool.** `WRITE_TOOLS` enumerated `Write`/`Edit`/`NotebookEdit` by NAME, so `bias-direction-reviewer` held `Bash` — `sed -i`, `git commit` — while CLAUDE.md said "neither has a write tool" and the assertion agreed: documented, tested and false. `Bash` is now in the set, the agent lost it, and the networked agent is held to a CLOSED minimal set rather than another forbidden list, because a no-shell rule can no longer fail on its own once no agent may hold one. (2) **A gate that fires on correct in-progress work is the cry-wolf pattern.** The registry pair necessarily fails while a new skill is being written — the directory exists before its `EXPECTED_SKILLS` entry and symlink do — so the hook reported failure on every edit until three more landed. Both are `--deselect`ed, and `test_only_the_registry_pair_is_deselected_and_both_ids_resolve` pins exactly which two AND that both node ids still resolve, since pytest ignores a `--deselect` matching nothing. (3) **The fourth copy of the sensitive-file list had already drifted.** `crypto_transactions.json` and `projection_inputs.json` are in the PII scan, `.gitignore` and the installer's patterns but were missing from the permissions hook — and being gitignored they are precisely what the commit-time scan never sees. The hook's list is now checked against the scan's own `case` by parsing it, turning a hand-mirrored list into a verified one. Also pinned: active runtime trees are incremental against a stamp (`-newercm`, so a mode change counts), while `archive/` polls only its immediate tax-year directories and recursively hardens changed years instead of restatting every historical PDF after every tool call; direct Write/Edit payloads harden a changed file inside an existing archived year, and a Bash payload naming `archive/YYYY` re-hardens only that year so a nested rewrite whose parent-year ctime did not move cannot stay 0644. Dynamic/module-driven archive commands and malformed/unparseable payloads fall back to a full archive pass; commands unrelated to archive do not traverse it. A failed `chmod` exits 1 with a safe generic warning rather than echoing a potentially identity-bearing filename, and materializing changed paths makes that failure reliably prevent the stamp from advancing; the hook creates nothing in a workspace without `.claude/`; and the path filter requires the edited file to be INSIDE the project, since the pattern matched on substring and a skill in another clone would run this repository's suite.
  - **The skill registry split names a boundary rather than relaxing one (`test_portability.py`):** `EXPECTED_SKILLS` is now `PRODUCT_SKILLS | CONTRIBUTOR_SKILLS`, and both halves face every rule — frontmatter, the neutralization lint, the line cap, a `.claude/skills` symlink each. The paired assertion is what keeps the label true: the two sets are disjoint, every `PRODUCT_SKILLS` name starts with `tax-`, and no `CONTRIBUTOR_SKILLS` name does — otherwise the contributor set becomes wherever a name is put when it does not fit, and the distinction stops being a fact about the data. `state-module` joins `WEB_DEPENDENT`, so filling a state's fetched-law block carries the same STOP-and-paste sentence as a federal fetch; that gate applies to indexed/year-dependent figures, while cited statutory-fixed structural constants remain inline rather than becoming fake annual fetch fields. The `codex-loop` consent boundary covers every remote write — triggers, replies, thread resolution, pushes and other GitHub mutations — on agents that can auto-select a skill despite `disable-model-invocation`; permission to inspect or fix locally is not permission to publish. The installer's prune loop widened from a `tax-*` glob to every installed entry, because ownership is proven by where a symlink RESOLVES and not by a name that happened to be co-extensive with the skill set when the loop was written; the speculative NOTE stays `tax-*`-only, which is the one place a name still carries information. `test_a_dangling_link_is_removed_whatever_the_skill_is_named` is mutation-verified against the old glob.
  - **Shared parse/validation infra (all lib parsers):** numeric validators reject non-finite `NaN`/`Infinity` (they pass `isinstance` + defeat `<0`/`>1` since NaN comparisons are False) — `_strict_num`, `_strict_non_negative`, `_optional_non_negative_num`, `_require_tax_law_amount`, `_validate_rate` (finite fraction in [0,1]), `_sched_a_amount` (non-negative), `_f2210_amount(allow_negative=…)`, `_field_num`. VALUE validation too: `filing_status` ∈ canonical set (Single/MFJ/MFS/HoH/QSS), `address.state` a 2-letter USPS code. Workspace-boundary writers reuse `rollover._validate_subpath` / `_validate_year_arg` / `_validate_name` / `_resolve_under`; planning skills reuse `scenario.extract_baseline_inputs` + `_rate_kwargs` + `rollover.load_prior`.
  - **Core 1040 engine:** `compute_qbi_deduction` — Form 8995 OVERALL CAP (deduction ≤ rate × (taxable income before QBI − net capital gain)); §199A(c)(4)/Reg. §1.199A-3(b)(1)(vi) QBI BASE NETTING (QBI reduced by trade-attributable `half_se_tax` + `se_health_insurance` + `se_retirement` BEFORE the 20%, passed separately so raw Schedule C net profit stays `qbi_income`); over-`federal.qbi_threshold` delegates to `compute_form_8995a` (Form 8995-A; `_qbi_phase_in_range`, §199A(b)(2) greater-of-50%-W-2 or 25%+2.5%-UBIA, §199A(d)(3) SSTB phase-out, year-aware range from `federal.qbi_phase_in_range` else statutory) when W-2/UBIA supplied and net QBI > 0; negative QBI floored not crashed; `over_threshold` warning when 8995-A limits unmodeled; planning callers (`compute_scenario`, projection) thread `half_se_tax`. Schedule 8812 CTC — ODC + refundable-ACTC split, phase-out on combined CTC+ODC, per-child `refundable_limit` cap (excludes never-refundable ODC), 15%-over-$2,500 earned-income formula; CTC tax-liability input is Credit Limit Worksheet A (line 18 minus Schedule 3 part-I credits). Form 8959 Part IV line 20 = Medicare WAGES × 1.45% only (SE income never enters withholding reconciliation); NIIT is Form 8960. Schedule A engine `compute_schedule_a` (itemize-vs-standard election, Schedule 2 additional taxes). `net_capital_gains` lives in tax_computations (projection reuses it; scenario keeps `_net_capital_gains` alias). Taxable-SS Pub 915 worksheet — provisional income adds tax-exempt interest + line-5 exclusion add-backs, filing_status REQUIRED (QSS = SINGLE $25K base; MFS lived-together $0-base 85% path), lump-sum LSE flagged upper-bound.

  - **The isolated cache copy the freshness auditor reads (`test_tax_law_export.py`):** V7. That agent is the only one here with network access, and its "read the two files in the directory you are pointed at, and nothing else" scope is a sentence in a prompt — `Read` is measurably not directory-confined, so nothing enforces it. What is enforceable is what the agent is pointed AT. **The class that comes FIRST is `TestTheCopyIsFaithful`, and it is there because the first draft filtered the cache against the schema while the auditor's whole job includes reporting keys the schema does not define** — item 6 (a statutory-fixed constant that has leaked in) and item 8 (`state.type` as a positive declaration) were both structurally unreachable after the export, and a cache falsely calling Colorado a no-income-tax state arrived clean. So four cases pin what must SURVIVE: an undefined key, a `state.type` its template does not carry, a `ptet` block and a historical rate (both dynamic-key blocks whose seeds ship empty, so a schema walk dropped every entry), and prose plus an unauthoritative pin — neither of which is this module's business, the source host being the auditor's item 3. The copy is compared BYTE for byte, because re-serialising lets key order and int/float identity drift. `TestTheExportRefuses` is the other half: SSN- or credential-shaped text blocks the copy from six positions — a schema-known string field, a KEY (`{<an SSN>: "Jane"}` puts the value where a value-only scan never looks), inside a list element, a URL query string, under `_provenance`, and a credential — with the refusal asserted never to echo the value and to leave no copy behind. Two properties are pinned as STATED rather than discovered: a synthetic `000-00-0000` is refused like any other, because `.pii-allowlist` is read by the shell gate and not by `secure_fs.redact`, and the counterweight is therefore ordinary cache content (six-figure thresholds, rates, ISO dates, IRS URLs) rather than a synthetic value. A symlinked cache is refused (`is_reparse_point`, not `is_symlink`, which is False for an NTFS junction) and so is a non-finite number, which `json.loads` admits and which would make the copy invalid JSON. `TestTheDestination` pins what the acceptance record already records in its own words — one directory, one file, named: a `--dest` inside the workspace is refused (the first draft's `mkdir(exist_ok=True)` plus a same-basename target OVERWROTE the live cache and then printed the workspace as the place to point the auditor), an empty `--dest` is refused (`Path("")` is `.`, so it chmodded the CWD 0700), a `--dest` already holding files is refused, the default destination is fresh each time, and `secure_path` is pinned on a caller-supplied directory because `mkdtemp` is already 0700 and a mutation removing the call left the default case green. Every test names an explicit destination: fourteen using the default left 225 directories in `$TMPDIR` on the machine that found it. Exit 2 is BLOCKED, the house meaning; the first draft used it for "copied, with findings", which is the opposite, so a wrapper written to the house rule would have discarded a valid copy. `TestTheSchemaTravelsWithTheCopy` pins the `schema.json` written beside the copy (Y2), which is what lets the auditor be told to read nothing outside its directory: the cache seed is `create_empty_tax_law` for the FILENAME's year, the one the auditor's item 0 holds the payload to; the state seed is `state_schema` for the code AS WRITTEN, chosen by `declared_state_code`, the helper `set` validates through — a lower-case `ca` gets the generic template `set` used, not California's — and null when the block carries no code, asserted beside `set`'s refusal of such a block; the regime table is `state_regimes`, `_expected_state_regime` asked of every code, with its `none` entries asserted equal to `check_freshness`'s `_none_type_allowed` either side of TN's repeal; and the host policy is `source_host_policy`, built from the tables the rule itself reads, with a literal reading of its `match` asserted to reach the rule's verdict on a probe set led by `www.law.cornell.edu`, which the first published policy had no subdomain clause for. The regime code is `state_regime_code`, normalised as `check_freshness` normalises it and null where it resolves none; the pin roster is `source_pins`, `_expected_pins` as a table, with a literal reading of the auditor's item 9 over it asserted to reach every `_provenance` entry `set` writes — Florida's county-rate pin among them, which a lookup by path never reached. The copy and the schema are written together or not at all: a failed schema write takes the copy back and the directory with it, a named `--dest` is left empty and reusable, and an engine that cannot describe the cache creates nothing. **What no test covers, and the runbook says so instead: the launch procedure.** Nothing here proves the auditor is ever pointed at the copy.
