Replay B in `docs/ops/review-subagent-acceptance.md` now hands it the export's directory rather than a file path (Y2), and `test_claude_agents.py` checks that the generator prints a directory holding exactly the cache and `schema.json` — but whether a dispatch names only that directory is the runbook's step, not a test's.
  - **Commit-time PII content guard (`test_pii_content_scan.py`):** drives the real `scripts/pre-commit-pii-scan.sh` inside a throwaway git repo. Pins that a full SSN in an ADDED line blocks; that the masked `XXX-XX-` storage format and `.pii-allowlist` values (`000-00-0000`) do not; that `tests/fixtures/` IS content-scanned even though it is exempt from the filename scan (a real SSN in a synthetic fixture is the mistake being caught); that only added lines count, so editing elsewhere in a file with pre-existing content stays committable; that tax figures, form numbers and ISO dates do not false-positive (a scanner that cries wolf gets `--no-verify`'d as a reflex); and that **the block message never echoes the matched value** — reporting file + count only, since printing it would perform the disclosure the scan exists to prevent. The clean-diff case is load-bearing: under `set -euo pipefail` a non-matching `grep` fails the pipeline and aborts the script, which exits non-zero with no output and is indistinguishable from a legitimate block. **Four bypasses found in PR-#67 review are pinned individually:** the allowlist filters extracted MATCHES with `grep -vxFf`, not whole lines (a line carrying both `XXX-XX-6789` and a real SSN previously passed); `SECRET_PATTERN` admits hyphens inside project-scoped keys (`sk-proj-…`, `sk-ant-…`), which a `[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}` class truncates at the first hyphen; staged paths are read NUL-delimited so a non-ASCII filename is not silently skipped by BOTH scans (`--name-only` quotes it, and the quoted form matches neither the extension rules nor a pathspec); and a git-classified-binary blob is BLOCKED rather than treated as clean, because it emits no `+` lines (a UTF-16 file holding an SSN is the concrete case, and byte-level grep would not find it either). **CI calls this same script with a `<base>...<head>` range** — `test_ci_invokes_the_shared_script_rather_than_reimplementing_it` stops the old hand-mirrored copy in `ci.yml` from creeping back, which is how all four bypasses came to exist in two places at once. **Round 2 added seven more, each pinned:** range mode inspects EVERY COMMIT individually (a net `base...head` diff hides a secret a later commit deleted — the case CI exists for); bare-nine-digit and space-separated SSN forms are detected, with exactly-nine-digit token extraction so 8/10/14-digit identifiers do not false-positive; `github_pat_` fine-grained tokens and `gh[pousr]_` join the credential set; added lines are marked via `--output-indicator-new` so real content beginning `++ ` is not mistaken for a `+++` file header; the allowlist is read from the STAGED snapshot (`git show :.pii-allowlist`) so an unstaged local edit cannot widen it, with the staged complement pinned too; an unusable base ref BLOCKS instead of yielding an empty file list that reads as clean; and the runtime workspace directories are path-blocked, making the README's promise true — with `tests/fixtures/` precedence pinned, since real fixtures live under `tests/fixtures/projection/archive/2025/result.json` and would otherwise trip both the `archive/` path rule and the `result.json` filename rule. **Round 3 added four more, all one shape — the scan asked git for a subset of the change and the subset had a hole:** merge commits are scanned against their first parent, because a conflict resolution is content present in the merge and in NEITHER parent, so `--no-merges` dropped the only commit carrying it while both sides scanned clean (re-examining the merged-in branch's lines is redundant, not a new false-positive source — they were already scanned via their own commits); `.pii-allowlist` is resolved PER SCANNED COMMIT rather than once from the range head, so allowlisting a value in a later commit no longer retroactively exempts the commit that leaked it, whose history is still pushed; the AWS pattern admits `ASIA` STS temporary access-key IDs alongside `AKIA`, and adds a CONTEXTUAL `aws_secret_access_key` rule — the secret access key is 40 unremarkable base64 characters, so `test_bare_forty_char_token_does_not_false_positive` is the deliberate counterweight pinning that an unlabelled 40-char string (a SHA, a checksum) stays committable; and the diff status filter became the DENYLIST `--diff-filter=d` (everything but deletion) after the `ACMR` allowlist silently dropped typechange, letting a tracked symlink be replaced by a regular file that bypassed the filename rules and the content scan alike — both the content and the blocked-extension halves of that hole are pinned, and enumerating statuses is the mistake the denylist stops repeating. **Round 4 turned the lens on the EXEMPTIONS, which are the gate's own attack surface, and on the one piece of content the content scan can never see — the pathname.** The allowlist has no working-tree fallback at all: the old one was guarded by "only when the file is untracked", and staging a DELETION of the tracked `.pii-allowlist` satisfies that guard, so recreating it in the working tree with the sensitive literal exempted a value the committed snapshot never allowed (`_make_repo` therefore STAGES the allowlist, mirroring the real repository where it is tracked). `count_hits()` now runs the same patterns over the PATHNAME as over the added lines — `reports/<ssn>.txt` committed cleanly with entirely innocent contents, since the filename rules know only extensions and known names — and it runs BEFORE every exemption, because no exemption should cover an SSN in a filename. Every message that prints a path prints the `redact()`ed form, with `test_ordinary_pathnames_are_not_redacted` as the counterweight pinning that redaction does not mangle the ordinary case and stop the message naming its file. The `documents/.gitkeep` exemption covers the path rule ONLY: it used to `continue` before the content scan, so a `.gitkeep` edited to hold an SSN passed — both halves are pinned, since the exemption itself must survive (an empty `.gitkeep` is what makes `documents/` exist in a fresh clone). Outside the scanner, `TestRuntimeWorkspaceIgnored` pins that every runtime directory AGENTS.md calls gitignored actually is, in `.gitignore` AND in the installer's appended patterns — `archive/` was in neither, and `tax-archive` relocates the documents and review workpapers into year subdirectories, so root-scoped patterns cannot cover them; `test_hooks_only_mode_fails_when_the_hooks_were_not_installed` pins that the security setup step cannot print success and exit 0 after warning that nothing was installed; and `test_fbar_trigger_covers_signature_authority` pins that a description's trigger is not NARROWER than the skill's own prerequisites — signature authority alone triggers FinCEN 114 Part IV, and the $10,000 aggregate is what the skill performs rather than a precondition for selecting it, so the circular trigger's failure mode was a missing filing rather than an error. **Round 5 hit the AWS rule from round 3 twice, and the second one is the load-bearing invariant:** the detector and the redactor were separate pattern lists, the AWS rule was added to the detector alone, and the scanner therefore blocked a path containing an AWS secret and then printed that secret in the block message — a rule that is detected but not redacted turns the gate into a disclosure channel, which is strictly worse than not having the rule. Both now consume ONE `PII_PATTERNS` array, and `test_every_detected_pattern_family_is_also_redacted` walks every family (SSN, AWS access-key ID, project-scoped API key, AWS secret access key) through the pathname route, where the value is guaranteed to reach the message. The other half: the `{1,4}` separator bound could not span YAML's aligned value columns, so a five-space gap carried the credential through untouched while the bare 40-character value deliberately matches nothing else — the separator is now `*`, which cannot overrun because the class excludes alphanumerics. The keyword is spelled in character classes rather than matched with `grep -i`, because `grep` and `sed` spell case-insensitivity differently and not portably; a pattern that is case-insensitive BY CONSTRUCTION behaves identically in both, and `test_aws_secret_key_name_is_matched_case_insensitively` is the regression guard on that construction. **Round 6 questioned a premise the first five rounds shared — that `git` returns what the script expects.** Local configuration changes the output format: `color.ui=always` wraps added lines in ANSI escapes so they no longer begin with the parser's marker, `diff.external` replaces the patch with a program's output, and a textconv driver rewrites the blob before diffing. Each one empties the added-lines set and the scan reports clean, so a developer's display preference silently disabled a security gate. Every `git diff` now passes `--no-color --no-ext-diff --no-textconv`, and all three configurations are pinned. `git rev-list`'s failure was invisible for a structural reason: a process substitution's exit status is not the `while` loop's, so `done < <(git rev-list ...) || die` never fired and an empty scope list exited 0 — CI passing having inspected nothing. The revision list is materialized and status-checked like the file list already was, and `test_failing_rev_list_blocks_rather_than_reporting_clean` injects the failure with a `git` shim that fails only for `rev-list`, the one way to reproduce it deterministically. Finally, underscore left the candidate-token class: including it merged label and digits in `W2_<ssn>.txt` into a single token that the exactly-nine-digits test rejected, and `label_<digits>` is precisely how this gets written — measured at zero false positives across every tracked path and line, in the pathname and added-line scans alike. **A self-review pass then ran the same six lenses against the whole diff and found twelve more.** In the scanner's own runtime assumptions: `LC_ALL=C` is pinned for the whole script, because under a UTF-8 locale (the macOS default) grep SILENTLY SKIPS any input line that is not valid UTF-8, so one stray Latin-1/cp1252 byte hid EVERY match on that line and a full SSN committed clean — squarely this toolkit's own use case, since it ingests foreign-language documents, with a UTF-8 counterweight pinning that legitimate accented text still scans. The script `cd`s to the repository root, because paths come back root-relative while a pathspec resolves against the CURRENT directory: from a subdirectory every per-file diff matched nothing and range mode printed its coverage banner having scanned no content — the exact false pass that banner exists to prevent. `${file##*/}` replaced `basename "$file"`, which parses a leading-dash NAME as an option and aborted the scan with a raw error and no banner, and `printf` replaced `echo` for filenames, which consumes a leading -n/-e/-E as a flag. The redactor's deliberately-broad `[0-9]{9}` was RE-EXAMINED AND KEPT: narrowing it to the detector's token semantics would print an SSN embedded in a ten-digit account number verbatim, so the legibility cost is paid instead by a standing message line explaining a redacted name and pointing at `git diff --cached --name-only`, with both directions pinned. **On the installer:** hooks are located with `git rev-parse --git-path hooks`, which honours `core.hooksPath` (husky, lefthook, the pre-commit framework, any corporate setting) where `--git-common-dir` does not — hooks had been written to a directory git never consults while the installer reported success and a full SSN committed cleanly; a pre-existing foreign hook is backed up rather than clobbered, with a counterweight pinning that re-installing does not back up its own; the installer refuses to write into an ENCLOSING repository, which it had been silently hijacking and bricking; the exit-status guard now covers EVERY mode, not just `--hooks-only`, since README's Quick Start, Upgrading and Contributing sections all use the bare form that printed "Installed 20 tax skills" and exited 0 with nothing guarding commits; one `IGNORE_PATTERNS` array feeds both the create and append paths, which had drifted by eight patterns; and a withdrawn skill is REPORTED rather than deleted, because `~/.claude/skills` is shared with skills from every source and a `tax-` prefix does not prove ownership — only a dangling symlink into this clone is unambiguously ours. **Four of the twelve were vacuous tests written earlier in this same PR**, each replaced with a behavioural check and each verified by mutation: the `.gitignore` cover test matched the explanatory COMMENT and the `!documents/.gitkeep` NEGATION, so it now drives `git check-ignore`; the installer-pattern test grepped source and could only ever match the append-path list, so it now runs the installer in both scenarios and compares the files produced; the README invocation-syntax registry had three of five tokens matching unrelated prose (`/tax-setup` inside `.agents/skills/tax-setup/SKILL.md`, and the bare words `Cursor` and `VS Code`), letting the whole table be deleted with CI green, so tokens are now backticked commands matched against the TABLE ROWS only; and the "redaction in other block messages" test never reached the branch it named, because the pathname scan runs first and `continue`s — an ALLOWLISTED value is the fixture that gets there, since `count_hits` filters it to zero while `redact` still rewrites it. Finally the scanner's own error message named git 2.9 as the floor for `--output-indicator-new`, which arrived in 2.20: a user on 2.17 read the requirement as met and was left with `--no-verify` as the only actionable instruction on screen — a false remedy pointing at disabling the gate. **A review pass over that same self-review found twelve more, and the pattern in the survivors was one shape: assert on a string the code happens to contain.** The SSN separator class admitted one hyphen or one space, so an aligned column run, a TAB, DOT grouping and SLASH grouping each committed a full SSN cleanly — dot grouping being the ordinary convention in the European documents this toolkit exists to ingest. Widening is safe only in the consistent-separator form, because the naive class-plus-`+` also matches a money RANGE, of which this repository contains five; `test_a_money_range_is_not_mistaken_for_an_ssn` is that counterweight. The bare-digit token still carried LETTERS, so `SSN<nine digits>` merged into one token the exactly-nine-digits test rejected — the same correction already applied to underscore a round earlier, with a ten-digit phone number pinning that a maximal digit run cannot be a substring of a longer number. `awk` left with it: interval expressions are an OPTIONAL awk feature and mawk, Ubuntu's default and therefore CI's, treats `{9}` as literal characters, so the rule was one `update-alternatives` away from being a silent no-op in the one venue that backstops `--no-verify`. `|| true` cannot distinguish "no match" (exit 1) from "grep could not run" (exit >= 2), and a shim failing only the extraction call made a staged SSN scan clean; the sentinel is returned rather than thrown because `count_hits` runs inside a command substitution, where `die` would exit only the subshell — and the flag is a FILE for the same reason one level down. Every intermediate now lives in one 0700 directory removed by a single trap: four of six temp files were uncovered, and the patch holds the unredacted value. On the installer, `core.hooksPath` may be GLOBAL — one directory for every repository on the machine — and installing there replaced the hook governing all of them, after which this toolkit's guard looked for its scanner in each other repository's root and refused every commit there; containment is measured against the common git dir as well as the worktree, because a linked worktree's hooks legitimately live under the main clone. A DANGLING hook symlink was invisible to `[ -e ]`, so no backup was taken and `cat >` wrote THROUGH the link to a target outside the hooks directory. **Six more vacuous tests, each replaced with a behavioural check and each verified by mutation:** the two installer `.gitignore` tests re-committed the exact `!documents/.gitkeep` NEGATION trap the sibling test's own docstring documents, so they now drive `git check-ignore`; `test_resolves_the_shared_git_dir_for_hooks` was INVERTED — it failed when only a comment was deleted and passed when the resolution was replaced with one that breaks every worktree — so assertions now run against comment-stripped source; the stale-tax-law lint required "paste" and "stop" anywhere in the file, which six unrelated occurrences of "stop" satisfied, letting the instruction be inverted to "continue with the cached figures" with CI green, and now requires them in ONE sentence; `test_a_mangled_name_is_explained_rather_than_left_confusing` matched only the standing footer and passed with `redact()` stubbed to a no-op; and `test_range_mode_applies_the_same_rules` could not tell detection from a crash, since `die` shares the CI banner. **The largest hole was not a vacuous test but an absent one:** both `BLOCKED FILENAME` branches — the scanner's ORIGINAL function and what the README calls security layer 2 — could be deleted wholesale with the whole suite green, so the PR that added ninety tests for the new content scan left the rule it was built on with none. **Y6 turned the pre-push hook from a blanket refusal into the same scan, and its cases drive REAL pushes at a real bare remote** — a hook invoked by hand is a hook whose stdin the test invented. Roughly half of them exist because a review reproduced a hole the draft shipped, and they share ONE shape: the hook believed something it had not established. It read `exit 0` from the scanner as proof a scan ran — and the scanner is read from the WORKING TREE at push time, so replacing it with `exit 0` published a full SSN with no output at all, while a SUBSTRING check on the clean banner accepted a stub whose entire output was "No PII scan clean result is available"; the banner is matched whole now, against the commit COUNT the hook enumerated. It read remote-TRACKING refs as a statement about the destination — they are a local cache, and a `pushurl` alone separates the two, so the range is `git ls-remote` against the location git is actually pushing to. It read `git rev-list`'s default DATE order as topology, so the "oldest" outgoing commit could be a descendant whose parent then became the base and was excluded; the graph that shows it is pinned (one commit introduces a value, two children remove it, a merge joins them, dates ordered so the introducing commit is not last), and with the ordering broken it is the range-covers-what-was-enumerated self-check that refuses the push. And it read a successful `git rev-list` as proof there was a commit: on a tag pointing at a BLOB that call succeeds with empty output, so the blob went out unscanned. Two more concern what a subprocess inherits — the scanner is run with stdin CLOSED, since anything consuming the remaining ref lines made the next `read` see EOF and published every later ref with neither scan nor guard, and `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1` stops a `git replace` mapping showing the scan a different tree from the one the pack carries. A REF NAME gets the pathname rule's argument and the scanner's third mode, `--names`, because a branch named after an SSN discloses it whatever it points at — pinned from three sides, since an allowlisted synthetic name must still push and the LOCAL ref must not be checked at all. That last one is the new rule crying wolf within the hour: `git push <sha>:refs/heads/x` makes the local ref the sha, about one 40-hex sha in eight holds a maximal run of exactly nine digits, and a case unrelated to names began failing one run in eight on a push carrying nothing sensitive — the same false-positive class the hashed-lock digest exemption exists for, arriving through a new caller. Only the ref that reaches the remote is checked. Around those: a clean branch pushes with no flag; a full SSN in an outgoing commit blocks without echoing the value and without naming the index, which is empty at push time; a value added and later removed still blocks; only the commits the destination LACKS are scanned, by name and by URL; a deletion of a feature branch passes while a deletion of `main` does not, the destination guard running before the skip; a force rewind that publishes nothing passes rather than reading as an unperformed scan; a missing scanner blocks; a SHALLOW boundary blocks rather than being handed to the scan as a root; and `PII_SCAN_CONTEXT` is a closed set. In the scanner itself, the binary-content refusal stopped depending on `awk`: `[ "$(awk …)" = "-" ]` inside an `if` is exempt from `set -e`, so an awk that could not RUN declared a UTF-16 blob scannable and the added-lines scan then found no `+` lines and reported clean. A self-review after those rounds added three more: a scan reporting FEWER commits than the push publishes is a partial scan and looks identical to a complete one from outside, so the count is read as a number and compared with `-ge` — not for equality, which would refuse a force push onto a history with more than one merge base; the `--names` call is verified the same way, having its own way of succeeding without working; and the refusal for a name check that reported nothing must NOT repeat the name, since it is the candidate value and has not been checked. That last one was found by its own new test, three lines after being written — the detected-but-not-redacted shape, in the check added to close a disclosure. Every mechanism mutation-verified — five of them twice, because the first probe came back green and it was the TEST that had never reached the path it named.
  - **Agent Skills spec limits (`test_portability.py::TestSkillLayout`):** the frontmatter checks used to stop at "name matches directory, description non-empty", which are this repo's own rules rather than the published spec's, so a skill could satisfy every assertion and still be rejected or truncated by an agent. `TestSkillLayout` now also pins `name` ≤64 chars matching `^[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*$`, `description` 1–1024 chars containing a "Use when" trigger, `SKILL.md` ≤500 lines, `license` equal to the repository's own (the VALUE, not merely the key: this is the only license signal that travels with a skill folder copied out of this repo, and a wrong one travels exactly as far as a right one — with `test_the_license_file_is_what_the_skills_declare` pinning `LICENSE` itself so the constant cannot drift from the text it names), and `references/` checked in all THREE directions, since none implies the others and each fails silently in its own way: a **dangling** link is a step the agent is told to read and cannot, an **unlinked** file is content no agent loads, and a **nested** file defeats `skill_text()` (which globs one level down), dropping out of every doc-sync assertion at once while they all stay green. `OVERSIZED` names the skills not yet restructured and asserts in BOTH directions: everything outside it is under the limit, and a paired test fails the moment an entry inside it is already under, so the list can only shrink. `xfail` was rejected for exactly that reason — it would have absorbed a FOURTH skill drifting over the limit silently, which is the "no silent caps" rule applied to a test. `test_frontmatter_values_have_no_unquoted_colon` is the one check whose subject is not the spec but this file's own `_frontmatter()` helper: it splits on the first colon and accepts anything after it, while every other agent uses a real YAML parser where an unquoted scalar containing `': '` is a parse error — and a skill that fails to parse disappears with no message, on precisely the agents this repo has no CI for.
  - **Doc-sync assertions read the skill TREE, not `SKILL.md` (`tests/skill_text.py`):** progressive disclosure moved the `tax-compute` step detail, the `tax-setup` interview and the `tax-review` report templates into `references/*.md`, and every assertion that greps a skill body would otherwise have gone green *because the text was gone* — indistinguishable from its having been deleted, which is the exact failure those assertions exist to catch. One `skill_text(name)` helper concatenates `SKILL.md` with every `references/*.md`, and it is sound only because `test_reference_files_are_one_level_deep` pins that each reference is ONE link-hop from `SKILL.md`; without that pair, content buried where nothing links to it would still count. It is deliberately a single shared module rather than a copy per test file — the PII scanner's hand-mirrored second copy is how four bypasses shipped to both at once. Re-pointed: the capability-flag interview coverage (all 27 rule flags now live only in `references/interview.md`), the `type: "none"` state allowlist doc, the four neutralization lints, and the OBBBA/QBI/big-ticket skill-doc checks. **Two assertions deliberately did NOT widen.** `test_web_dependent_skills_carry_a_stale_data_degrade` splits: "search the web" may be satisfied anywhere in the tree (a fetch list is ordinary step detail), but the STOP-and-paste sentence is still required in `SKILL.md` itself, because the whole body loads when the skill activates while a reference is read only if the agent chooses to — a gate the agent can decline to load is a gate that silently over-states completeness. `tax-compute` Steps 0.5, 0.7 and 12.7 are held to the same rule and are byte-identical to their pre-split text. Each re-pointed family was mutation-verified (delete the flag, plant a Claude-only tool name in a reference, invert the STOP sentence) rather than trusted to pass.
  - **The installer installs the DIRECTORY, not the body (`test_portability.py::TestInstaller`):** `install.sh` linked and copied `SKILL.md` alone, which was correct while a skill *was* a file. Progressive disclosure made it a directory, and the two paths that populate `~/.claude/skills/` were not updated — so a global install shipped `tax-compute` as a 110-line stub naming five files that were absent from the installed tree, with 1588 lines of procedure unreachable. Nothing errors: the agent reads a well-formed skill, finds no procedure at Step 1 and improvises the return, and Step 12.7's tie-out gate does not catch it because correctly-parsed documents tie out against an invented computation. `test_reference_files_are_installed_alongside_the_body` drives the real script under a sandboxed `HOME` in BOTH modes and reads the installed reference *through*, so a symlink resolving nowhere fails like a missing file; `test_upgrading_a_file_only_install_gains_the_references` covers the migration every existing install needs, where the destination is already a real directory holding just a `SKILL.md`. Driving the script rather than grepping it is the same rule the `.gitignore` tests record — and the reason the gap survived a class of six install.sh assertions is that every one of them grepped.

  - **The engine preflight's fail-closed half is in the SKILL text, not the script (`test_preflight.py`):** the installer bullet above covers a skill installed without its `references/`; this is the same separation one level out — a skill copied away from `agents/lib/`, which is a SIBLING of `.agents/skills/` and never travels with it. `tax-compute` shells out to three gates (`tax_law_fetcher.py freshness`, `capabilities.py`, `tie_out.py`), so a copied skill runs three commands that do nothing while the body still reads as a complete procedure — the tie-out gate's absence being indistinguishable, downstream, from its passing. **`preflight.py` structurally cannot report its own absence**, shipping inside the very directory whose disappearance is the failure, so the block is carried by a sentence in each skill making a failure to RUN the check the blocking condition rather than a step to skip; `test_the_gate_names_the_command_and_fails_closed` asserts that sentence verbatim per skill, because it is the entire mechanism and prose is where it lives. The skill roster is DERIVED from the skill text rather than hardcoded, so a new engine-calling skill is covered the day it is written — but a derived roster fails in its own direction, by SHRINKING invisibly: the per-skill assertions iterate whatever survives, so a predicate that stops matching half the skills still reports every skill it can see as covered. The first version matched only the PATH form (`agents/lib/tie_out.py`) and so missed all eleven skills invoking the engine as a MODULE (`python3 -m agents.lib.projection`) — every planning skill, whose entire output is a number the taxpayer acts on, plus `tax-archive` and `tax-rollover`. Three assertions now guard the roster rather than the skills: `test_the_roster_recognises_both_invocation_forms` drives the predicate directly on one string of each form plus a negative, and `test_the_gate_is_not_vacuous` pins a floor plus one named positive control per form, since a roster that collapses to empty (a moved directory, a renamed path) satisfies the per-skill assertion over nothing and reads exactly like full coverage. The known-answer probe exists because importable is not correct: a bare existence check passes against a half-applied engine, and `compute_bracket_tax` is driven with SYNTHETIC brackets deliberately — a real one would rot under AGENTS.md §2 and would mislabel the failure, presenting a stale figure as a broken engine and sending the reader to reinstall instead of refetch. `SystemExit` is caught alongside `Exception` in both the import and the probe: it is not an `Exception`, and uncaught it would end the process with the interpreter's status rather than this module's — reporting "could not run" (1) or a false clean (0) for what is a broken engine (2). Contributor skills are exempt and asserted exempt, since they work ON the toolkit from a clone that by definition contains it. **The MODULE roster had the same shrinkage bug the skill roster did, one layer down.** It listed only the four gate-bearing modules, on the reasoning that they import each other so a partial copy surfaces as an ImportError against a listed one — true within that cluster, and false for every leaf. Nothing imports `projection`, and `projection` IS what `/tax-project` does; a copy keeping the four gates and dropping the leaves therefore printed `OK` and exited 0 while thirteen of twenty-one modules were absent and eleven skills had no computation left to run, which is the gate certifying exactly what it exists to refuse. `ENGINE_MODULES` now covers every module in `agents/lib/`, each paired with the skill it powers so a failure line says what stopped working rather than only that something did. The roster is written down rather than globbed at runtime for the same reason the skill roster is derived: globbing is self-fulfilling here — a module that failed to travel is simply not listed, and the check passes over its own blind spot — so `test_the_roster_lists_every_engine_module` pins the list against the directory instead, which is what catches an ADDED module, and `test_a_leaf_module_no_one_imports_is_still_checked` names three leaves as the positive control. **A verdict about the wrong interpreter is not a verdict (`TestInterpreter`).** The engine runs as `python3 …`, and some agents open a fresh shell per command, so an activation stated once at the top of a block does not reach the next one: on a stock macOS the preflight could certify a supported virtualenv while `python3 -m agents.lib.projection` ran on /usr/bin/python3, a 3.9 this toolkit does not support. The activation is therefore chained into EVERY engine invocation, `test_every_engine_invocation_activates_the_workspace_venv` holds that per skill across `SKILL.md` and its references, and `_venv_error` blocks when a workspace `.venv` exists that the running interpreter is not in — enforcement, since the prose alone is what a reviewer is right to distrust. The primitive is `sys.prefix`, never `sys.executable`: a venv's `bin/python3` is a symlink chain out to the base interpreter, so resolving the executable lands OUTSIDE the venv and would have blocked every correctly activated run — `test_the_symlinked_executable_is_not_the_test` builds that symlink and pins it. **That audit then repeated the shrinkage bug a third time, and it is the same shape every time: a roster derived by a predicate that matches less than it is believed to.** The audit read one LINE at a time and matched only the path and `-m` forms, so it never saw the three inline `python3 -c "…"` commands whose `from agents.lib.tie_out import …` lands on the line AFTER the `python3` — neither half is an engine call by itself, and the test passed over commands it had not looked at. `_shell_units` now yields command UNITS rather than lines, running an inline `-c` on to the line that closes its opening quote, and a unit is an invocation when it both runs python AND names the engine: prose about `agents/lib/` carries no `python3`, and `/tax-parse`'s warning against inline `-c` strings names no engine module, so neither is flagged without an exemption list to drift. `test_the_audit_sees_an_inline_engine_import` drives the predicate on the multiline shape plus both negatives, and all four prefixes are mutation-verified — removing any one turns the audit red. `/tax-setup`'s dependency probe gets the same prefix and its own assertion: it is not an engine call, so the audit does not reach it, but unactivated it certifies that the HOST has `pdfplumber` while `/tax-parse` runs `parse_utils` inside `.venv`, and the `[ -d .venv ]` guard leaves the first run, before any venv exists, behaving as it did. `test_the_remedy_is_activation_not_a_reclone` covers the other half of a refusal: "re-clone the toolkit" is useless advice to someone whose only problem is an unactivated virtualenv, and following it fixes nothing, so the two failures carry separate remedies. **A remedy that cannot resolve what was reported is worse than none** — it is followed, it fails, and the reader is back at the same block with no route out, which for this gate means every skill stays blocked. Two such loops are pinned. Under Python 3.9 the engine remedy said `git clone` then `python3 -m venv`, and a venv inherits the version of the `python3` that builds it, so the reader lands on 3.9 again: `PYTHON_REMEDY` names a supported interpreter explicitly, `MIN_PYTHON_STR` is derived so the failure line and the remedy cannot drift into stating two different minimums, and it is ordered FIRST because an old interpreter presents AS a missing engine — ten modules use PEP 604 `X | None`, so 3.9 reports them all unloadable and a reader led with "re-clone" re-fetches files that were never the problem (`test_an_old_python_leads_with_the_version_not_the_reclone`). The second loop is a `.venv` that exists but cannot be activated — no `bin/activate`, or a `bin/python3` symlink left dangling when the base interpreter was upgraded away — where the activation remedy IS what just failed, and `/tax-setup` is no escape because it opens with this same gate and stops before reaching its own environment step. `_venv_defect` tells the causes apart (`.exists()` follows symlinks, which is the point) and `venv_broken` rides on the report as STATE rather than being re-derived by matching the message text, so rewording the diagnosis cannot silently swap the advice back to the one that loops. **A third cause then showed that diagnosis-by-enumeration has the same failure direction as a derived roster: the case not enumerated gets the advice that loops.** Move or rename a workspace and its `.venv` passes every structural check — `bin/activate` is a real file and survives, `bin/python3` is a symlink into a base interpreter that never moved — while `activate` still exports the absolute path it was BUILT at and prepends a `bin` that no longer exists, so `source` returns 0, prints nothing, and leaves `python3` on the host interpreter. The reader had already chained the activation and was answered by being told to chain it. `_relocated` reads the `VIRTUAL_ENV=` assignment back out of `activate` — `activate` rather than `pyvenv.cfg`, because `activate` is what the remedy actually runs, while `pyvenv.cfg`'s paths point at a base interpreter that relocating does not touch — and the comparison is `os.path.samefile`, never string equality, since a symlinked workspace or a case-insensitive filesystem spells the same directory two ways and `test_a_symlinked_path_to_the_same_venv_is_the_same_venv` pins that a working setup is not sent to `rm -rf` over a spelling. An assignment it cannot parse means "no literal to contradict" and never "relocated": 3.14 writes a `$(cygpath …)` form above the plain one, and an activate script that DERIVES its own location is by construction not broken by being moved. Each defect now carries its own consequence clause instead of a shared one, because a moved venv's `source` SUCCEEDS — telling that reader "activating it cannot have succeeded" contradicts their own terminal on the first line, and advice contradicted there is not read to its second. `test_a_real_venv_moved_on_disk_is_caught` builds a real venv with `venv.EnvBuilder`, renames its parent and replays: the hand-written fixtures encode what this suite BELIEVES CPython writes, and only building one can say whether the belief still holds on the interpreter running the suite — the quoting changed between 3.9 and 3.14 with nothing to warn when it changes again. Finally, `VENV_REMEDY` now ends with the rebuild conditioned on "if you had ALREADY chained it", which is a state the reader can check and this check cannot: enumeration will miss a fourth cause, and the point is that missing one stops meaning there is no way out. **Landing this on a `main` that had moved underneath it produced three more, all from the same seam — two directories that each branch had grown independently.** `secure_fs` was added to `agents/lib/` by the permissions work and so was absent from a module roster written before it existed; `test_the_roster_lists_every_engine_module` is what caught it, which is the pin doing exactly its job, and it is NOT one of the thirteen leaves — all four gates import it, so a roster admitting only what the gates cannot reach would have had to know that, which is why the roster is whole rather than reasoned about. The forty-six `secure_fs` invocations that same work added to the skills predated the activation rule and were all unchained. And the audit that found them then reported a FALSE positive on the one it should not have: `_shell_units` joined an inline `-c` body but not a `\`-continued line, so `/tax-amend`'s `find … -print0 \` / `| xargs -0 sh agents/lib/run.sh -m agents.lib.secure_fs` was read as two units and the activation chained onto the `find` did not count for the `python3` two lines down — the same shrinkage bug a fourth time, in the direction that refuses correct data, which is how a gate teaches the reader to reason past it. **`preflight.py` also joined the redaction gate by existing:** that gate derives its module list from `agents/lib/*.py`, and `_main` printed a bare `{e}` from a `check()` that can raise an OSError naming the workspace. The first fix wrapped the whole rendered verdict, which passed the gate and broke the scenario this module exists for — `secure_fs` is part of the engine being checked, so a copy missing it has nothing to redact WITH, and the agent got a withheld marker where it needed the block, the module list and the remedy. Only the VARIABLE parts are redacted now, `_redact` imports lazily so the preflight cannot die of the ImportError it exists to report, and `test_the_verdict_survives_a_missing_redactor` is the counterweight that pins the trade in both directions. **An xhigh review of the rebased branch then found thirteen more, and the cluster is instructive: most sat in the REMEDY layer of the module whose thesis is that a remedy which cannot resolve its failure is worse than none.** Three separate paths ended with the reader destroying a working environment. The remedy ORDER led with `rm -rf .venv` whenever `python_ok` was false — and on a stock macOS an unactivated supported workspace venv IS that state, host `python3` being 3.9 and every PEP 604 module then reporting missing, so the advice deleted the one interpreter that would have fixed the run; the unactivated venv is now the root cause and leads, with the version demoted to the case where activating is not enough. On Windows the whole gate was an inescapable loop: the skills' chained command and both venv remedies hardcoded `.venv/bin/activate` while a stdlib venv there lives in `Scripts/`, so `source` no-oped, the block advised the command that had just no-oped, and its own escape hatch rebuilt `Scripts/` and looped — `_venv_layout` now ASKS the venv on disk rather than guessing from `os.name`, which is wrong exactly when it matters because the check runs on the interpreter that did not build it. The third, a CRLF `activate` reading as RELOCATED, was REFUTED: two independent things stop it (`Path.read_text()`'s universal-newline translation, and `_activate_target`'s `.strip()`), and the guard added for it goes red only when BOTH are removed — which is what a redundant-protection test should do, and is why it says so in its own docstring. `--workspace` joined the CLI, since every sibling gate takes it and without it the interpreter check silently resolved `.venv` against the CWD; `_venv_error` now returns its defect alongside its message instead of `check()` re-deriving it, which removed both the duplicated I/O and a real divergence on the OSError branch. **Wiring the new module into the PostToolUse hook then exposed a THIRD copy of the check-name list.** CLAUDE.md names two — `WATCHED_ROOTS` and the hook's `_ARMS` — and adding `preflight` to both, plus `apply_red_checks` and `remember_red_checks`, still left `read_red_checks`'s own token allowlist behind: the hook wrote `preflight` into the stamp and then dropped it as junk on the way back in, so a still-red module reported clean. `test_the_check_name_lists_agree` compares all three as sets now. The watch entry is the DIRECTORY `agents/lib`, deliberately: the roster test pins against `agents/lib/*.py`, so only an ADD or REMOVE can turn it red, and that shows up as the directory's own timestamp moving — editing `tax_computations.py` still selects nothing and stays silent. **Two of the findings were against the tests themselves, and both are the vacuity shape this file keeps meeting.** `test_the_gate_is_not_vacuous` stopped discriminating the day the gate landed: every skill now carries `sh agents/lib/run.sh agents/lib/preflight.py`, a PATH form, so its `>= 20` floor and both named controls pass against the path-only predicate that is the original bug (mutation-verified). The replacement excludes the preflight command and reads command UNITS rather than raw text — the gate RATIONALE is prose naming `agents/lib/` in all twenty skills, so a line filter proves nothing either. And the per-skill gate assertions read `skill_text()`, which concatenates `SKILL.md` with its `references/`, so a gate MOVED into a reference file satisfied every one of them while the agent need never load it; that is settled precedent here — the stale-data STOP sentence is required in `SKILL.md` itself — so the gate now is too, and its position ahead of the first engine invocation is pinned rather than habitual. **A second xhigh pass then found that two of those fixes were HALF fixes, and both half-fixes had passed a test I wrote for them.** The remedy reorder changed which advice came FIRST and not which was EMITTED, so `rm -rf .venv` still printed in the unactivated case — under a sentence ("recreating `.venv` with the interpreter that just failed leaves it failing") that is FALSE there, the `.venv` being the supported interpreter nobody activated. The test pinned ORDER, which is exactly what a half-fix satisfies. The other remedies are SUPPRESSED now, and that is the honest verdict rather than merely the safe one: every other failure on that report was observed on the wrong interpreter and is a symptom until the right one is running. The hook narrowing was the same shape — the `agents/lib` arm was reduced to the bare directory on the reasoning that only an ADD or REMOVE can break the roster test, which is true of the roster test and false of the module: `check()` IMPORTS all twenty-one modules and executes one, so a SyntaxError in `projection/` turns test_preflight red while the hook, selecting nothing, exits 0 silent. Verified by breaking it. The cost trade the three older silence tests encoded expired with that, and they now name paths no watched module reads. **The drift test added to catch the third copy of the check names pinned three of SIX places**, and the two it missed are the two that fail permanently: omit a check from `run_focused_modules` and its flag is set by every trigger, written to the stamp on every red verdict, restored on every later pass, and never handed to pytest — carried red forever while its assertions never run; omit it from `nothing_selected` and the fast path still exits 0. All seven lists are compared as sets now, both additions mutation-verified. Smaller ones from the same pass: `_venv_error` compares with `os.path.samefile` rather than `==` on resolved paths, which does not canonicalise CASE and so refused a correctly activated run on any case-insensitive filesystem (the test probes for one and skips where the defect cannot occur); its OSError branch returns a DEFECT rather than an empty string, since rendering an unstattable `.venv` as "merely unactivated" answered it with the activation remedy that had just failed; `--json` carries the rendered remedy, because a consumer given `blocking: true` and no way to proceed has the one thing `render_markdown`'s docstring forbids; three module-level remedy constants had no readers at all and were a second layout-fixed rendering of texts the functions produce — the third-copy drift `_rebuild_steps` was introduced to remove, reintroduced by the change that removed it. The extracted `no-income-tax-state-review.md` ends by sending the reader back to step 4, which is the fix `references/interview.md` already earned once: an agent walking a reference in order hits EOF with no instruction, and a survival-based relocation audit cannot see it because nothing was deleted. **A third pass found that the fix protecting the redactor had disabled it in the one scenario this module exists for.** `_redact` imports `secure_fs` lazily and withholds when it cannot — correct when `secure_fs` really is absent, and catastrophic on an old interpreter, because `secure_fs` carried a PEP 604 annotation evaluated at def time and so did not import on 3.9 either. Run under a stock macOS `/usr/bin/python3`, every one of the twenty-one module errors came out as `<withheld: the redactor is part of the missing engine>` under a banner saying the engine was missing, when the engine was complete and only the interpreter was old — and the single line naming that (`TypeError: unsupported operand type(s) for |`) was the one suppressed. `secure_fs` now carries `from __future__ import annotations`, which is a rule about THAT module and not about the engine: it is the redactor preflight reaches for before it can report anything, so it alone must import on an interpreter the modules it reports on legitimately reject. Two tests, one structural and one running the real old binary where a second interpreter exists, both mutation-verified. **`--json` leaked the workspace path raw** while every sibling field was redacted, invisible to the structural gate because that walks `raise` and `print` nodes and this was a dict value handed to `json.dumps`. `_relocated`'s absence check moved from `Path.exists()` — which swallows EACCES and answers False, i.e. "demonstrably absent", the conflation its own comment ruled out — to `os.lstat` with an explicit ENOENT. MEASURED COSTS was RE-MEASURED end to end rather than patched: the previous revision shipped a hooks count of 105 on a branch that had added a test to that module, and derived rows that were arithmetic over stale parts. Counts are derivable from one collection and a stale one has no excuse; the readings beside them were not, which is why they carried run counts until ruling Q13 retired them. Two smaller ones are worth the line because of HOW they were found: the watched-set coverage test scans raw file text for `REPO`-relative literals, so a test-side comment EXPLAINING why the virtualenv must not be spelled that way tripped it by containing the spelling; and four tests that assert the gate passes now skip, rather than fail, when the suite itself runs off the workspace venv — an environment mistake was reporting as four assertions about the code. **Rounds five and six then produced the sharpest bug of the series, and it was ordinary shell:** tax-fbar's foreign-payer cross-check is an inline `python3 -c` whose body sat INDENTED inside a list item, and only **3.14+** auto-dedents a `-c` body — this sentence said 3.13+ until it was checked against `Doc/using/cmdline.rst`, so below that it exits 1 with `IndentationError`, and the interpreter a skill's `python3 -c` lands on is whatever the HOST resolves rather than any version this repository supports, which is why raising the floor does not retire the rule and the whole step silently never runs, so a foreign payer in `parse_ledger.json` and absent from the FBAR account table would never have been surfaced. `test_an_inline_python_body_is_not_indented` walks every skill's command units, and a repo-wide scan found no second instance. The same pass caught the OK verdict claiming an engine 'present and computing correctly' with NONE of the four pinned dependencies installed: every third-party import in the engine is lazy or guarded, so all twenty-one modules import and the probe passes on a clone where `pip install` never ran, and `/tax-parse` is reachable without `/tax-setup`'s own probe — `RUNTIME_DEPENDENCIES` closes it by IMPORTING each package — `find_spec` answers only "is there a package by this name", which a half-installed or ABI-broken `pdfplumber` satisfies while its `__init__` raises. It also found the gate verifying by IMPORT while every step it protects invokes by PATH, so a tree holding only `preflight.py` on a `pip install -e`'d machine reported the engine present and `tax-compute` Step 12.7 then exited 127; `_load` checks the file beside itself first. **The Windows activation was fixed rather than documented away** — the chained prefix is layout-tolerant across all 116 sites, so a `Scripts/` venv activates instead of silently no-opping and blocking every skill with no way forward. **And the headroom warning fired for real:** the suite outgrew the 180s hook timeout's implied floor mid-round, which is precisely the silent-cancel the MEASURED COSTS block warns about, so the registered timeout moved to 300s. Two findings were the previous round's own overreach — promoting the gate heading nested a Steps section inside a Steps section, and the tax-ptet move carried `## Usage`'s code block with it, leaving an empty heading and two lists both restarting at 1. **A seventh pass found the gate certifying a module that would exit 0 having done nothing.** Every check in it asks whether a module IMPORTS, and no skill imports: `tax-compute` Step 12.7 RUNS `tie_out.py` and acts on its exit code. A file cut at a statement BOUNDARY between its last function and its `if __name__ == "__main__":` tail passes the file check, the import and the roster-symbol check alike — reproduced at 18 KB of `tie_out.py`'s 107 KB, where the verdict named "the intake-to-result tie-out gate" as available and running the file exited 0 with no output, which that step reads as TIED OUT and the dropped-1099 CP2000 sails through. Zero bytes was the same failure at a coarser cut and was already caught; a cut mid-statement raises SyntaxError; this is the middle band, and it is the one that looks healthiest. `_cli_dispatch_error` reads the SOURCE for a column-anchored guard — an indented one never runs at module scope — rather than paying twenty-one subprocesses on the first step of all twenty skills; `test_such_a_file_really_exits_zero_when_run` pins the half of the claim that lives outside this module, and `test_every_engine_module_carries_the_dispatch` keeps the new check off the gate-that-cries-wolf side by pinning AGENTS.md §5's CLI-entry-point rule structurally for the first time. Its remedy is the shared `import_failure_remedy`, whose opening now covers all three shapes (raised, stubbed, headless) instead of asserting an import failure over a module that had just imported — a mismatch the stub case had carried since it landed. Three smaller findings from the same pass, each a shape this file already records: the known-answer probe tested `abs(got - expected) > 0.005`, which NaN defeats SILENTLY because every comparison with NaN is False, so a damaged math module returning NaN was certified as computing correctly — `math.isfinite` runs first now, which is the rule AGENTS.md §8 already states for every parser under `agents/lib/`; `--json` exported four venv states and not `no_workspace_venv`, leaving a consumer with `blocking: false`, an empty `venv_error` (that condition deliberately does not write it) and an empty `remedy` — indistinguishable from a healthy workspace, so it computes on the host interpreter with unpinned versions, the same one-channel omission `probe_ran` was added for, and `no_venv_remedy` now owns the steps both channels print; and `_cd_step` emitted the workspace root UNQUOTED, so an ordinary root holding a space (`~/Documents/Tax Prep`) rendered `cd /Users/x/Tax Prep`, which is two arguments — and since every remedy CHAINS off that step with `&&`, the entire recovery path failed on its first command for a reader who is already blocked. **An eighth pass found the two fail-opens left in the VERDICT itself, and both are the gate certifying something it never checked.** `blocking` deliberately excludes a missing dependency, on the recorded premise that an absent package surfaces LOUDLY at first use as an ImportError — true of three of the four and false of the one that matters, because `parse_utils` CATCHES the `defusedxml` failure, warns on a stderr no agent reads, and opens the workbook with the stdlib XML parser: a crafted `.xlsx` among a taxpayer's source documents is then parsed with no XXE guard and nothing anywhere fails. The fix goes where the false premise lives rather than into the gate — `extract_excel` REFUSES before it imports openpyxl (AGENTS.md §1: source documents are untrusted input), which makes the warning into the first-use failure the gate's comment already claimed, while `/tax-calendar` and every other skill that opens no workbook keeps running; blocking globally on it would be the cry-wolf shape this file keeps recording. The second: the probe imports four packages and the OK line then read "Pinned dependencies importable" — true, and not what the lock is for. AGENTS.md §7 pins the whole TRANSITIVE tree by hash because pdfplumber 0.11.10 shipped no pdfplumber code at all, only a `pdfminer.six` bump, and `pdfminer.six` is the engine document parsing actually runs on, so a venv built by `pip install pdfplumber openpyxl reportlab defusedxml` imports all four and parses a return on a resolution nobody tested. `_drifted_dependencies` compares every INSTALLED distribution against the lock's pins and reports as a loud WARNING for the reason above, with the pass line withdrawn rather than reworded. Four ways it could have fail-opened are pinned, each mutation-verified: PEP 503 canonicalisation on BOTH sides (the lock says `pdfminer-six`, the metadata says `pdfminer.six`, and compared raw the one package whose republication changes what a document parses to is the one silently skipped); a SET of versions per name, since `--universal` legitimately pins one name twice under opposite markers and comparing against whichever line came last reports the CI runner's own correct install as drift; an unreadable lock and a lock that parses to NO pins both report NOT VERIFIED, never "no drift", because an empty pin table certifies every installed version of everything; and a vacuity guard driving all four direct pins through as wrong versions, since the regex is one character from matching nothing. `requirements.txt` joined `WATCHED_ROOTS` and the hook's case arm with it — the gate reads the lock now, so a Dependabot bump that drops a pin turns `test_preflight` red and that is the arm that says so locally. **Two smaller findings from the same pass, both about a message rather than a computation.** `run.sh`'s one failure path echoed `$0`, which is absolute whenever a skill invokes the launcher by an absolute path, and the tree it sits under is named by the person who made it; the shell's own `cd: can't cd to …` underneath disclosed it just as completely, so both are gone and the path is DROPPED rather than masked, a POSIX shell having no redactor to reach for. That half is pinned STRUCTURALLY and the test says why: opening `<id>/agents/lib/run.sh` and `cd`-ing to `<id>` need the same traversal permission on `<id>`, so no test can produce an absolute `$0` with a failing `cd`, and a behavioural test that ran anyway would assert an absence it had no way to produce — the vacuity shape, met head-on rather than shipped. The fail-closed half stays behavioural, with a counterweight that the same tree still runs. And the hook's `agents|scripts` catch-all matched those two words EXACTLY — a POSIX `case` pattern is not a prefix — so `agents/tools/check.py` and `scripts/helpers/check.sh` selected nothing while `test_secure_fs` sweeps both trees whole for a network import, a bare `chmod` and a raw path reaching a message; the descendant arms are scoped to `*.py`/`*.sh` so that `__pycache__/*.pyc`, rewritten by every engine run, cannot put a pytest module behind the next `ls`. **A ninth pass closed the OTHER direction of that comparison and moved the severity to where it belongs.** `_drifted_dependencies` walked the INSTALLED distributions and compared the ones the lock also named, so a locked package that nothing provides matched nothing, was compared against nothing, and came back as a clean tree — under a verdict that then certified "at the locked versions". The four import probes do not reach it either: they import the four DIRECT dependencies, and what can be absent without an import failing is the transitive tail nothing loads until it needs it (`cryptography` only on an encrypted PDF, `pillow` and `pypdfium2` only on a page render). An absence is now a drift record with `None` in the version slot — a STATE, so a JSON consumer separates "wrong version" from "gone" without matching on prose it does not control. Deciding it needs the lock's environment MARKERS, previously discarded because a marker cannot change whether an installed version matches: a `--universal` lock names packages this interpreter is not supposed to have, and reporting one of those raises an alarm the printed remedy cannot clear, since `pip install -r requirements.txt` will not install a pin whose marker excludes the machine — a warning that survives its own fix being the one an agent learns to skip. `_marker_applies` therefore answers True/False/**None**, and the third answer is the whole design: `packaging.markers` is the real evaluator and is not in the lock, so the workspace this check certifies cannot import it, and a hand-rolled one that guessed at an unfamiliar marker would put the certification straight back where this pass found it. Undecidable is REPORTED, and only ever for an absent pin, since a marker cannot make an installed package un-installed; a definite exclusion outranks an undecidable sibling in an `and` chain, or `cffi ; implementation_name != 'PyPy' and <something new>` becomes a permanent warning on the one machine where it is plainly correct. `test_every_marker_in_the_shipped_lock_is_decidable` is what keeps the deliberately small evaluator big enough, failing on the commit that regenerates the lock — when a human is already reading the lock diff — rather than degrading a taxpayer's workspace later; the undecidable cases are parametrised over all four reasons it refuses, because the structural ones (`or`, parentheses) exit at a different guard from the term-level ones (an unknown variable, an unreadable release, an ordering comparison on an opaque string), and one case leaves whichever path it misses untested — which the mutation run caught doing exactly that. **The severity is now two severities at two boundaries.** The gate still WARNS: blocking it refuses /tax-calendar, /tax-scenario and every skill that opens no document, on a signal parsed out of a text file. `parse_utils` REFUSES, at all five document entry points, before the path is validated and before pdfplumber or openpyxl is imported — that is where an untested parser turns into a wrong figure in an unpredictable direction, and no downstream check can catch it, `tax-review` auditing the aggregated result and the tie-out comparing the ledger to the result, so a misparsed box ties out perfectly. The refusal is scoped by a PARTITION of the lock (`_PARSING_TREE` / `_GENERATION_TREE`) pinned by `test_every_locked_distribution_is_classified`: refusing to read a 1099 because the PDF *generator* was upgraded is the cry-wolf shape, and a new transitive package landing in neither set — what an ordinary bump produces — would silently escape the check. `extract_csv` is deliberately outside it (stdlib `csv`, no locked package, and /tax-parse-crypto runs on exchange CSVs), an unreadable lock refuses rather than passing as verified, and one test asserts the real workspace refuses NOTHING, since a refusal that fires on a correct tree is the same defect one bit over. The fifth entry point is the one most easily missed and the only one where placement is load-bearing: `scan documents/` is the FIRST command /tax-parse runs, it opens every PDF to classify it, and its output is the routing table for everything after — inside a `try` that turns any exception into a `doc_type: "error"` entry and a stderr line, so a refusal placed one line lower degrades into a warning and the scan returns a list built by the parser it was refusing. It sits OUTSIDE that handler and PER-PDF, both pinned by their own test, since hoisting it to the top of the scan refuses a CSV-only workspace that opens no locked parser at all. **A tenth pass made that comparison pick the RIGHT pin.** The versions for one name were pooled into a set and an installed version accepted if it matched any of them, which is correct only while a name is pinned once. A `--universal` lock pins one name TWICE under opposite markers (`python_version < '3.12'` and `>=`) at two different versions, and the pool then accepts the version meant for the other interpreter as locked — so the parse boundary reads the tree as pinned and opens a taxpayer's documents on a stack the suite never ran there. Each version is now kept PAIRED with its own line's marker verdict, and `_pins_here` splits a name's pins into the ones that apply here and the ones whose marker could not be read; a definitely EXCLUDED pin is in neither, so it is neither required nor compared against — PyPy ships its own `cffi`, and `pip install -r requirements.txt` will neither install nor remove it. Three states fall out, and each is a channel rather than a guess: installed-and-not-the-applicable-version is drift, reported against the applicable pin ALONE, since a record naming both lines tells a reader on this interpreter to install the one meant for the other; installed under an unreadable marker at a version no readable pin names goes to the undecided channel, answering it either way being an alarm the remedy cannot clear or a certification of an unexamined pin; and a lock naming two versions that BOTH apply is reported as undecidable rather than resolved by picking one — the pooled-set fail-open surviving in the single case where the pool is genuinely ambiguous. The twice-pinned test now derives its markers from the running interpreter's own minor: written against a fixed 3.12 it asserted the opposite of what it says on 3.10 and 3.11, which `MIN_PYTHON` admitted at the time.
  - **A reference file's own table of contents must not lie (`test_interview_contents_matches_where_the_groups_actually_live`):** `tax-setup` keeps interview Group 10 in `SKILL.md` deliberately — Step 9.5 runs `/tax-rollover` between Groups 9 and 10, and the rollover pre-fills the prior-year overpayment Group 10 asks about, so asking it inside the reference would make the taxpayer recall from memory a figure the next step supplies. That makes the TOC load-bearing, and it listed Group 10 with no note: an agent walking `references/interview.md` in order hit EOF one group short and the interview ended early, with the estimated-payment questions never asked. **Neither relocation audit could see it** — the atom-survival scan and the line-level set difference both look for text that VANISHED, and Group 10's text was moved rather than deleted, so both reported zero loss against broken navigation. The check pins that every group in the Contents exists somewhere in the skill tree, that any group NOT in the reference says where it lives, and that the file ends by sending the reader back. Mutation-verified against the pre-fix tree.
  - **An exemption from the engine gate has to be a claim, not a silence (`test_preflight.py::TestSkillGate`, `tests/skill_text.py`):** the engine-calling roster is DERIVED from skill text, which is what makes a new engine-calling skill covered the day it is written — and what makes the absence of a call unreadable. A skill that never calls the engine and a skill that LOST its engine call in an edit both simply fall out of the roster, so both stop being asserted against, identically and silently. `/tax-safe-harbor` is the first skill that is engine-free on purpose, so the property is declared in `ENGINE_FREE_SKILLS` and then tested: every member's text must reach the engine by neither path nor module, and the set must be disjoint from both the derived roster (a contradiction) and `CONTRIBUTOR_SKILLS` (a different exemption — those run from a clone that HAS the engine rather than avoiding it, and merging the two would leave a contributor skill's real engine calls unchecked). That is also what stops the declaration becoming the escape hatch it obviously could be: naming a skill there does not silence the gate, it asserts a property of the file, and a declared skill that grows an engine call fails HERE. Deliberately whole-text rather than per-`_shell_units` unlike every positive audit in that module: units exist because an inline `-c` body splits one command across LINES, a blind spot when proving a call is COVERED, and no blind spot at all when proving absence — every unit is a substring of the text, so the loop could never fire on its own and iterating it would advertise a third form being checked separately when it is not. Mutation-verified red against both the path/module form and a multi-line inline `-c` body appended to the skill.
  - **Prose that restates the engine's law is a second copy, and the arithmetic is a third (`test_portability.py::TestTheAdvisorySkillRestatesTheEnginesLawExactly`):** `/tax-safe-harbor` runs where `agents/lib/` is absent, so the one rule it needs — §6654's prior-year prong — is restated in its body instead of computed. That makes it the only place in the toolkit where a tax figure exists both as a constant the engine applies and as a sentence a reader acts on, with nothing structural keeping them equal: a statutory amendment lands in `projection` and leaves the skill quoting the old number to a taxpayer who pays the old amount, its arithmetic still perfectly self-consistent because it is consistently wrong. The expected strings are DERIVED from `PRIOR_YEAR_AGI_THRESHOLD{,_MFS}`, `PRIOR_YEAR_FACTOR{,_HIGH}`, `CA_ES_SCHEDULE` and the two CA cliff pairs rather than spelled out — a hardcoded `$150,000` would be a third copy with the same drift problem, green while it and the skill agreed with each other and not with the engine. Whitespace is collapsed first because the prose WRAPS (`30% / 40% / 0% / 30%` spans a line break, and a false red on correct text is how an assertion gets deleted rather than fixed). Parameters are the weaker half: the worked example is PARSED out of the body and re-run through `compute_safe_harbor` and `compute_w4_4c`, because prose can cite every threshold correctly and still print a product that is wrong, or divide by the wrong denominator, and no substring search notices. The §6654(e)(1) $1,000 floor is derived the same way, from `tax_computations.DE_MINIMIS_BALANCE` — the one §6654 figure the skill states without applying it, since that test reads current-year tax; a threshold quoted in a refusal drifts exactly as a threshold applied in a computation does. A money figure is matched with a RIGHT BOUNDARY (`(?![\d,.])`) rather than as a plain substring, because `"$500" in text` is true of a document that only ever says `$500,000` — and this class asserts both, so the CA de-minimis bullet could be deleted outright while the §19136.3 bullet kept the assertion green. Same vacuity the suite records for sha256 digests versus nine-digit SSNs, arriving as money; mutation-verified by deleting that bullet, which passed before the boundary and fails after. The engine is imported at CALL time, not module scope: every other assertion in that file reads the repository as FILES, and an import at the top would hand every portability test's COLLECTION to the health of a five-module chain, so one SyntaxError in the engine would report an unrelated ImportError instead of the four named subprocess failures a broken engine used to cost. Four further mutations verified red: the engine threshold moved, CA's zero September installment made nonzero, the example's product mistyped, and its divisor changed. The class's CA-farmer test is the same derivation on a case the schedule EXCLUDES: `_ca_notes` prints "not modeled: farmers/fishermen (FTB 5805F ... this 30/40/0/30 schedule does not apply to them)" beside every projected CA voucher run, so the skill computing that schedule by hand needs the same branch or it reports April and June shortfalls against a calendar holding one January instalment — cry-wolf, not conservative. Both the engine note and the skill are asserted, and the branch must appear BEFORE the weights: stated after them it is read after the wrong schedule has been applied. Two mutations verified red (the branch deleted; the branch moved below the weights) — note that replacing a single "5805F" occurrence does NOT go red, because the paragraph names the form twice.
  - **The three engine rosters PARTITION the skills tree (`test_preflight.py::TestSkillGate::test_the_three_rosters_partition_the_skills_tree`):** the declaration above closes the escape hatch (a named skill still has to BE engine-free) but not the shrinkage, because every assertion in that class ranges over a set a regression makes smaller. Remove the name from `ENGINE_FREE_SKILLS` and nothing requires that skill to be gated or declared; delete a skill's LAST engine call and it leaves the derived roster while `test_the_gate_is_not_vacuous`'s floor of 20 absorbs the loss — both holes are satisfied by a smaller tree, so neither is closeable by talking about either set. The fix is a statement about the whole directory: every `.agents/skills/` entry is engine-calling, declared engine-free, or contributor tooling, and nothing is in two of those. A skill can then only leave one roster by joining another, which is an edit to `tests/skill_text.py` rather than a side effect of editing a skill. Both halves mutation-verified red — the declaration emptied, and a member renamed so it names no directory (a stale declaration exempts nothing while reading as current).
  - **The advisory skill's instalment allocation is CUMULATIVE, and that is a deliberate divergence from the engine (`test_portability.py::TestTheAdvisorySkillAllocatesInstalmentsCumulatively`):** `compute_estimated_vouchers` and `compute_ca_estimated_vouchers` spread the annual shortfall across whichever deadlines are still open, a simplification their own docstrings name — each instalment is tracked cumulatively, so an even split puts less at the earliest open deadline than that deadline requires and leaves it penalised until the next lands. `/tax-safe-harbor` cannot carry that shape, its whole licence being "too high, never too low", so step 4 walks the deadlines (`required x n/4` against `expected full-year withholding x n/4` plus estimates dated on or before, floored at zero). The test parses the worked September figures and checks them against that identity, and against the annual gap the step-3 subtraction produced, so the two instalments still sum. Pinned against the identity and NOT against the engine on purpose: a later reader harmonizing the prose with `compute_estimated_vouchers` is reintroducing the understatement, and this is what fails when they do. The closing assertion is an INEQUALITY — the cumulative amount must exceed an even split — so it survives a rewrite of the example with different figures, and fails if the example ever shows the even split it claims to correct. Five mutations verified red, including substituting the even-split figure. Money captures are bounded `[\d,]+(?:\.\d\d)?`, not `[\d,.]+`, which swallows the sentence's closing period and raises `ValueError` where an assertion should have run. The class's second test pins CALIFORNIA, and has now been written both ways — which is why it is pinned against the FORM. FTB 5805 Worksheet II line 1 enters the required annual payment at 30/40/0/30 and line 2 enters Part II line 3, headed "Withholding taxes", at the SAME 30/40/0/30; Part I question 3 ("was your California withholding NOT withheld in equal installments") is the election OUT of that default, and the form's own Short Method — total withholding and estimates against the required annual payment, no per-column allocation — is coherent only because the two sides agree. A revision read the credited side as four equal quarters, which makes April 15 need 30% of the requirement against 25% of the withholding and reports a shortfall of 5% of the year's withholding for a taxpayer who owes nothing: the OVER-stating direction, and it reached the skill, both renderers' notes and a test asserting it. `compute_ca_estimated_vouchers`'s docstring was the source cited for it, which is the lesson — a docstring is not a citation. The test now asserts the required sequence PRESENT and equal quarters ABSENT, both derived from `CA_ES_SCHEDULE`, guards that the two still differ, and runs `_voucher_catchup_note` on the one case that separates the readings. Mutations verified red in both directions. A third test qualifies the paragraph's one sentence about WHEN a Californian pays: "only on the three dates that carry weight" is true of a scheduled instalment and false of a catch-up, since R&TC §19136(a) conforms to §6654 and (b)(2) runs an underpayment until it is PAID — so the sentence is asserted to distinguish the two and to date the catch-up to the day it is computed, rather than deferring a missed April to January 15.
  - **The handoff carries the limitations the better tool does not (`test_portability.py::TestTheAdvisorySkillCorrectsTheEngineItHandsOffTo`):** step 0 sends a workspace's computed-liability question only to `/tax-withholding`, because it reads `result.json` line 24; `/tax-project` is named only as a separate forecast that annualizes `projection_inputs.json` and must not replace the known liability. Federal prior-year intake is now fixed at the source: `_prior_tax_agi` ignores archived line 24 and the prong activates only from `projection_inputs.json.prior_year_form_2210_tax_base`, an explicitly completed covered-year Form 2210 line-8 pre-factor composition. The tests build a real template return to prove line 24 is ignored, assert the new field exists in the planning template, and pin Step 0 to the same unresolved/degraded contract. This added template read is mapped in `.claude/hooks/skill-invariants.sh`, so editing either input schema selects portability. The voucher limitation remains: after an installment deadline has closed, the undated-payment fallback can split the annual gap across open dates and underfund the earliest cumulative requirement, so Step 0 still names both voucher functions and routes to the cumulative test. The date half now pins two clocks: every valid stored `as_of_date` remains the annualization provenance even after year-end, while the real current date controls calendar notes and closes all covered-year W-4/DE 4 actions.
