  - **The advisory skill's intake is Form 2210's lines 8, 6 and 11, in all three cases entire (`test_portability.py::TestTheAdvisorySkillsIntakeIsTheForm2210Intake`):** every omission overstates what the taxpayer is told to pay — a refundable credit missing from figure 1 raises the harbor, excess social security missing from figure 4 or a credit-elected overpayment missing from figure 5 raises the gap — and the "too high, never too low" licence covers the choice of PRONG, not arithmetic on a short intake. The refundable roster is asserted in BOTH this skill and `/tax-compute`'s `references/tax-and-credits.md`, per credit and per SPELLING (a tuple of alternatives, so "EIC" satisfies the same entry as "earned income credit" and neither document is forced into the other's house style); they were already apart once, this skill's list having been hand-copied before Form 8839 line 13 became partly refundable. Only the refundable PART is netted — netting the whole credit shrinks the harbor, the forbidden direction. The excess-SS assertion is positional on both sides: present in figure 4, ABSENT from figure 1, because §31(b) makes it a credit for tax WITHHELD (Form 2210 line 6, ratable) and netting it against the prior year's tax instead would shrink the harbor. Figures are sliced by their numbered markers and the slice asserted non-empty by construction — a mis-slice would make "not in figure 1" vacuously true. Four mutations verified red, including migrating excess SS into the credit figure. `test_figure_4_admits_every_credit_form_2210_line_6_admits` adds line 6's third member: the instructions admit the Form 8689 credit for U.S. tax paid to the USVI beside the withholding lines, and figure 4 says nothing else is needed, so omitting it counts money already paid in as unpaid and inflates the gap. The match is bounded on the form NUMBER — `"8689" in text` is satisfied by anything merely starting with those digits, and the first mutation of it proved that by surviving. Two further asymmetries are pinned there: figure 1 is what line 8 is computed FROM, never line 8 itself (line 8 already carries step 2's factor, so reading it off an old 2210 applies the factor twice), and the refundable adoption credit is netted per the PRIOR YEAR, and it has been refundable TWICE. OBBBA makes part of it refundable for prior years beginning after 2024; ACA §10909 had already made it fully refundable for 2010 and 2011 by redesignating §23 as §36C, and both those years route it to Form 1040 line 71 rather than to Form 8839 line 13, which was a limit line on those forms. It reverted for 2012 — that year's Form 8839 instructions open "Credit is nonrefundable" — and stayed so through 2024, where an ungated instruction to subtract it removes a nonrefundable credit twice and understates the harbor. The gate shipped as "refundable only after 2024, wholly nonrefundable before", which is false on two years INSIDE the skill's own 2003 covered-year floor and false in the opposite direction: not subtracting a genuinely refundable credit makes figure 1 too HIGH, an overshoot in a skill licensed to overshoot only when it says so. `test_the_adoption_credit_gate_covers_both_refundable_windows` pins all four years, the reversion word, the year-specific line number, and the routing back to that year's own line-8 chart LABEL. A later PAIR of tests covers a couple whose status changed. Separate last year, joint this year takes ONE factor on the COMBINED prior-year AGI, applied to the summed tax: Reg. §1.6654-2(e)(3)(i) makes the prior year one quantity — "the sum of both the tax shown on the return of the taxpayer and the tax shown on the return of the taxpayer's spouse" — and §6654(d)(1)(C)(i) substitutes 110% inside clause (ii) of §6654(d)(1)(B), of which there is one, not one per spouse, while $150,000 is the joint-scale threshold (C)(ii) halves only for a filer SEPARATE in the covered year. This shipped as a factor per RETURN, cited to the ORDER of two paragraphs in the Form 2210 line-8 instructions, and the second test pins the honesty the first cannot: line 8 tests "the AGI on your 2024 return" singular and never per return, whether its "as figured earlier" reaches back past the 110% sentence is stated in neither the instructions nor Pub 505 nor IRM 20.1.3, the two readings diverge in exactly ONE window (combined AGI over $150,000, NEITHER separate AGI over it), and the higher is taken there because the lower can come out SHORT — with the ten per cent band said out loud rather than folded into the number. The converse case is pinned too: joint last year, separate this year tests the WHOLE joint AGI against the MFS threshold.

Every figure is year-keyed, so the covered year is fixed BEFORE the first figure is asked — asserted positionally, not by presence, because a year question stated after the prior-year figure has already collected the wrong return. The far end is §6654(b)(2): each instalment runs only to the earlier of payment or the 15th day of the fourth month after the year closes, which an extension does not move, so past that date the penalty is fixed and a voucher is inert as well as misdirected. The ceiling is parsed rather than spelled out and asserted to sit at or above the California floor, and the refusal must say what lifts it — AGENTS.md §2's stop is to ask the user for the current figures and their source. `test_a_nonstandard_instalment_calendar_is_refused_not_quartered` pins the third applicability question, which step 4 had assumed: §6654(k) substitutes the corresponding months for a year not beginning January 1, and §6654(j) gives a nonresident alien three instalments with 50 percent substituted for 25 at June 15 and withholding credited in thirds, so n/4 invents deadlines for both. Mutations verified red: the caveat deleted, the caveat moved below the intake, and the 2019 line-24 warning dropped while the rest of the paragraph stood. Round fifteen closed the half that round left open. One test over N files is the shape a sweep finding takes here.
  - **Two facts the skill already stated but could never REACH (`test_portability.py::TestTheAdvisorySkillReachesTheAnswersItAlreadyHolds`):** §6654(e)(2) — 12-month prior year, no liability for it, US citizen or resident throughout — is a complete no-penalty answer and requires no filed return, yet it sat two bullets below the "a return must have been filed" STOP, so someone under last year's filing threshold was refused instead of cleared. Step 4's timing check likewise reported which deadlines were missed and then scheduled the next voucher, never computing what was outstanding TODAY, while §6654(b)(2) runs an instalment's underpayment until it is PAID. Pub 505 chapter 2 supplies the second route ("your total tax ... was zero or you didn't have to file an income tax return"), and the carve-out is asserted to name it AND to keep it apart from a return that was required and never filed, whom §6654(d)(1)(B)'s closing text withdraws the prong from anyway. A total already under the floor therefore proves the prong however the credits fall, and the open question about whether §19002 withholding is a credit "provided by Part 10 or this part" never has to be answered. Handed over, the skill and the engine recommended the whole prior-year harbor against a $0 requirement — the overstatement PR #35 had already settled for the covered-year prong of the SAME subdivision, which is why a note beside a demanding schedule was not the fix and the shortfall is suppressed. The bound is ONE-DIRECTIONAL and the tests are asymmetric for that reason: under the floor proves no penalty, at or above it proves nothing either way. Chart C, "other situations when you must file", carries no income threshold at all, and the line of it this skill's own population trips is §6017's $400 of net self-employment earnings — someone paying estimated tax usually has income nobody withholds on, which is the same income that requires the return. That second assertion exists because the first mutation — reverting the body alone — stayed GREEN on the reference's copy. The reference holds the three charts, what each answer does next, and the instruction to read Chart A's indexed thresholds off the covered year's own instructions rather than from this repo. **And the proved prong then had to reach the two warnings it settles (`test_projection.py::TestTheProvedPriorYearFloorReachesEveryWarningItSettles`):** the flag was folded into `no_penalty`, which zeroes the shortfall and the vouchers, and stopped there — while the de-minimis CLIFF and the voucher TIMING caveat both went on asking `de_minimis` alone. So the same report that shows a $0 required payment told a taxpayer §19136(c)(2) clears outright that $100 more tax than projected makes a penalty attach, and that a late estimate may have one accruing "before concluding no CA penalty applies". Neither is possible on a year the prior-year prong carries: it is measured on a CLOSED year, so no projection of this one and no payment date of this one can move it — and the two floors hold together constantly, a small prior-year tax beside a small current-year balance being the ordinary shape of a small return. It is false whenever the prior-year prong is the lesser one: 110% of a $100,000 prior base is $110,000, and against a $130,000 actual tax that leaves $20,000 owing rather than a refund. Because the sentence spread by being COPIED, the check is a SCAN and not four more per-site pins — every document carrying the licence is split into sentences and any sentence pairing overpay/overshoot/overfund with an unqualified "refund" fails, so a fifth copy fails with it. Its pair asserts the TRUE claim present on all three surfaces (the overshoot credits against the tax actually due and comes back only insofar as it passes it), because deleting the sentence outright would satisfy the scan and leave the reader no reason to accept a figure the skill admits is too high; `skill-catalog.md` is included there as the document a contributor rewrites the skill's prose FROM. (2) The provably-safe shortcut in `references/status-change.md` compared each cumulative payment against the WHOLE annual ceiling, which asks January's money in April: with no withholding, a $20,000 ceiling and $5,000 paid each quarter, every instalment that could possibly be owed is covered on every allocation, and the shortcut cleared them only at the last deadline. Sound because the true required annual payment is at most the ceiling, so `credited >= ceiling x n/4 >= required x n/4` at every deadline; the direction is unchanged and pinned as such, a shortfall against an upper bound still being no number anyone owes. A $0 penalty target and a demand for money cannot both be right, and the pair erred by collecting what California cannot penalise not having — the OVER-statement AGENTS.md ranks as the hard error, reached this time by hedging rather than by a wrong number. The reading was verified, not assumed: R&TC §19136.3's whole operative text is that IRC "§6654(d)(1)(B) ... is modified to additionally provide that clause (ii) shall not apply" at $1,000,000 ($500,000 MFS), and clause (ii) is the prior-year component of the REQUIRED ANNUAL PAYMENT, while §19136(c)(2) sits in another section and reads "an addition to tax shall not be imposed under this section if..." — §19136.3 never mentions it. FTB 5805's own General Information keeps the same order: the floor sentence is unconditional ("you are not subject to the underpayment of estimated tax penalty"), and the $1,000,000 sentence is the one carrying the proviso, telling those taxpayers how to figure their penalty "if they are subject to the underpayment of estimated tax penalty". So the limitation decides `required_annual`, which still reads 90% of the current year up there, and the floor decides whether missing it costs anything. Two sibling defects came out with it. The $0-prior-year case was the same rule and was NOT in the finding: `prior_year_floor` carries a `> 0` guard so the $0 belongs to its own note pair, and BELOW the limitation it needed nothing because `prior_year_prong` already zeroes the requirement off a $0 base — which is exactly why the gap was invisible, the one AGI band where the $0 does not travel through the requirement being the one band where it was dropped. And the status CELL, which had been readable while the prong also shrank the requirement, now shows a six-figure required payment, a fraction of it paid, and "covered": the bare-cell-against-a-visible-gap shape `_de_minimis_cell` already carried a reason to avoid, so it gained one, deferring the citation to the note for the reason `_CLIFF_SECOND_EXEMPTION` gives. `prior_year_floor_applied` stays a PROPERTY and is now `prior_year_floor or prior_tax == 0`; `compute_ca_safe_harbor` spells the same two ingredients out again to reach `no_penalty` before the object exists, so a grid test pins the local and the property equal across both floors, both AGI bands, the degraded year and a negative prior tax — nonsense input reaching neither term, which leaves it in the over-stating direction. The skill was hedging in the same place and in the same words, so `references/california.md` states the two-sections argument and carries an explicit "do NOT send this taxpayer to a CA preparer": the handoff is the reflex an agent reaches for at a $1M threshold, and deleting the hedge without a directive in its place leaves the reflex. In the SAME round, the skill's opening promise — "they simply cannot be penalized for having paid it late" — was narrowed to what §6654 actually provides (`test_portability.py::...::test_the_promise_is_the_estimated_tax_penalty_and_not_the_others`): the harbor closes the ESTIMATED-tax penalty and only once step 4's timing check is clean, since §6654(b)(2) runs each instalment from its own due date, and it reaches nothing at all of the §6651(a)(2) failure-to-pay addition and interest that run on a balance still unpaid when the return falls due. Both were already acted on further down the same file, which is the shape that makes an opening dangerous: a reader who takes it at face value stops there. **Round ten found the hole round nine's own fix left, and refuted the statutory mechanism of a second finding while fixing the defect beside it (`test_projection.py::TestTheCaPriorYearFloorIsProvedByTheFigureAlreadyHeld`, `test_portability.py::TestTheAdvisorySkillStopsInventingWhatItDoesNotHold`, `test_irs_dates.py`):** §19136(c)(2)'s prior-year half has THREE states and the engine had notes for two. Under the floor it is proved, at or above it the credits have yet to come off and prove nothing either way — and ABSENT it had nothing at all, so a report with no prior-year CA figure printed a full 540-ES schedule without ever saying that the figure nobody had looked up could zero every row of it. `prior_year_floor_unknown` is deliberately NOT `degraded`: `degraded` means the prior-year PRONG could not be computed and its note offers to restore the prong, so it correctly carries `and not high` — at the §19136.3 limitation the prong is withdrawn by statute rather than missing, and up there the offer would be one the statute refuses. The FLOOR is a different rule in a different section that round nine established survives the limitation intact, so its flag carries no `and not high` and fires at every AGI alike; the finding cited only the high-income band, where the gap is largest because `required_annual` stays at 90% of the current year, but the same silence sat below it, where a first-year business against a large covered year gets the same untested exemption and a five-figure schedule. Gated on the schedule actually asking for something — under the covered-year floor, or with the requirement already paid, the prior-year half is moot and the note would be the gate crying wolf. **The same note's neighbour was advertising a remedy that does not exist:** the degraded note told the user to "supply the prior Form 540 total tax and CA AGI", and `_prior_ca_tax_agi` takes the archive and nothing else — there is no `projection_inputs.json` key for it, so supplying the figures could not change a rerun. Fixed by making the note honest (the archive is the only route, and a prior year computed outside this toolkit cannot supply the CA prong at all) rather than by building the input, which the federal side's own history argues against: that base took an elaborate "completed line-8 composition, never raw line 24" contract to keep a hand-entered figure from arriving net of the wrong things, and a hand-entered CA total arriving net of withholding or of credits already taken would invert the §19136(c)(2) bound in the understating direction. The premise is pinned where it lives — `_prior_ca_tax_agi`'s signature — so an override parameter added later turns the note red instead of leaving it lying the other way. **REFUTED in the same round: that a Chart C tax offset by refundable credits gives §6654(e)(2)'s zero-liability exception.** The finding reached it through §6654(f), and (f) does say "for purposes of this section", which would net the part IV credits and clear the year. But the IRS reads (e)(2) off the return's own line: its estimated-tax penalty guidance says in as many words that "your total tax was zero if the line labeled 'total tax' on Form 1040 ... was zero", and that line sits ABOVE the refundable credits, so a $500 SE tax against a $2,000 earned income credit is a total tax of $500 and no exception. Acting on the statutory reading where the two disagree CLEARS a penalty, which is the understating direction this skill promises cannot happen — so `no-return-year.md` names §6654(f), hands it to a preparer, and does not act on it. **The defect BESIDE the refuted mechanism is real and was fixed:** figure 1 is not "total tax". The Form 2210 line-8 instructions subtract the refundable credits by name — earned income credit, additional child tax credit, refundable AOTC, premium tax credit, the fuels credit, §1341(a)(5)(B) — so the same taxpayer has a figure 1 of $0, and with a filed 12-month prior year 100% of $0 is a required annual payment of $0: a complete answer reached through the ordinary prong with no exception in it. The file said of them "this is not a $0" and routed the whole tax-named Chart C branch to step 6, which is 90% of the current year for someone who owes nothing in — so the branch now splits on whether the return was FILED, the unfiled half keeping step 6 because §6654(d)(1)(B)'s closing text withdraws the prong there whatever figure 1 would have been. The categorical "where the line names a tax, total tax cannot be zero" is scoped as well: it is true of the taxes that land on the total-tax line and false of AMT and an excess advance-PTC repayment, which are added above the nonrefundable-credit subtraction and can be offset to nothing. **Step 0's third scope gate was refusing an answer the engine produces:** it blocked the `/tax-withholding` handoff for a short or unfiled prior year, and step 6 then said the remaining 90% prong is not computable here while step 7 said to run the full toolkit — from inside the full toolkit. What the gate actually guards against is a POSITIVE prior-year base accepted as given with nothing asking whether the prong was available, so the fix is to hand off with `prior_year_form_2210_tax_base` CONFIRMED null, which is the tools' documented 90%-of-current degraded mode. California is the real exception and keeps the refusal, its prong coming from `archive/{year-1}/result.json` rather than that field, with no availability check and nothing to null. Asserted against the ENGINE and not only the prose, a skill that says "hand off, the tools degrade" being right only while they do. **AGENTS.md §2 stopped enumerating and started pointing:** its five-figure sentence read as the whole grant while the `allowed` registry in `tests/test_portability.py` had already outgrown it — §19136(c)(2)'s $500/$250 and §6017's $400 are both there, pinned to engine constants, and neither was in §2 — so a reader auditing the skill against §2 would flag two compliant figures and miss the point. Copying the list into §2 is not available: AGENTS.md sits inside a 28 KiB cap enforced by test, and the replacement had to come out BYTE-NEGATIVE. The deferral is itself pinned, a pointer nothing checks rotting back into the enumeration it replaced. **And `next_business_day_state` stopped degrading an unrecognised code:** it accepted any non-empty string and sent everything outside the conforming set down the weekend-only branch, so "California", "CA1" or "ZZ" published TY2023 April 17 where FTB publishes April 18 — and the passed-deadline gate reads these dates, reporting a 540-ES row missed on a day it is still open. Every live caller passes a literal "CA" or an already-validated code, which is the reason the boundary is where to say it: nothing downstream would notice a new caller that did not. The USPS set is imported from `capabilities` rather than re-listed, the odd-looking import direction being the cheaper half of that trade. **A sweep for siblings of round ten then found the same substitution twice more, and refuted it four times (`test_retirement_dashboard.py::test_a_two_letter_typo_for_a_non_conforming_state_is_refused`, `test_projection.py::TestFloridaStateSection::test_a_shape_valid_code_that_names_no_state_is_malformed_too`):** the class is *checking the SHAPE of a value whose only use is MEMBERSHIP of a small set*. `retirement_dashboard` reads `address.state` for exactly one purpose — CA/NJ for the HSA, PA/NJ/MA for the IRA and 401(k) rates — and its guard rejected "California" while accepting every two-letter value, with a comment directly above naming the harm it was failing to prevent ("would silently report CA/NJ HSA contributions as state-deductible — overstating state savings"). Replayed: `"CN"` for CA and `"NU"` for NJ both flip the HSA row from `hsa_non_conforming` to `hsa_conforming`, inventing a state deduction at the taxpayer's own marginal rate, silently — the over-statement AGENTS.md ranks as the hard error, and a two-letter typo is at least as likely as a full name. `projection._normalize_state_code` was the second: both it and `loss_harvest` refuse a bad cache code with "not a valid 2-letter USPS code" while the predicate underneath tested the shape alone, so `"ZZ"` was reported as valid by the very sentence promising otherwise. Tightened rather than traded — an unreal CACHE code becomes the loud malformed-code note instead of a code trusted for a comparison, and an unreal RESIDENT code becomes the already-modeled "resident unknown" degrade — and it DEGRADES rather than raising as `capabilities` does on the same field, state modelling there being optional with a labelled fall-through already. **REFUTED, each by replaying the production path rather than reading the branch.** (1) `tax_calendar._strict_state_list` validates `k1_states` against `^[A-Z]{2}$` alone while its sibling `_strict_state_str` checks the USPS set, and the asymmetry looked exactly like the defect above — but `build_events` emits `_ptet_not_modeled_note(tax_year, k1_state)` whenever `events_ptet` returns nothing, so `k1_states: ["CN"]` produces a visible "PTET reminders not modeled for state CN" naming the typo. Not silent, so not the same defect; the sibling's hard error is justified by ITS silence, not by the shape check. Caught only by running `build_events` — calling `events_ptet` directly bypasses the note and reads like a silent drop. (2) `ptet._normalize_state` is shape-only for the same reason: an unmodeled or typo'd code returns a `StatePtetResult` carrying "{state} has no PTET regime modeled in v1 (CA/NY/NJ only)". (3) `capabilities._validated_state` already checks membership. (4) The whole class of REMEDIES round ten fixed in `_ca_notes` — every "supply X" / "run Y" string in `agents/lib/` was swept and each named field, flag and schema path exists: `forecast_overrides.schedule_1a_se_tips`, `--ira-basis`/`--ira-value`/`prior_year.ira_basis`, `hsa_coverage`, `account` on a buy txn, and `federal.retirement_401k.secure_2_0_high_earner_threshold` (present in `create_empty_tax_law`). The CA baseline was the only one pointing at nothing. **Round eleven — one finding, and it held.** `references/status-change.md` took the 100%/110% factor per RETURN for a couple who filed separately last year and jointly this: two $100,000 AGIs each drew 100% where the couple's own $200,000 draws 110%, short by a tenth of the whole prior-year tax, the one direction this skill promises cannot happen. The finding's own GROUND was checked as well as its verdict — Codex said Form 2210 "combines both spouses' prior-year AGI and tax before selecting one factor", and Form 2210 says no such thing: it tests "the AGI on your 2024 return" singular, then separately says to add the two spouses' taxes "as figured earlier", and neither the instructions nor Pub 505 nor IRM 20.1.3 says which reading wins. The authority is Reg. §1.6654-2(e)(3)(i) plus the statute's single clause (ii), not the booklet, and the branch now says so rather than trading one citation of an ORDERING for another. Corroboration came from the engine, which had been quietly on the other side of this the whole time: `_prior_tax_agi` takes ONE `prior_year_form_2210_tax_base` and ONE `prior_year_form_2210_agi`, so a factor per return was not merely wrong but INEXPRESSIBLE in `projection_inputs.json` — the same engine-versus-skill disagreement that helper's own docstring records for the $0 base. No engine change was needed; both SKILL.md copies moved with the branch file (step 1's precondition bullet, step 2's threshold paragraph), line-neutral, that file being at its 500-line cap. **Round twelve — two findings, both held, and the first was ten sites wide.** §6654(e)(2) has three conditions: a 12-month preceding year, NO LIABILITY for it, and US citizenship or residence throughout. Ten places stated the first and third, called that the whole test, and reached the subject only when the Form 2210 line-8 base came out at $0 — so the BASE was silently standing in for the liability. It cannot: line 8 SUBTRACTS the refundable credits by name and Form 1040's total-tax line sits above them, so a prior year with $500 of SE tax against a $2,000 EIC has a $0 base and a $500 total tax. Where a return was FILED that costs nothing — the ordinary prong pays 100% of $0 — but §6654(d)(1)(B)'s closing text withdraws that prong on an unfiled or short prior year, and there the mis-set flag is the only thing zeroing a required annual payment the taxpayer owes in full. Codex named one site (the template comment) and the harm; the sweep found the other nine: the engine's $0-base refusal, both `compute_form_2210` $0 branches — one of them the taxpayer-facing `no_penalty_reason`, which also now says the engine did not VERIFY the confirmation, having no line 24 to verify it against — /tax-compute's intake contract, /tax-project, /tax-withholding, tax-safe-harbor's own precondition bullet, and at the end of the chain the sentence in `skill-catalog.md` equating a netted $0 with the exception outright, which is where the other nine came from. Documentation was the whole available lever (nothing here can see a line 24) and the flag was already NAMED for the liability, so no fourth flag was added: every site now asks for what the flag is named after. `test_every_statement_of_6654e2_names_the_no_liability_condition` sweeps all ten, windowed on the CITATION rather than the whole document, and CASE-SENSITIVELY — the first draft matched case-insensitively and passed all ten UNCHANGED, because every one of them already contained "no liability" somewhere as narration; what none contained was the condition stated as a thing to confirm. `test_the_prior_zero_exception_states_all_three_of_its_conditions` was the same failure inside the suite: named for three, asserting two, green throughout. The second finding was tax-safe-harbor step 0 contradicting itself in five lines — the opening demanded an explicit YES to "was a return filed?" before ANY handoff, while the paragraph three below it (round ten's own fix) said to hand off on a NO or unknown with the base left null. Which branch an agent obeys depends on how far it reads, so the same workspace got an answer or a refusal unpredictably; only the first two questions gate the handoff now, and the test pins BOTH halves so a later fix cannot resolve the contradiction by deleting the handoff instead of the gate. **Round thirteen** took the same shape one layer out: a caveat that was RIGHT, keyed on a table that cannot answer the question it was asked. `_CLOSED_VOUCHER_NOTE` calls a closed year's penalty FIXED and says no payment reduces it by a cent — the strongest claim either projection report makes — and hedged it only for years in `_RETURN_DUE_POSTPONED`, which holds the two IRS-WIDE COVID postponements and nothing else. §7508A relief is overwhelmingly the county-scoped kind, dozens of notices a year, postponing estimated-tax instalments along with the return, and §7508A(a)(2) disregards the postponed period in computing an addition to the tax, which is what §6654(a) adds; nothing reaching that function says whether the taxpayer was inside one. So for every year but two the lookup read its own silence as "not postponed" when it established only "not in this table", and a taxpayer who waits on that verdict goes on accruing what they were told was settled. The table now picks the LEAD and never whether to warn, and the caveat moved to sit directly after the sentence it qualifies rather than at the end of a note that long — `test_the_fixed_penalty_verdict_is_qualified_on_every_closed_year` pins the placement by CHARACTER OFFSET, a reader who stops at "FIXED ... by a cent" being the one the finding is about. No disaster-coverage input was added: the only person who can answer is the one the caveat sends to the notice, and a flag reading "no disaster" while unanswered deletes the hedge for everyone who never saw the question. The sweep found the same categorical verdict in two more places the round that widened it had missed — step 0's window table and `references/withholding-cure.md`, both read on their own — and `test_every_categorical_fixed_penalty_claim_reaches_the_caveat` windows SYMMETRICALLY, a forward-only window having failed the one site that was already correct, because step 4 states its claim AFTER the caveat qualifying it. Round fourteen closed the same class one step earlier, at step 0's WORKSPACE HANDOFF: its third scope question (a full 12-month prior year for which a return was actually filed) is §6654(d)(1)(B)'s closing text, which withdraws the PRONG and nothing else, but the instruction it drove was written about the FIELD — "hand off with `prior_year_form_2210_tax_base` confirmed null, never populate it for a withdrawn prong". Both routes to a $0 requirement reach the engine through that one field, the prong through a positive figure and §6654(e)(2) through a $0 beside `prior_year_zero_liability_confirmed`, and `_prior_tax_agi` consults that flag ONLY at a $0 base — so a null base discards the confirmation silently and the handoff bills 90% of the current year to a taxpayer no penalty can attach to. Step 0 now works the first precondition, and `references/no-return-year.md` with it, before the handoff and TRANSMITS its answer; the categorical sentence carries its own "unless §6654(e)(2) is still open" rather than being corrected further down, because an agent reads an instruction as an instruction — the lesson the sibling test in this same class already records. Three ordering tests here anchored on `text.find("§6654(e)(2)")`, the FIRST occurrence in the file; step 0 now cites the section when it routes, so they anchor on step 1's precondition bullet instead. The proxy became wrong, the invariant did not. Round fifteen found the same defect one sentence further down that note. The caveat qualified "the penalty is FIXED"; "this schedule is obsolete" and "Do NOT send a voucher ... its instalments belong to dates that are all behind us" stayed flat, and they are false for exactly the taxpayer the caveat was written for — for a reason that is NOT the caveat's open question, which is why hedging one did not hedge the other. Whether §7508A(a)(2) reaches §6654(b)(2)'s accrual endpoint is unresolved; whether §7508A moves the INSTALMENT is not, since §7508A(a)(1) reaches §7508(a)(1)(B), "payment of any income ... tax or any installment thereof". A covered instalment is payable on its postponed date and paying it then is TIMELY, so the note talked its reader out of the one action still available. The condition now LEADS the instruction rather than trailing it, the header says "obsolete on the STATUTORY calendar" because the header is what a summarising agent lifts whole, and the test asserts the ORDER as well as the words. It also pins the SCOPING, which is deliberate: the passed-voucher and both balance branches name dates no LATER than a postponed one, so their claims over-state urgency, and a disaster caveat on advice that is already safe is the cry-wolf shape this file is otherwise about. **Round sixteen** put a fourth case in the same class, and the same shape as rounds thirteen to fifteen: a rule stated once, and not at the place an agent acts on. Step 6 refuses a fiscal year and a §6072(c) 1040-NR filer outright — §6654(k)(1) substitutes the corresponding months of a non-January year, §6654(j) gives three instalments with 50% at the first — but the check sat at step 1, and step 0 hands a workspace user off before step 1 runs. Since `ES_DUE_DATES` is fixed and `/tax-withholding` has no input that could move it, the two filers the skill promises to refuse were the two who reached the engine first. Asserted on the gating SET rather than on the words, on both statutes, on the direction (four equal instalments UNDERSTATE the June one the statute weights double), and on `ES_DUE_DATES` itself, since the gate is only worth its words while that calendar is fixed. The §6072(c) narrowing is pinned beside it: an NR filer whose wages ARE US-withheld is on the ordinary quarters and computable here, so closing the gap may not ship an over-refusal in its place. **Round seventeen** is the fifth, and the first where the OTHER copy was already right: round fifteen split the engine's closed-schedule note — the fixed-penalty verdict stays hedged, a postponed instalment does not need hedging because §7508A(a)(1) reaches §7508(a)(1)(B), "payment of ... any installment thereof", so it is still OPEN and paying it by the postponed date is timely — and the skill kept saying "do not send a 1040-ES" flat two sentences after naming the same notice. Pinned three ways, because one assertion would have let the flat form back: the statute and the two facts it settles must appear in SKILL.md; the condition must LEAD the imperative by character index, the engine's own lesson that an agent reads an instruction and stops; and the engine's caveat is asserted to still carry all three fragments, so resolving or rewording the instalment half there cannot leave the skill stating it alone. The categorical pin in `TestTheAdvisorySkillIsAnchoredToOneCoveredYear` was updated rather than deleted — it now pins condition-with-imperative, since a test asserting the flat words is a test that restores them. **Round eighteen** is the sixth, and the first where the sweep itself was the limit: rounds fifteen and sixteen bound prior-year AGI to the 2020-and-later Form 1040 in all four documents that name it, and `projection_inputs_template.json` — the template of the very file two of those documents write into, and the headless input path under AGENTS.md §6 — went on saying "Form 1040 line 11 AGI" flat. The sweep could not have reached it: it was spelled `SKILLS_DIR / skill / rel` and structurally could not name a path outside `.agents/skills/`. `AGI_SITES` now carries repository-relative paths and the template is a fifth entry, so the next copy is caught by the same loop rather than by a second test written to agree with the first.
  - **A de-minimis floor must reach the voucher SCHEDULE, not just the harbor's field (`test_projection.py`, `test_withholding.py`):** `compute_ca_safe_harbor` zeroed `CaSafeHarbor.shortfall` under R&TC §19136 so the DE 4 figure would not demand payments that avoid nothing, but `compute_ca_estimated_vouchers` took `required_annual` and recomputed a shortfall of its own — so one CA block printed the de-minimis status line, a $200.25 January instalment and a note directing an immediate catch-up payment, together. The federal side modeled no floor AT ALL, while `compute_form_2210` returned `no_penalty` on the same figures one import away: two engine modules, one statute, opposite answers. Both harbors carry `de_minimis`, and both voucher builders now take the harbor's `shortfall` rather than a requirement they re-derive a gap from — so the suppression propagates by construction and the catch-up note's existing all-zero guard silences it with no second condition. The flag that forwarded it was the first fix and left the recomputation standing with an override on top; what can still break is a CALLER passing something other than the harbor's own figure, which `test_the_report_hands_the_voucher_schedule_the_harbors_own_shortfall` pins on the report, with a counterweight off the floor so it cannot pass against a builder that always returns zeros. Seven tests hold it: that the report hands each schedule the harbor's own figure rather than re-deriving one; the federal harbor's flag and its parity with `compute_form_2210`; the zeroed schedule and note with a **one-dollar-above counterweight**, since `$1,000` is `<` and not `<=` and the boundary is where an off-by-one hides; that the floor reads WITHHOLDING only (§6654(e)(1) reduces the tax shown by tax withheld — an identical balance covered by ESTIMATES is not excused, and the two are interchangeable everywhere else in the harbor, which is what makes it a trap); the CA end-to-end contradiction reproduced through `build_report`; and that the ZERO-BALANCE column is untouched in both, because the floor excuses the penalty and not the tax — silencing that would be the under-stating direction. The status cell states the statute rather than a bare "covered", which against a visible balance owed reads as a bug in the report — and states the MARGIN with it (`test_the_de_minimis_status_states_how_far_under_the_floor_it_is`), because the floor is a cliff evaluated against an ANNUALIZED projection and "no penalty applies" is only as good as the projection. That is carried as the continuous distance rather than a "near the cliff" flag: any threshold for near would be invented, and the repo's own rule is to model the continuous version wherever a statute has a discontinuity. The test pins $1 of headroom, the whole $1,000 of it, the exactly-at-the-floor lapse (`<`, not `<=`) and the CA twin, which shipped its floor long before the federal one and went the whole time without a margin. **The cell may only claim a penalty attaches past the cliff when one does** (`test_the_de_minimis_cliff_is_only_claimed_when_something_attaches`): it said so unconditionally, which is false wherever a prong already covers the year — $5,000 of tax against $4,500 withheld and a $4,000 prior-year prong reads "$500 more tax and it attaches", and at $5,500 the prong still binds against $4,500 paid. `de_minimis_cliff_more_tax` and `de_minimis_cliff_less_withholding` decide it, one field per path; the test pins the covered case silent on both, the same figures with no prior year loud at $450 and $500, and the report agreeing with itself one step past the cliff. **And only the report whose inputs can TAKE a path may name it** (`test_each_cliff_path_is_named_only_by_the_report_that_can_take_it`): the two were a single worse-of-the-two field, and /tax-withholding's renderer attributes every positive to withholding, so $500 of computed tax against $0 withheld — where the whole $900 comes from the tax path — announced "$500 less withholding and it attaches" about a figure that cannot fall. That case has no lapse at all (`withholding < de_minimis_margin` is the same statement as a tax under the floor), so it also cannot take the generic "nothing attaches" tail, whose REASON — the requirement being met on its own — is false there; the test pins both the field values and the two renderers' sentences.

  - **The current-year tax is a line-24 total where §6654 measures Form 2210 line 4 (`test_the_current_year_tax_is_disclosed_as_unnetted_wherever_it_asks_for_money`, `test_the_unnetted_current_year_note_reaches_this_renderer_too`):** the current-year figure drives BOTH prongs and the $1,000 floor. `compute_form_2210` states the contract on its own argument; neither caller of `compute_safe_harbor` can meet it (`project_federal_tax` models the CTC as nonrefundable only and no EIC/ACTC; result.json has a field for neither), and the floor's other side is short the §31(b) excess-social-security credit as well. Both gaps run one way, so $5,000 of tax with $1,000 of refundable AOTC and $3,500 withheld — a true $500 balance §6654(e)(1) excuses — is reported as $1,500 to pay. `current_tax_unnetted` fires only where a shortfall is actually asserted: netting cannot take a run OUT of the floor, and a covered year has nothing to over-state, so the tests pin the asking run loud and both the under-floor and the covered-by-estimates runs silent, in both renderers, in the markdown and in `--json`. `test_the_prior_zero_exception_states_all_three_of_its_conditions` separately pins `compute_form_2210`'s §6654(e)(2) result to all three conditions.

  - **Nothing outstanding is not nothing was late (`test_a_year_covered_by_an_undated_estimate_still_gets_the_timing_caveat`):** the all-zero guard below silences the catch-up note, correctly — but it silenced everything, and a $20,000 requirement met by one undated $20,000 estimate has April, June and September behind it with penalty already accrued. The gate now returns the jurisdiction's timing note instead of None, gated on `estimates_paid > 0` and OUT under a de minimis; the test pins the estimate-paid case loud, the withholding-paid counterweight silent (§6654(g)(1) and FTB 5805 Worksheet II line 2 both spread withholding across instalments), the de-minimis case silent, and the CA reader getting CA's weights rather than four equal federal ones. `test_the_timing_caveat_never_claims_a_funded_schedule_is_settled` then pins the two ways that note over-stated: its lede claimed the ANNUAL requirement was met in every case the gate reached, including a schedule still printing instalments ($20,000 required, $15,000 in by July 1, two $2,500 vouchers directly below the sentence), so there are two ledes over one shared body per jurisdiction and a 72-case sweep asserts the settled one only ever appears above an all-zero schedule; and it fired before the first deadline had passed, where every payment made is timely for every instalment and there is no late one to warn about. Silence where a penalty really is one projection-miss away is the under-stating half and is pinned too. Suppressing `shortfall` in place is what destroyed the input that decides this, so the pre-suppression gap survives as `shortfall_before_de_minimis` and is pinned to agree with `shortfall` off the floor — otherwise it is a second quantity rather than the same one. `test_both_status_cells_quote_the_floor_their_arithmetic_used` closes the last drift: the CA cell hardcoded "$500/$250-MFS" where the federal interpolated its constant, so an R&TC amendment would have moved every computed figure and left the sentence quoting the old floor with every margin assertion still green. Both cells are one shared `_de_minimis_cell` now, differing by a `_DeMinimisFloor` built FROM the constants — and by `annualized`, which is the one thing the two reports must NOT share: /tax-project annualizes the tax, /tax-withholding reads it from result.json and annualizes only the withholding pace, so the shared sentence would otherwise point that reader at the one input in the report that cannot move.
  - **A referral inherits the referred tool's calendar, and `as_of_date` cannot reach January 15 (`test_portability.py::test_the_engine_cannot_be_told_that_january_15_has_passed`):** when valid stored figures are dated 12-31, that is the largest date the CLIs can express inside the covered year, so no value reaches the fourth instalment's due date in the following January. Both schedules can therefore retain that row however late the real date is. A January 20 inquiry needs the real calendar applied separately; once statutory April 15 arrives, §6654(b)(2) has fixed the penalty and no 1040-ES payment reduces it. The skill's handoff reads the row against figure 6 and names §6654(b)(2), April 15, the §6654(h) January 31 escape and "pay NOW". The two special-case gates run before this handoff: `/tax-withholding` has no filing-status-change or farmer/fisher input, so either fact (or an unknown answer) stays in the advisory procedure instead of delegating to ordinary one-return/four-installment logic.
  - **"Last year's filed return" is the wrong return once it has been amended (`test_portability.py::test_figure_1_asks_which_return_where_the_prior_year_was_amended`):** IRM 20.1.3.3.1.1 — the preceding year's tax is the tax shown on the ORIGINAL return, or on an amended one only where the amendment was filed before that year's return was due, extensions included. The ordinary 1040-X is later than that and does not replace the figure, and neither does an IRS adjustment: §6654(d)(1)(B)(ii) reads the tax SHOWN ON THE RETURN. Asked without the question, an amendment that lowered the tax lowers figure 1 and the guaranteed payment with it — a payment too low, the one failure the skill's licence rules out — so the test pins the question, the answer, the form's name, the citation, the extensions half of the cut-off and the DIRECTION, plus figure 2 sending AGI to the same return.
  - **The §6654(e)(1) flag is a subtraction, and a subtraction can come out negative (`test_portability.py::test_neither_skill_reads_the_de_minimis_flag_as_a_balance`):** both consuming skills read `de_minimis` as "penalty excused, tax still owed at filing" in every case, and the flag is set just as readily where withholding covers the whole liability — $5,000 of tax against $6,000 withheld is a refund reported as $X due in April, with the audit line persisting that into `answers.md`. The flag says nothing about the balance; `federal.balance` and `zero_target` do, and the test pins each skill naming its own and neither carrying the old wording.
  - **The catch-up notes are decided in `build_report`, so `--json` carries them (`test_projection.py::test_the_catchup_notes_reach_the_json_payload_too`, `test_withholding.py`):** both notes were computed inside `render_markdown`, and both consuming skills read `--json` FIRST — `/tax-project` step 5 takes the payload and step 6 re-runs for the report — so an agent summarising the payload handed over the under-funded earliest voucher with nothing marking it. They are now fields on the report, like every other renderer-only warning there (`nonresident_income_warning`, `state_unsupported_note`), and the tests assert the payload value IS the rendered string rather than merely truthy, which is the drift a second computation would reintroduce.
  - **A field added to `--json` that no skill reads reaches no reader (`test_portability.py::TestTheSkillsReadWhatTheEngineEmits`):** the catch-up notes were moved into `build_report` precisely because both consuming skills summarise the payload before they render — and the skill half of that change was never written. `de_minimis` then made `safe_harbor.covered` true with every voucher at $0.00 against a real balance owed, while `/tax-project` step 7 and `/tax-withholding` step 5 still templated the persisted audit line as `Safe-harbor: {covered/underpaid}`: a $5,000/$4,001 projection reported "covered" to a taxpayer owing $999 in April. Five tests hold both directions — that each skill names every disclosure field (`de_minimis`, `de_minimis_margin`, `de_minimis_cliff_more_tax`, `de_minimis_cliff_less_withholding`, `degraded`, `current_tax_unnetted`) and both catch-up notes, that neither audit line is a bare two-way verdict any more, and that every field the skills instruct a read of is still a `dataclasses.field` of the harbor or the report, since `vars()` builds the payload and a rename would simply make the key vanish. The resolution half named TWO skills and checked ONE engine: `voucher_catchup_note` is a field of `withholding.TrueUpReport`/`CaTrueUp` as well, so /tax-withholding's half was free to be renamed — or dropped from `_report_to_dict` — with both tests green and the skill still naming a key nothing emits. `test_the_withholding_half_of_the_payload_is_resolved_too` resolves it there. The same class now pins both skills to the as-of provenance versus real action-date contract. The converse case — a payload field no RENDERER reads — is `TrueUpReport.has_prior_archive` (`test_withholding.py::test_has_prior_archive_is_a_documented_payload_contract`): its twin on `ProjectionReport` gates the "`recurring_from_prior` defaulted to $0" warning, but this report annualizes no income, so there is nothing here for that warning to be about, and the header clarifier one might expect it to drive correctly gates on the stronger `ca_prior_resolved` instead. Described as the projection field's "twin" it read as a renderer branch someone had forgotten to write, and as spare weight a tidy-up could delete along with the JSON contract the skill docs implied. Both skills now document it — what it means, that it is NOT `degraded`, and that archive PRESENCE is not a computed CA prior-year harbor — and the test pins the field in the payload and those two distinctions in the prose.
  - **Raw Form 1040 line 24 is not the Form 2210 line-8 pre-factor base (`test_portability.py::TestTheAdvisorySkillCorrectsTheEngineItHandsOffTo`, `test_projection.py::test_the_prior_year_prong_uses_only_a_completed_line8_base`, `test_withholding.py::test_the_federal_prior_prong_requires_the_completed_line8_base`):** besides the refundable credits §6654(f) nets below it, line 24 includes Schedule 2 taxes that Form 2210 line 8 admits or excludes under its own enumerated rules — including the conditional household-employment-tax exclusion and possible §965 exclusion. Blindly subtracting credits therefore does not reconstruct the base. `_prior_tax_agi` now reads NO archive at all — it takes both figures from the planning input, the tax base from `prior_year_form_2210_tax_base` and the paired AGI from `prior_year_form_2210_agi`, and its archive parameter is gone (pinned on the SIGNATURE, since "passing an archive changes nothing" is no longer expressible). Both are read at the SAME parse boundary, which is the half that shipped wrong: the tax base accepted `"18400"` while the AGI went through the ARCHIVE sanitizer and dropped any string to `None`, so a hand-typed `"162000"` never selected the 110% factor, the required annual payment came out 10% low — the understating direction — and the report announced "Prior-year AGI unavailable" about a figure sitting in the file. `test_prior_tax_agi_reads_the_paired_agi_at_the_same_parse_boundary` pins every hand-typed form through to `compute_safe_harbor`'s factor, and pins the split that ABSENT still degrades to the flagged 100% path while PRESENT-but-unreadable raises naming the field: disbelieving a supplied answer without saying so is the failure the prong exists to prevent. When that completed composition is absent, both reports leave the prior prong unresolved and use loud 90%-current degraded mode. The federal raw-line-24 note was removed because it described behavior the engine no longer has; the CA twin stays narrower and live because an unverified CA credit list would be worse than an explicit gap.
  - **Today's date is the right `as_of` for the current year and no other (`test_projection.py::test_a_closed_year_defaults_its_as_of_to_december_31`, `test_cli_defaults_a_closed_year_to_december_31`, `test_withholding.py::test_main_defaults_a_closed_year_to_december_31`):** both CLIs defaulted `as_of` to `today.strftime("%m-%d")` parsed into `--year`, and the only guard beside it asks whether `as_of > today` — which cannot fire for a year that has closed, 2025-01-05 being no part of the future of 2026. `--year 2025` run on January 5 therefore annualized a FINISHED year from 5/365, multiplying its withholding by about 73 and printing the resulting shortfall as a payment to make. `default_as_of` returns 12-31 for a closed year and today's MM-DD for the open one; both branches are pinned, the open-year one as the counterweight that stops the fix freezing the current year at year-end, and the elapsed fraction is asserted at 1.0 against the old 0.0137 so the test states the size of the error rather than only its existence. Pinned on BOTH CLIs, because sharing the helper is what makes them one fix and a test on one alone stays green when the other's call is deleted. Every stored `as_of_date` is dropped from the fixture copy first — with one present the default chain never runs, so a fixture carrying it cannot see this bug at all.
  - **A valid stored `as_of_date` is provenance, not an expendable stamp (`test_projection.py::test_resolve_as_of_preserves_a_valid_stored_date_on_a_closed_year`, `test_cli_preserves_a_stored_as_of_on_a_closed_year`, `test_withholding.py::test_main_preserves_a_stored_as_of_on_a_closed_year`):** the date is attached to the YTD actuals beside it. Replacing a stored June 30 with 12-31 after year-end relabels six months of wages and withholding as a full year and changes every actionable projection without changing its source figures. `resolve_as_of` therefore gives an explicit override first place, preserves every valid stored date in open and closed years, and uses the closed-year 12-31 default only when the stored value is null/missing. Every other present value is validated: falsy malformed values no longer bypass parsing, a malformed closed-year value is omitted rather than interpolated (closing the sensitive-value emission path), and both CLIs carry the redacted warning in Markdown and JSON. Annualization provenance is separate from action availability: the real date sets remaining covered-year paychecks, so a valid stored June snapshot reopened after year-end keeps its projection while every W-4/DE 4 figure is zero. The tests pin both clocks, the null default, falsy invalid metadata, and both output formats of the malformed branch. The open-year refusal is also pinned to be ACTIONABLE (`test_the_open_year_as_of_refusal_names_its_field_and_its_remedy`): validating every present value is a deliberate break with workspaces predating it — the old `args.as_of or stored or today` chain skipped `""`/`0`/`false` in silence and stamped today's date onto figures entered as of some other one — so the refusal names the file, the key and all three ways out (a real MM-DD, `null`, `--as-of`) without ever echoing the value, and `projection_inputs_template.json` carries the same rule in a `_comment_as_of_date` beside the field. A break announced only in the source reads as a bug in the tool.
  - **The wrong-bracket fallback is at the projection boundary, not only in the harbor's prior-year branch (`test_projection.py::test_the_federal_projection_value_checks_its_filing_status_too`):** `compute_safe_harbor` value-checks `filing_status` inside its `else:` — the prong that needs the $150,000/$75,000 window — so a first-year workspace, which passes `prior_tax=None` and takes the degraded path, never reaches it. Everything the harbor is then handed was computed from the same unchecked string: `_full_status_key` resolves anything non-canonical to "single" through a `.get(fs, "single")` default, single brackets always exist, and no error is raised, so "Married Filing Jointly" is silently taxed on single brackets and the single standard deduction. `project_federal_tax` and `build_report` both refuse now — the first because it is where the key is USED and is called directly, the second because its `fs` reaches the state projection and the harbor before the federal call would have raised. The test also asserts the two statuses compute DIFFERENT tax on the fixture inputs, without which it could pass while showing nothing.
  - **The catch-up note and the timing note are mutually exclusive, so the catch-up one carries both jobs — for the readers it is true of (`test_projection.py::test_the_catchup_note_names_undated_estimates_only_where_there_are_some`):** `_voucher_catchup_note` returns the catch-up text as soon as something is outstanding, which makes the `estimates_paid > 0` timing branch below it unreachable in exactly the case where a payment is already late — the reader who most needs "these estimates had no dates on them" is the one who cannot be shown it. The federal note sent them back to the cumulative test without saying so while its CA twin said "with your payment DATES", so both gained the clause. Both then said it to everyone: this gate fires on WITHHOLDING alone — with `estimates_paid` at 0 the cumulative test reduces to (requirement − withholding) × weight — so a taxpayer who had made no estimated payment at all was told "the estimates behind this schedule arrived as an undated TOTAL" and sent to re-run a dated test with nothing to date. The clause is now a separate `_CatchupNote` field appended only where estimates were paid, which puts the condition in the code that knows the answer rather than in the prose. Both directions are asserted, on both jurisdictions, THROUGH the gate rather than against the constants — a clause spliced back into the base text passes any substring check on `_ES_VOUCHER_CATCHUP_NOTE` while still reaching the zero-estimates reader — plus that the ungated note still carries its own step pointer, and that it is a strict PREFIX of the gated one, so the shared half cannot be fixed for one reader and not the other.
  - **A note bound to one jurisdiction's weights must refuse the other's harbor (`test_projection.py::test_a_catchup_note_refuses_the_other_jurisdictions_safe_harbor`):** `_CatchupNote` binds the instalment fraction to the text that explains it, which is what stops the CA schedule being described in four equal federal instalments — but `harbor` was left unbound, and the gate is duck-typed over the four fields the two harbors share, so `_voucher_catchup_note(_FEDERAL_CATCHUP, ca_vouchers, ca_safe_harbor, as_of)` type-checked and ran 25/50/75/100 against CA figures. FTB 5805 Worksheet II weights BOTH the requirement and the withholding at 30/40/0/30, so a $5,000 CA requirement met by $4,189 of CA withholding owes nothing at April 15 and the federal weights called it $452.75 short — the OVER-warning direction, and the exact error `_ca_instalment_fraction`'s docstring exists to record. Both harbors now carry a `jurisdiction` label the gate checks with `getattr`, so a REAL harbor cannot be mispaired while a test STUB that declares none still runs: an isinstance check would have bought the guarantee by closing the seam that makes the gate testable. Both mispairings are pinned, in both directions, alongside the stub.
  - **`estimates_paid` is validated before the covered-year early return, and named for the file (`test_projection.py::test_estimates_paid_is_validated_before_the_covered_year_early_return`):** both schedules validated it AFTER `if shortfall <= 0`, so the check ran only on a year that happened to be under-withheld — `compute_estimated_vouchers(0.0, as_of, -5000)` returned a clean all-zero schedule with nothing said. And the message named `estimates_paid`, a parameter of the function that appears nowhere in `projection_inputs.json`, which is the complaint `annualize`'s own comment records against its pre-fix state. The check moved above the return in both schedules and now names `estimated_payments_made.federal` / `.state`; `_early_estimate_vouchers`'s third application of the same check is gone, both callers passing the already-coerced value. The test pins the covered-year case, every non-finite and wrong-typed form, the per-jurisdiction field name, and that a valid figure still yields the zero schedule.
  - **A voucher table's share column is derived from its own amounts (`test_projection.py::test_ca_voucher_share_column_matches_the_amounts_beside_it`):** the column renormalized the statutory 30/40/0/30 weights over the still-open instalments, which agreed with the amounts on the `_split_remainder_last` path and nowhere else. Before the first deadline `compute_ca_estimated_vouchers` allocates against the cumulative 30/70/70/100 checkpoints NET of estimates already paid, so a $700 shortfall with $300 already in prints $0 / $400 / $0 / $300 — 0/57/0/43 — under a weight column reading 30/40/0/30, with "30%" beside $0.00 at April 15 and "40%" beside 57% of the shortfall. The share is now `amount / total`, which cannot drift from the column it sits next to by construction and reproduces the renormalized weight on the path where that was right. The test pins the amounts, the derived shares, AND that the old renormalization still disagrees on this input — without that last assertion it would quietly stop testing anything. The HEADING was the third column of the same contradiction and is now prose that can qualify itself (`test_build_report_ca_block_present_for_ca_state` pins that it no longer reads "(30% / 40% / 0% / 30%)"), and the note explaining a non-statutory split is gated on the rendered shares rather than on `len(r.ca_vouchers) < len(CA_ES_SCHEDULE)` — that gate sees only the closed-instalment cause and was blind to the early-estimate one (`test_the_ca_table_explains_an_early_estimate_split_the_old_gate_missed`). `_percent_shares` apportions by largest remainder so the column sums to 100% instead of the 101% independent rounding produced (`test_the_ca_share_column_sums_to_one_hundred_percent`). The heading, weighting sentence and all three explanation bodies are ONE copy (`_CA_ES_HEADING` / `_CA_ES_WEIGHTING` / `_ca_split_note`) shared with `withholding.render_markdown`: fixing only projection.py left withholding hardcoding the contradicting heading with no Share column to qualify it, and the two modules' suites asserting opposite contracts for the same string. Each cause selects its own sentence and never another's — the estimates-already-paid body was printed unconditionally, telling a taxpayer who had paid none that their payments caused the split (`test_the_ca_share_note_does_not_blame_estimates_that_were_never_paid`, whose first version asserted only the arithmetic premises and stayed green with the gate removed).
  - **The CA $500 floor reads on EITHER year, and the engine can only test one (`test_projection.py::test_the_ca_notes_hand_over_the_floors_prior_year_half`):** R&TC §19136(c)(2) imposes no CA underpayment penalty when the PRECEDING year's CA tax less credits, **or** the covered year's, is under $500 ($250 MFS) — the statute reads "or" and FTB 5805 Part I lists both. `compute_ca_safe_harbor` runs the covered-year prong (CA tax less withholding) and structurally cannot run the prior-year one, which needs that year's own credits against a `CaSafeHarbor` carrying only its total tax. The gap is one-directional and it is the bad one: a taxpayer the prior-year prong clears outright was shown a CA required annual payment, a shortfall and a voucher schedule with nothing on the page naming the rule — and §6654(e)(1) has NO prior-year half, so the federal section beside it reads as confirmation. `_CA_EITHER_YEAR_FLOOR_NOTE` HANDS THE RULE OVER rather than applying it: whether "credits ... provided by Part 10 or this part" reaches the §19002 withholding credit decides how much of the prior year it clears, and deciding that wrong the other way CLEARS a penalty that attaches — the under-statement this section exists to prevent. It fires outside the de minimis (where the covered-year prong already answered) and outside a $0 prior year (where the §19136(c)(2) note above already clears it by the same subdivision), both pinned negatively so the note cannot become the noise that gets skimmed. **Round eighteen widened the UNKNOWN sibling of that note.** `prior_year_floor_unknown` was gated on a positive annual gap, on the reading that a met requirement makes the floor moot. It does not: FTB 5805 measures each instalment cumulatively, so estimates arriving after a deadline meet the annual figure while leaving that instalment accruing — and the report says exactly that, `_voucher_catchup_note` returning its timing warning on those inputs. §19136(c)(2) bars the addition to tax outright on either year's figure, dates and all, so the one unread number could end the question; at or above the §19136.3 limitation nothing else pointed at it either, `degraded` being False up there by design. The new disjunct is `_voucher_catchup_note`'s own gate minus the calendar — estimates paid, withholding short of the requirement — because withholding alone is deemed paid ratably by §6654(g)(1) and Worksheet II line 2 and cannot leave an instalment late; that silence is now pinned beside the flag, so the two cannot part. A second note constant carries the case, since the existing one says "could zero the schedule below" and "before paying anything below" to a reader whose every voucher row is $0.
  - **A state deadline rolls on ITS OWN state's holiday set, and California's INCLUDES the federal one (`irs_dates` self-test, `test_projection.py::test_a_rolled_april_installment_stays_open_until_its_observed_date`, `::test_voucher_rows_serialize_observed_section_7503_deadlines`, `::test_the_dates_note_rolls_each_jurisdiction_on_its_own_rule`, `test_tax_calendar.py::test_ca_540es_rolls_on_the_federal_holiday_set`):** Epic L established "federal rolls past weekends AND DC legal holidays, state past weekends only", on the premise that a state does not observe DC Emancipation Day. For California that premise is false. **R&TC §18410**: "a legal holiday includes a federal legal holiday recognized by the Internal Revenue Service under Section 7503 of the Internal Revenue Code." FTB applies it by name in the Form 540-ES instructions this toolkit dates ("Due to the federal Emancipation Day holiday observed on April 15, 2022, tax returns filed and payments mailed or submitted on April 18, 2022, will be considered timely"), and its published TY2023 quarters are April 18 2023, June 15 2023, September 15 2023 and **January 16 2024** — the first rolled past observed Emancipation Day, the last past MLK Day, neither reachable by a weekend-only rule. The weekend-only rule therefore published CA payment dates up to three days EARLY, and once `_voucher_dates_note` was put on the same rule it reported a 540-ES row as already missed on the very day it was due — a warning firing on a deadline that has not passed, which is the gate readers learn to skim. `next_business_day_state` now REQUIRES a state code and resolves it against `_STATES_CONFORMING_TO_FEDERAL_HOLIDAYS` (CA today); a state outside that set keeps the weekend-only roll as a labelled DEGRADE, not a claim about its law — NY's and NJ's statutes have not been read here, and under-rolling stays the safe direction for a payment date. Membership is evidence-only: add a state with its statute or its agency's published dates in hand. `tax_calendar._state_due` and `ptet._obs_date` (which had a fourth inlined copy of the rule) both thread the code through, and `tax_calendar._observed_note`'s "state-specific legal holidays are not modeled" caveat became jurisdiction-aware in the same change — on CA it had turned into a lying caveat, telling a Californian to go and confirm a date the engine now gets right, which teaches the reader to discount the same sentence where it is still live on NY and NJ. The self-test pins CA against the federal roll, an unread state against weekend-only, that the two do not resolve identically, and that a missing or blank state code is refused rather than guessed. **The DOCUMENTATION is pinned too, because the first correction changed the rule and left the premise standing in seven comments that a contributor reads BEFORE the code** — `tax_calendar`'s module docstring and its §7503 section header, `ptet._election_deadline`, three comments in `projection`, and `irs_dates`' own consumer list, which still claimed /tax-calendar and /tax-ptet emit unrolled statutory dates. `test_irs_dates.py::TestNoModuleRestatesTheSupersededStateRule` bans the superseded JUSTIFICATIONS verbatim across every `agents/lib/*.py` — "does not observe DC Emancipation Day", "DC holidays don't apply to state authorities", "WEEKEND rollover", "still emit statutory dates", and the CA-dated idempotence example — while deliberately NOT banning "rolls past weekends only", which is the TRUE description of the degrade an unread state keeps. The ban is absolute rather than "unless a nearby sentence marks it superseded": that escape was tried and let two of the seven reverts through on nothing more than an adjacent §18410 citation, which every corrected site carries. Paraphrase the history; do not quote the wording that was wrong. Its sibling `test_california_is_still_in_the_conforming_set` pins the behaviour the ban presupposes, so CA leaving the set fails there instead of turning the ban into a demand to delete a correct comment. **The user-facing half is `test_tax_calendar.py::test_no_skill_calls_the_generated_calendar_statutory_dates_only`:** the Epic-L v1 caveat ("v1 surfaces the statutory date — cross-check IRS.gov") outlived the wiring in three skills at once — `/tax-calendar`'s own Caveats block, `/tax-setup` directly beneath the invocation, and `/tax-extend` a few lines below a step that rolls the date ITSELF so §6601 interest cannot start early. All three told a taxpayer to go and correct a date the engine already gets right. The test asserts the rolled dates FIRST, so a release that genuinely emitted statutory dates would fail on behaviour rather than on prose. **Round seventeen read the verb.** §18410 says a CA legal holiday "INCLUDES" a federal one — additive, not a substitution — so CA keeps its Gov Code §6700 holidays (Lincoln's Birthday, César Chávez Day, Native American Day, the day after Thanksgiving) and its set is a SUPERSET of the federal one, of which only the federal half is modeled. Six comments across three modules said "IS", and `_observed_note` suppressed the go-and-confirm caveat for CA on the strength of "CA's holidays ARE modeled", which is false of the §6700 half. Unioning §6700 in is NOT the remedy and `TestTheUnmodelledHalfOfCaliforniasHolidaySet` is why: every CA date emitted already matches FTB's published one, so the union would move no wrong date and would move dates on unverified authority — the direction that turns a timely payment late, and the same reason the FL county dates keep theirs. What the unmodeled half gets instead is a GUARD, in two parts because either alone is vacuous: the risky (month, day) set is re-derived by walking BACK from each §6700 holiday over 2000-2060 and asserted non-trivial (24 pairs, September 22-28 among them), and the dates the live emitters actually hand the CA roller are CAPTURED by instrumenting `next_business_day_state` rather than listed, then asserted disjoint from it. A CA deadline placed on a colliding date fails the suite instead of publishing early into the passed-deadline gate.
  - **§6654(e)(2)'s zero-year gate requires a literal boolean (`test_projection.py::test_the_form_2210_zero_year_gate_requires_a_literal_boolean`):** the confirmed branch sets `no_penalty=True` and returns, clearing the whole year, and it was a truthiness test — so the JSON string `"false"`, or `1`, suppressed a penalty that may apply. `bool("false")` is True. The planning twin `projection._confirmed_flag` has rejected non-booleans since it shipped; this is the copy that prices the penalty on the FILED return, called by an agent assembling arguments out of `answers.md`, which is where a hand-typed `"false"` comes from.
  - **A rejected numeric value is never echoed (`test_projection.py::test_a_rejected_numeric_value_is_never_echoed_back`):** `_num` embedded `{value!r}` in its error, both CLIs print `redact(e)`, and `redact()` masks SSN and credential SHAPES — not names, email addresses or street addresses. A misplaced taxpayer name in `prior_year_form_2210_agi` therefore reached the terminal, against AGENTS.md §1. The message now names the field and the type only, the rule `_canonical_status` already followed.
  - **A non-zero amount never prints as "$0" (`test_projection.py::test_a_nonzero_amount_never_prints_as_zero_dollars`):** `_money` formatted whole dollars unconditionally, so every figure under fifty cents printed as "$0" — the same string the CA de minimis path prints for genuinely-zero vouchers, where it means nothing is owed. It surfaced as a voucher row reading "| Q4 | 2027-01-15 | 100% | $0 |", a hundred percent of nothing, reachable whenever a shortfall clears the $500 CA floor by cents ($500.03 of tax over withholding leaves $0.03 to schedule). Cents now appear ONLY in that window, so every ordinary figure is unchanged and the fix sits in the one formatter both reports share rather than at the table that exposed it. `retirement_dashboard._money` has the same shape and is knowingly left alone: it is outside this branch and its figures are contribution limits.
  - **A qualification lives on every surface that states the number, payload included (`test_projection.py::test_a_non_ca_state_balance_is_qualified_where_it_is_stated`, `::test_a_non_ca_state_balance_is_qualified_in_the_table_too`, `::test_the_payload_qualifies_a_non_ca_state_balance_on_an_open_year`):** California has a dedicated projection block; other taxed-state balances in this projection are bracket estimates. The hedge was added to one surface at a time across three rounds — first the closed-year W-4 lede, then the markdown State projection table, then the JSON payload — and each intermediate state was worse than none, because the lede says "see the state section below" and routes the reader from the qualified sentence to an unqualified restatement. The payload matters most: both SKILL.md files instruct the agent to read it BEFORE summarizing, and on an OPEN year `w4_lever_note` is `_W4_LEVER_LEAD`, which carries no state clause at all. `state_bracket_estimate_note` is now the single source for both the field and the banner, written from the same `ca_safe_harbor is None` discriminator the Caveats section uses, and it is plain prose so an agent reading `--json` never meets raw markdown. Documented in tax-project's payload list only — `withholding`'s state figure is always California, so the field does not exist there.
  - **A tolerance for one unknown must not quietly cover a second (`test_claude_hooks.py::test_the_measured_costs_counts_match_live_collection`):** the suite scale row was checked as a gap of 0-4 against live collection, to absorb the platform skips the RUN figure subtracts — wide enough to sit green on a row a full test out of date, which it did. It was then pinned exactly, and AA8 removed the pin altogether: the scale row states no count, because its only job is the ratio to a cold pass and a pinned suite count made every change that added a test anywhere hand-edit the hook. The test now REQUIRES the row to state none. The lesson holds for the hooks row, whose run figure is still derived from a stated skip count rather than tolerated.
  - **The filing-status guard lives in the shared helper, not at whichever caller remembered it (`test_projection.py::test_the_status_helpers_refuse_rather_than_defaulting_to_single`, `test_scenario.py::test_rate_kwargs_refuses_a_non_canonical_status_for_all_three_skills`):** `_full_status_key` ended in `.get(fs, "single")` and `_binary_status_key`/four inline capital-loss tests in `(filing_status or "").upper() == …`, so every non-canonical spelling resolved to the single column in silence. The value check was first added at the projection and withholding call sites only, which left /tax-scenario, /tax-roth-convert and /tax-harvest — all three read `filing_status` off `result.json` meta and hand it to `scenario._rate_kwargs` — pricing a conversion or a harvest on single brackets and the single standard deduction. Single brackets UNDER-state the tax on an MFJ or HoH filer, the direction AGENTS.md makes a hard error. `_full_status_key`/`_binary_status_key`/`_capital_loss_limit` now all route through `_canonical_status`, and `_FULL_STATUS_KEYS` is total over the canonical set so there is no default left to fall back to. `retirement_dashboard` keeps its own copy: it validates at its entry point and maps `None → "Single"` deliberately.
  - **Absent is not unrecognized (`test_projection.py::test_an_absent_filing_status_is_refused_as_missing_not_unrecognized`):** both cases raised "got an unrecognized value (0 chars)", which describes a value that is not there and names no remedy — a reader hunts for a bad spelling in a file where the key is simply gone. The missing branch now names `meta.filing_status` and the file to add it to; the unrecognized branch still never echoes the raw content, since a malformed profile can carry a taxpayer name there.
  - **Both harbors reconcile the triple they print (`test_projection.py::test_both_harbors_reconcile_the_triple_they_print`):** `withholding`/`estimates_paid` were rounded INTO the dataclass while the arithmetic ran on the raw floats, so `required_annual - (withholding + estimates_paid)` sat a cent away from the `shortfall` printed beside them — two payload fields a reader adds together disagreeing. The CA path took the round-once fix a round earlier and the federal twin did not; both now round at the top. `compute_estimated_vouchers` reconstructs `shortfall + estimates`, so this is also what keeps the voucher column reconciling.
  - **A confirmation flag is validated on every path, not only the one that reads it (`test_withholding.py::test_every_zero_liability_flag_is_validated_without_a_ca_section`):** `prior_year_ca_zero_liability_confirmed` was coerced by `_confirmed_flag` inside `build_ca_true_up`, which returns None before reaching it when `result.json` has no `california_540` section. The JSON string `"true"` — the exact case that helper exists to reject, since `bool("false")` is True — ran clean for a season in a non-CA workspace and began hard-erroring the moment a CA section appeared. That flag is now RETIRED (see the §19136(c)(2) entry below) and a legacy file carrying it is inert rather than refused, so the invariant is pinned on the two FEDERAL flags instead: `build_report` coerces both unconditionally, and the test drives a workspace with no CA section to prove it.
  - **The two routes to a $0 federal prior-year base are independent tests (`test_projection.py::test_a_filed_12_month_zero_reaches_the_ordinary_prior_year_prong`, `test_projection.py::test_the_form_2210_zero_year_gate_requires_a_literal_boolean`, `test_tax_computations.py::TestForm2210::test_prior_year_zero_exception`):** one flag stood for both. §6654(e)(2) needs a 12-month prior year plus US citizenship/residency throughout it and needs NO filed return; §6654(d)(1)(B)'s closing text needs a 12-month prior year plus a filed return and says nothing about citizenship.
