Collapsed, a first-year-resident or dual-status filer holding a full 12-month $0 return could not affirm the citizenship half, so the $0 was discarded and both engines demanded 90% of the current year from someone the ordinary prong covers at 100%/110% of $0 — while `tax-safe-harbor` SKILL.md, which lists them as separate preconditions, cleared the same taxpayer. `prior_year_return_filed_12_months_confirmed` is the second flag; either alone accepts the $0, neither still refuses it, and both are literal-boolean gated on the filed-return path because either one zeroes the requirement. The dropped-$0 note now names BOTH ways out, and the "prior AGI unknown → may be UNDERSTATED" caveat is suppressed at a $0 base in all three engines: 110% of $0 and 100% of $0 are both $0, so the caveat is false exactly where it used to fire.
  - **California's $500 de minimis is a California rule, not a conformity modification (`test_projection.py::test_no_ca_confirmation_flag_survives_anywhere`, `test_projection.py::test_the_degraded_ca_note_no_longer_offers_a_confirmation_route`):** an archived, genuinely COMPUTED prior-year Form 540 total tax of $0 was held back behind a federal-shaped citizenship confirmation, degrading the CA harbor to 90%-of-current for ever. R&TC §19136(c)(2) has no such gate: no addition to tax arises when EITHER year's tax less credits (withholding included, estimated payments excluded) is under $500 ($250 MFS), and FTB 5805's own no-penalty list reads it for either year while stating no citizenship, residency or 12-month condition. A $0 prior total necessarily clears it, every component of that figure being non-negative. The flag is retired and its absence pinned across engine, template and both skills together, so it cannot return half-wired; `_ca_section_computed` stays as the guard that actually matters, separating a computed $0 from an untouched template scaffold. §19136.3 still wins at $1M/$500K-MFS current-year CA AGI — the over-stating direction — and `_ca_notes` hands (c)(2) over there rather than applying it unasked. The degraded note now names one cause, because it has one.
  - **The prior-year prong discloses §6654(d)(1)(B)'s closing text (`test_projection.py::test_the_prior_prong_discloses_its_own_statutory_precondition`, `test_withholding.py::test_the_true_up_carries_the_prior_prong_precondition_too`):** "Clause (ii) shall not apply if the preceding taxable year was not a taxable year of 12 months or if the individual did not file a return for such preceding taxable year" is a precondition of the PRONG, not of a $0 base — but the engine enforces the confirmation flags only at $0, where the prong would zero the requirement outright. A positive base is accepted as supplied, because the flags default false in every template and refusing the overwhelmingly ordinary filed-a-12-month-return case is the gate that gets reasoned past. The precondition is therefore DISCLOSED beside the figure rather than enforced: an always-true sentence about the number, not a warning that fires on correct data. The direction is what makes it matter — 100%/110% of a small prior-year tax, for a first short filing year or a year with no return, sits far below the 90%-of-current prong that is the only one such a taxpayer has, and understatement is the one direction this module promises cannot happen. Gated by one shared predicate, `_prior_prong_precondition_applies`, on TWO exclusions — and each of them makes the note a LIE rather than merely noise (`test_projection.py::test_the_prong_precondition_note_stops_at_a_confirmed_zero_base`). DEGRADED: with no prior-year figure on screen the caveat qualifies nothing. A $0 BASE: it is reachable only through a confirmation the user gave, and one of the two routes to it is §6654(e)(2), whose three conditions do NOT include filing a return — Form 2210's instructions say the prior year need only have been 12 months "or would have been had you been required to file", and someone under last year's filing threshold is the ORDINARY case. Telling that taxpayer their $0 target "would then be too LOW" contradicts the correct result beside it and points at 90% of the current year: money §6654(e)(2) says they do not owe. The predicate is shared for the same reason the note is — a suppression in one renderer only is the same drift as a caveat in one renderer only. So the note is true of exactly the case nothing verifies: a POSITIVE base, whose only route is the ordinary prong, §6654(e)(2) being a no-LIABILITY exception that cannot produce one. Both renderers carry it, and the note is worded for both — /tax-project prints a prior-year ROW, /tax-withholding a safe-harbor TARGET derived from the same prong. `/tax-safe-harbor`'s step-0 handoff gate asks the question outright (a third scope check, requiring an explicit YES), since the handoff jumps over the skill's own preconditions section; `/tax-project` and `/tax-withholding` ask it before storing a POSITIVE base, and say explicitly that it does not carry over to a $0 one — instructing both to demand a filed return of every base is what shipped, and it would have billed a §6654(e)(2) taxpayer with no exposure whatever for 90% of this year's tax.
  - **No voucher row is ever negative (`test_projection.py::test_no_voucher_row_is_ever_negative`):** `_split_remainder_last` hands the LAST positive-weight entry whatever is left after the earlier shares, and each earlier share rounds independently — so on a shortfall smaller than a cent per open quarter every one of them rounds UP, their sum overshoots, and "whatever is left" goes negative. `_split_remainder_last(0.02, [1, 1, 1, 1])` returned `[0.01, 0.01, 0.01, -0.01]` and both report paths printed a negative Q4 voucher. The §6654(e)(1) floor does not stop it: that floor tests the BALANCE (tax less withholding) against $1,000, not the shortfall, so a two-cent gap behind a five-figure balance is ordinary. The running allocation is now capped at what actually remains — a NO-OP wherever the rounding behaves, so $100.00 over three open quarters is still 33.33/33.33/33.34 and the CA 30/40/0/30 splits are unchanged. Swept rather than spot-checked (every cent 0.00-0.39 across eight weight shapes, then end to end through both builders), because the failure lives in the interaction between the cent grid and the number of open quarters and the exact-sum contract has to survive the cap.
  - **The line-4 note names every credit Form 2210 line 3 does, and says the list is not closed (`test_projection.py::test_the_line4_note_names_every_credit_form_2210_line_3_does`):** line 3's instruction enumerates the refundable adoption credit (Form 8839 line 13) beside the EIC, ACTC, refundable AOTC, net PTC and fuel credit, and OBBBA made part of the adoption credit refundable for tax years beginning after 2024. The note omitted it while closing "if this year's return claims none of them, the figures above are already correct" — which told a filer whose only refundable credit is that one that an OVERSTATED required payment was correct, while the same skill's Form 2210 input instructions required subtracting it. The year condition is pinned ON THE ENUMERATION ENTRY, not anywhere in the note: the closing sentence names the year too, so a bare containment check stays green with the entry unconditional — and unconditional it sends a TY2024 filer subtracting a nonrefundable credit, which UNDERSTATES the requirement. No amount appears: the refundable portion is indexed.
  - **The template names BOTH routes to a $0 prior-year base (`test_projection.py::test_the_template_names_both_routes_to_a_zero_base`):** the two flags' own comments were correct and complete, but the tax-base field's comment still said a $0 base "requires prior_year_zero_liability_confirmed" — the pre-split wording. An agent reads the comment on the field it is filling in, asks only the citizenship question, and a taxpayer who filed a full 12-month $0 return but cannot affirm citizenship or residency throughout is refused a prong they have; the base is left null and the report demands 90% of the current year. Pinned as ALTERNATIVES ("EITHER"), not merely as two names present: naming both while reading as a conjunction refuses the same taxpayer.
  - **Route A sizes 4(c) against a PASSED instalment, not only the year's gap (`test_projection.py::test_the_skill_sizes_withholding_against_a_passed_instalment`):** §6654(g)(1) deems withholding paid in equal parts on each installment date, so raising it now also raises what counts at every deadline already passed — the skill's own step 4 says so. Route A nevertheless sized 4(c) from `still to pay`, the ANNUAL gap, and a late estimated payment that meets the year's total while leaving April short makes that figure zero. The skill answered $0 to the one question it exists to answer, and its timing check went on to call the surviving shortfall one nothing undoes — when a larger 4(c) undoes it exactly. The second figure solves step 4's own cumulative test for the withholding (`(required by deadline n - estimates credited by then) / the cumulative fraction at n`, which is `n/4` federally and CA's 30/70/70/100), and BOTH figures are reported: curing a passed instalment can need withholding far above the year's requirement, and the excess is a refund at filing rather than a payment, so the trade is the taxpayer's — the skill does not price the penalty, §6621 resetting quarterly. Pinned on `skill_text`, which reads `SKILL.md` PLUS its references, and separately on the link, so the fix cannot be "solved" by moving the paragraph somewhere nothing opens.
  - **The projection CLI refuses a declared wrong year (`test_projection.py::test_the_projection_cli_refuses_a_declared_wrong_year`):** the withholding CLI has compared `projection_inputs.json`'s `tax_year` against `--year` since it shipped and this one never did. Survivable while the file held only YTD figures; not once `prior_year_form_2210_tax_base` and its paired AGI moved in, because those are composed under the COVERED year's own Form 2210 line-8 instructions and a TY2025 file run with `--year 2026` feeds the wrong return's base to the prong. A DECLARED mismatch refuses naming both years. An ABSENT `tax_year` was at first allowed unconditionally — which the template defeats, since it ships the field as `null`, so the common case is a taxpayer who fills in the base from the template, never touches `tax_year`, and re-runs the file a season later. The absence is now tolerated only while the file answers NONE of the four year-bound `prior_year_*` fields (`test_a_yearless_file_that_answers_a_prior_year_field_is_refused`); answering one makes the year required, and the refusal names both the field that made it required and the field that fixes it. Presence of the KEY is deliberately not the test — the template ships all four, two as `null` and two as `false`, and a guard firing on those would refuse every file written from the template. Both CLIs share the one guard, `_require_inputs_tax_year`, since they read the same file and the same fields; `test_withholding.py::test_the_withholding_cli_shares_the_year_guard` pins the other caller. A MALFORMED `tax_year` — anything but a plain integer year — is refused on TYPE before the comparison, naming only the value's SHAPE (`test_projection.py::test_the_year_guard_never_echoes_a_malformed_tax_year`). The file is hand-edited, so a misplaced name, address or whole nested object lands there as readily as a wrong year does and the CLI prints the refusal; `redact` masks SSN and credential shapes by design and a taxpayer's name has neither. A non-int was already refused, but by the COMPARISON, which made nonsense of it — the string `"2026"` is `!= 2026`, so a file for the right year was turned away as "for TY2026, not TY2026". `secure_fs.year_or_shape` owns the one rule (a genuine year prints, because which year the file is for IS the message; everything else becomes "a string (N chars)" / "a dict"). **INTEGER-ness is not year-ness, and the first version tested the wrong one (`test_secure_fs.py::TestYearOrShapeOnlyPrintsAYear`):** `isinstance(value, int)` was taken as proof the value "cannot carry anything sensitive", but the field is hand-edited and an account, policy or member number lands in it as an int exactly as a name lands in it as a str. `redact` covers only the tail of that — `_NINE_DIGITS` masks a nine-digit run and nothing shorter, so eight digits printed in full. A bare integer now prints only inside a declared window (`_MIN_PRINTABLE_YEAR`/`_MAX_PRINTABLE_YEAR`), anything else becoming "an int (N digits)". The window is pinned to COVER every caller's accepted range rather than to equal it, because only one direction leaks: narrower than a caller and a legitimate mismatch merely loses the year it exists to name, wider and the window stops meaning "a year". Two callers were then still interpolating the raw value past the guard — `projection._require_inputs_tax_year`'s DECLARED-mismatch branch (the type check above it proves only int-ness, so the account number falls through to exactly there) and `archive.py`'s `meta.tax_year` comparison, which type-checks nothing at all. Three modules carried one message and the fix had reached two of them, which is this repo's recurring shape rather than a variation on it. Mutation-verified red per file, whole-file reverts, shared by the three modules that compare a hand-edited year so they cannot drift on what is safe to print — `retirement_dashboard`'s profile and projection-inputs checks and `tax_calendar`'s profile check carried the same leak verbatim (`test_retirement_dashboard.py::test_the_year_mismatch_errors_never_echo_a_malformed_year`, `test_tax_calendar.py::test_the_profile_year_mismatch_never_echoes_a_malformed_year`).
  - **A bracket-estimate state figure is qualified where it is STATED (`test_projection.py::test_a_non_ca_state_balance_is_qualified_where_it_is_stated`):** both closed-year ledes bold a state balance as "ALSO projected due" at the top of "Recommended actions", and only California has a dedicated projection block. The single qualification the report carried sat in Caveats at the very bottom, past a figure that reads as an amount owed. Unearned confidence over-states the number the taxpayer acts on as surely as a wrong figure does. California, being modeled, carries no such hedge — a caveat false of its reader is one they learn to skip.
  - **"Read these N things" is the N things listed (`test_projection.py::test_the_payload_field_count_matches_the_bullets_that_follow_it`):** the sentence said "eight" while nine bullets followed, because `has_prior_archive` was added without touching the count. An agent taking it literally stops at the eighth and drops `stale_as_of_note` — the one the same paragraph says must be surfaced verbatim before reporting any number. Pinned rather than deleted: the count is what tells an agent whether the list it can see is the whole list, in a skill read in pieces.
  - **The payload-disclosure roster is DERIVED from the payload (`test_portability.py::TestTheSkillsReadWhatTheEngineEmits::test_both_skills_name_every_note_the_payload_carries`):** the roster whose job is to guarantee every payload note is documented in the consuming skills was itself hand-kept — three substrings, covering five of the ten `_note`/`_warning`/`_mismatch` fields `projection._report_to_dict` emits. It was blind to `state_unsupported_note` (which says an entire state return is unmodeled and must be filed by hand), `nonresident_income_warning` and `state_cache_mismatch`, which is exactly how all three came to be missing from tax-project's payload list. The roster is now read off the skill's own payload builder and is default-DENY: every key the builder emits is a disclosure unless `PAYLOAD_FIGURES` classifies it as a figure, so a key added to either payload fails until it is classified or documented. The suffix rule that came first (`_note`/`_warning`/`_mismatch`) is not enough on its own — it caught the two note-shaped reasons `state` comes back null and missed the flag-shaped third, `state_no_income_tax`, which is the only one of the three meaning nothing is owed. A rule keyed on a naming convention sees only the names that follow it. Each field needs its own BULLET HEADER, matched exactly. Two vacuities were fixed to get there: the first check passed on any occurrence anywhere, so deleting `stale_as_of_note`'s entire bullet stayed green on one incidental prose mention — the field whose own text says it must be surfaced verbatim before reporting any number from the run; the second folded the `ca_` prefix away, so the two halves of a paired field satisfied each other and either could be deleted. Both spellings of a CA field are accepted (`ca_voucher_dates_note` on the report, `ca.voucher_dates_note` off /tax-withholding's nested block), neither for the federal half.
  - **The prior-year baseline clarifier has one copy (`test_projection.py::test_the_prior_baseline_clarifier_has_exactly_one_copy`):** `projection.render_markdown` and `withholding.render_markdown` each spelled out the clarifier, the degraded-mode bullet and the same `archive/{year-1}/` interpolation. `_prior_baseline_lines` is now the single copy; only the attribute path to the CA harbor differs between the two reports, and that difference is passed in.
  - **The MEASURED COSTS block is checked in its PROSE as well as its table (`test_claude_hooks.py::test_the_measured_costs_counts_match_live_collection`):** the rows were pinned while a sentence two paragraphs down said the hooks module "spawns bash processes across its 122 tests" against a row reading 123. Every `N tests` in the block must now be one of the block's own derived figures, read off the joined text, since a numeral and its `tests` either side of a line break once kept a stale count green. The same share of a cold pass was once also stated as "at least 84%" in one place and "roughly nine tenths" in another; since ruling Q13 each ratio is stated once, in words, and no test compares two statements of one, so that half is kept by a reader and not by a check. The reconciliation sentences for BOTH modules are required to parse — the old pattern demanded the word "module", which "portability collects 150 and runs 148" does not contain, so that figure was never checked and a `str`-valued fallback made the hooks comparison unable to hold either way. The whole check now costs ONE `--collect-only` over the seven modules the hook selects (over `tests/` until AA8), attributed per file from the node ids, instead of seven subprocesses inside the module the block calls the dominant share of a cold pass.
  - **The MEASURED COSTS counts are enforced, not asked for (`test_claude_hooks.py::test_the_measured_costs_counts_match_live_collection`):** four hand-refreshes of the block produced four drifts — most recently on the branch that made the block the SOLE owner of the figure by deleting it from this file, where it shipped a portability row five tests light and a whole-suite row twenty, under its own paragraph asserting every count had been re-derived by measurement. The test reads each of the seven modules off one collection over exactly those modules (not the suite since AA8), checks both halves of every `collects N and runs M` sentence against the rows they restate, requires every copy of a restated sentence to agree, derives portability's gap from the number of `--deselect` flags the hook actually passes, and recomputes both DERIVED rows (skill-edit, cold-pass) as sums of the parts — the half the block calls exact. COLLECTED rather than run, because collection is deterministic across machines while the run count subtracts platform skips that are not.
  - **MEASURED COSTS states no absolute timing, and nothing else in the hook states one in figures (`test_claude_hooks.py::test_the_hook_states_no_absolute_timing`):** ruling Q13 keeps the block to counts, ratios and ordering, and the block is the one owner of every runtime figure about the hook. Four reviews running each found a spelling of a duration that the pattern before them missed — `150-second`, `thirteen seconds`, `half a second`, `2m30s` — so the block may not use the words of duration at all, the ordinal `second` included, however its number is spelled. The figure rule runs inside the block too, since a unit glued to its number (`150sec`) has no word boundary for the word rule to find, and every numeral inside the block must be one of its counts, so a timing is refused for what its number is not, however its unit is spelled (`2:35`, `millisec`). Outside the block the same words are ordinary prose, so there the rule is narrower: no number in front of a unit in any comment of `skill-invariants.sh`. Code is exempt, being behaviour rather than a reading.
  - **A voucher column that does not sum to the shortfall leaves the taxpayer short (`test_projection.py::test_the_federal_voucher_column_sums_to_the_shortfall`):** the federal schedule computed one `round(shortfall / len(open_q), 2)` and printed it for every voucher, so three open quarters split $100.00 into $33.33 × 3 = $99.99. `compute_ca_estimated_vouchers` has always landed its remainder on the last weighted instalment "so the column sums to the shortfall exactly", and both docstrings describe the two as one design taking one pre-derived shortfall — a reader comparing them was told they agreed. The exposure is a cent, but §6654 has no de minimis on the instalment itself, and the test sweeps BOTH schedules across three as-of dates and four shortfalls, including one ($3,333.33) that does not divide evenly by two, three or four.
  - **An engine note that cites another skill's step NUMBER is pinned to that skill's headings (`test_portability.py::TestTheEngineNotesPointAtRealSkillSteps`):** both catch-up notes end "(/tax-safe-harbor step 4)" / "step 5", and step 0's only carve-out from its own hand-off gate keys on the same numbering. Insert or renumber one step in a 500-line skill and both notes point at the wrong procedure while the carve-out still names the old one — bouncing the reader back to the very table the note told them to distrust, which is the failure the carve-out exists to fix. Resolving to SOME heading is not enough, so the tests also check that the federal note lands on the cumulative-test step and the CA note on the California one, that the two differ, and that step 0 names the same two numbers the notes do.
  - **`/tax-project`'s voucher advice leads with the §6654(g)(1) cure (`test_projection.py::test_the_voucher_advice_leads_with_the_retroactive_cure`):** an estimated payment credits on the day it is made and cannot cure April or June; withholding is deemed paid in equal parts on every due date, so a W-4 4(c) bump cures an already-missed instalment where a voucher only stops further accrual. `/tax-withholding` has led its recommendations with that sentence since it shipped, while `/tax-project` printed "pay catch-up amounts NOW rather than at the printed dates" immediately ABOVE the 4(c) figure — steering a taxpayer with paychecks left to the weaker remedy at the one moment both were on screen. The test pins the preface and the note appearing together, so it fails if either the sentence or the fixture's note goes away.
  - **The engine's even split now ships with a banner, not just a caveat in the skill people are told to leave (`test_projection.py::test_render_markdown_federal_voucher_schedule_carries_the_catchup_note`):** the federal 1040-ES table renders `_ES_VOUCHER_CATCHUP_NOTE` the way the CA table has always rendered its twin, and `compute_estimated_vouchers` documents the simplification its missing docstring made step 0's claim about it false. AGENTS.md's rule is that an UNDER-stating fallback degrades loudly; a warning reachable only through `/tax-safe-harbor` step 0 reached nobody who took step 0's advice. Both notes are then gated on being TRUE of the table beneath them (`_voucher_catchup_note`), and two further tests pin the silences: an all-zero schedule (the harbor already covered, so "pay catch-up amounts NOW" asks for money that avoids nothing) and a date before the first deadline (all four still open, the even quarter — 30/40/0/30 for CA — exactly what each instalment requires, nothing missed). A banner that fires where it is false OVER-states, which AGENTS.md makes a hard error rather than a conservative default. The pre-existing test proved this by accident: its fixture's schedule was all zeros, so it pinned the note against a table the note was false of. **The first gating was a PROXY for that claim and both of those silences were partly wrong**, which is why the tests now run the claim itself. `test_the_ca_catchup_note_is_silent_in_march_too_because_both_sides_weight` is the third version of that assertion and the one that matches FTB 5805: both sides carry 30/40/0/30, so the March schedule's $243.30 at April 15 — 30% of the $811 the withholding leaves of a $5,000 requirement — is exactly what that instalment requires and nothing is owed. The version before it read the credited side as quarters, called $452.75 required and directed a catch-up against nothing. `test_the_catchup_note_is_silent_where_payments_already_meet_the_requirement` pins the other end, where estimates already satisfy the cumulative test and the proxy still demanded a payment. The federal all-open silence survives both, for the reason it was always true THERE: federal quarters are flat on both sides. `test_withholding.py` then pins the same two notes in the second renderer, which had the CA note unconditionally and the federal note not at all — one note, two modules, one fix that reached one of them. `test_the_catchup_note_fires_on_an_instalment_that_is_already_overdue` closes the last gap in the claim itself: the gate ran the cumulative test only at the earliest STILL-OPEN instalment, and after September 15 the one remaining instalment carries the whole annual requirement, so the printed amount matches what January asks and the note went silent with 70% of it (CA) or 75% (federal) overdue since an earlier deadline. The LAST PASSED instalment is what the gate tests, at what is outstanding NOW rather than what was ever late — `test_an_overdue_instalment_since_paid_does_not_reopen_the_note` pins that other end, because money already in cures an earlier instalment going forward and a penalty already fixed cannot be paid away. It is now the ONLY test in the gate: the open-side comparison that sat beside it could not fire alone, and `test_the_only_test_left_in_the_catchup_gate_decides_every_verdict` sweeps both schedules across nine as-of dates to hold that. Writing X for requirement minus withholding and E for estimates paid, the open side reduces federally to `E < 0` at n=1, to `X - E > X - E` at n=4, and at n=2 and n=3 to exactly `outstanding_at(n-1) > 0`; the CA weights collapse the same way. Two tests where one decides is not redundancy, it is a maintainer defending a branch that has never changed an answer — and the sweep is what makes re-adding it a deliberate act with a counter-example in hand. It fails if a future weighting makes the two genuinely diverge, which is the signal to bring the second test back. With both sides on one fraction the whole predicate reduces to `(requirement - withholding) x weight - payments`, and `_voucher_catchup_note` takes ONE `instalment_fraction` rather than a required and a credited one: two names for one quantity is what let the quarters reading ship, and the helpers are renamed `_federal_instalment_fraction` / `_ca_instalment_fraction` because "required" was the half-truth that made two of them look necessary.
  - **The catch-up note is about an AMOUNT; the DATE beside it needs column-specific guidance (`test_withholding.py::test_a_covered_harbor_does_not_silence_the_zero_columns_calendar`, `test_projection.py::test_the_note_inverts_once_the_accrual_period_has_closed`):** `_voucher_catchup_note` is scoped to the PENALTY schedule, which is all $0.00 whenever the harbor is covered or under a de-minimis floor. `_voucher_dates_note` takes penalty and zero-balance schedules separately: a funded penalty row gets accrual/cutoff guidance, while a balance-only row stays optional until the unextended balance deadline and then changes to the balance-due instrument without inventing a penalty. Every scan remains gated on `amount > 0`. Voucher builders now serialize the observed §7503 deadline itself — TY2022 Q4 is 2023-01-17 and TY2023 Q1 is 2023-04-18 — so Markdown/JSON agree with the filters and catch-up gate. A direct statutory-date caller is still observed defensively. The §6654(b)(2) accrual endpoint is distinct: it closes at the start of statutory April 15 (`today >= cutoff`) and never rolls, while the unextended balance deadline can roll later. `compute_form_2210` uses the same fixed endpoint. `today` remains optional and absent means silent. Tests pin both columns, both payment windows, observed dates in federal and CA rows, continued penalty-row guidance, the amount gate, and every field reaching JSON.
  - **"Prefer withholding" is the wrong lead once there is no payroll left (`test_projection.py::test_the_recommended_actions_lead_names_the_lever_that_exists`, `test_withholding.py::test_the_w4_lead_is_replaced_once_no_paycheck_remains`):** both renderers opened "Recommended actions" with the §6654(g)(1) cure unconditionally, directly above a 4(c) figure reading $0.00/paycheck over 0 remaining paychecks. The clause "when paychecks remain" makes it defensible rather than false, which is worse in a heading — the reader takes the recommendation and finds the number blank. `_w4_lever_note` is one shared constant pair returning the lead or its inversion, always a string because the section has always opened with a sentence and the two states are two sentences rather than one and its absence. It keys on the real action date's `paychecks_remaining`, NOT on the stored annualization date or a per-paycheck figure of $0.00. The closed-year default at 12-31 and a preserved valid mid-year stamp reopened after year-end therefore both close W-4/DE 4; a fully covered live mid-year run still prints $0.00 for a different reason, pinned by a counterweight. The note says "not because nothing is owed", since those same zeros are what a covered year shows.
  - **A gate that fires where nothing can be done about it teaches an agent to read past it (`test_claude_hooks.py::test_the_hooks_dependents_are_real_and_complete`):** the hook's `HOOKS_DEPENDENTS` subset carried `test_the_measured_costs_counts_match_live_collection`, which goes red the instant a test is ADDED to one of the seven modules the hook selects and stays red until the MEASURED COSTS block is refreshed by hand in several places — a refresh a round can only make coherently once its tests are final. So every tool call of the round that makes the refresh possible was blocked, on correct in-progress work, with the red carry-forward re-blocking each one. It is out of the subset and enforced in CI, in the review, and on every edit that takes the whole hooks module. The same judgement the `--deselect` registry pair records, reached through a check that is factually RIGHT.
  - **A dependency list that calls itself complete is pinned as a SET (`test_claude_hooks.py::test_the_hooks_dependents_are_real_and_complete`):** the pin was a lower bound over two hardcoded names, and a lower bound cannot see an omission — so the list described itself as "the whole of what an edit to one of those modules can turn red" while leaving out the deselect pin, the one assertion that resolves `--deselect` ids against `tests/test_portability.py`'s own source. Membership is exact now, both directions. Its resolution check was a substring search too, which a strict PREFIX satisfies while collecting nothing: pytest answers such a selection with exit 4 for the whole pass, and the hook holds its stamp on 4 and exits 2 — a wedge that re-blocks every later call, produced by a rename, with the suite green.
  - **A skip must name the environment that cannot answer, not every failure in it (`test_claude_agents.py::test_a_blobless_clone_is_not_reported_as_a_broken_pin`):** the Replay A pin guard excused any `git show` failure on a partial clone, so a misspelled path or a revision that never held the file skipped green under a message blaming a promisor nobody asked. A blobless clone HAS its trees — `--filter=blob:none` omits blob content only — so `ls-tree` separates "this clone cannot fetch the blob" from "the commit does not hold this path" locally. The shallow arm had this control already; the blobless one did not, which is how one defect shipped into two environments.
  - **A shell construct is read as the shell reads it, never as the spelling a corpus happens to hold (`test_preflight.py::test_a_command_word_may_be_an_alias_and_a_path_may_be_relative`, `::test_a_heredoc_bound_to_a_variable_is_the_third_pair`):** two more instances, both fail-open. A script operand is a PATH and the relocation class held a bare FILENAME, so `cd agents && python3 lib/ptet.py` and `cd agents/lib && python3 ./ptet.py` were rewritten to nothing and cleared while `cd agents && python3 -m lib.ptet` — the same command through the `-m` half of the same rule — was reported. And a `read` option was tested as a whole WORD, so the bundled `read -rd '' CODE` bound nothing, its heredoc body went unread, and the `python3 -c "$CODE"` below it named no engine: the exact pair that audit exists for, defeated by the spelling every runbook uses. Options are walked letter by letter now, with the value attached or detached as getopt takes it. **That audit is now the SECOND line and is FROZEN.** The engine refuses a host interpreter at import (`agents/lib/__init__.py`), so a spelling this parser misses no longer reaches a return — and every round of widening it added parser surface for the next round to find bugs in. Its module docstring carries the threat model and the three accepted misses; a finding outside that model is refuted by citation, not fixed.
  - **The launcher rule is enforced by the engine, not only audited (`test_host_guard.py`):** `agents/lib/__init__.py` exits 2 at package import when a workspace `.venv` exists and the interpreter is not it, so `python3 -m agents.lib.X`, `python3 agents/lib/X.py`, a `-c` body and a heredoc all fail the same way — every one of them imports the package first, which is why the spelling of the command stopped mattering. Four things are pinned. The three states (no venv / inside / outside), because "no venv" must stay SILENT: CI installs into the runner's own interpreter and a fresh clone has none until `tax-setup` Step 6, and a gate that fires on a correct state teaches its reader to reason past it. AGREEMENT with `preflight._venv_error` over those same three, because the rule is deliberately written twice — importing preflight here would pull 2,400 lines into every engine process and invert the dependency direction, since preflight must stay runnable when the guard is what is wrong. The WIRING, in a subprocess against a copy of the file, because the function tests cannot show that the module-level call exists at all. And package-import COVERAGE of every module under `agents/lib/` except the two exempt ones, statically, because the script-path form is guarded only by each module importing the package — a new leaf written without that import is a silent hole, and it fails the day it is written. The message names no path: `sys.prefix` sits under a workspace folder that may carry a taxpayer's name, which is the same reason `run.sh` drops `$0` from its own errors. The preflight exemption is matched by IDENTITY, never by name: `os.path.samefile` against this package's own `preflight.py`, so a symlink and a relative spelling still match while a stranger's file called `preflight.py` — which a basename check exempted, and which could then compute on the host interpreter with no refusal — does not. And the PROGRAM is asked of CPython rather than re-derived from the command line, which is the second thing that changed after review. The walk that stood here read `sys.orig_argv` from the left, skipping interpreter options until it reached a token that was not one — a model of CPython's option table, and it shipped two defects in opposite directions. An ATTACHED `-c<code>` is not the token `-c`, so `python -c'<engine call>' agents/lib/preflight.py` fell through the generic option branch and read preflight's path as the program: the exemption taken on a bare `-c` body, fail-OPEN. And "the last letter of the cluster spends the next token" was applied to the whole token, so `-Xtracemalloc` and `-Wignore::DeprecationWarning:pkg.algorithm` were misread as operand-taking and preflight lost its exemption under either — end to end, `python3 -X tracemalloc agents/lib/preflight.py` printed the refusal once per roster module and never rendered preflight's own diagnosis. Two questions replace the table. `__main__.__file__`, which CPython sets to the script before that script's own imports run, so no option in front of it can matter; and `sys.argv[0]` when there is none, which CPython writes as `-m`, `-c` or `-` itself and `runpy` does not overwrite until after this package is imported. Only `-m` can be preflight, so a `-c` body is refused on CPython's own word rather than on a reading of the command line. Which module `-m` names is then arithmetic — `sys.argv` holds `['-m', *the module's own arguments]`, so the name sits exactly `len(orig_argv) - len(argv)` tokens in — and nothing is searched for, so a second `-m` among those arguments cannot confuse it. `test_the_option_table_is_gone` fails the day a table comes back. The one knowing gap is `-m` below MIN_PYTHON, where there is no `orig_argv` to measure against: fail-CLOSED, and the refusal names the script form, which needs no `orig_argv` and is what all twenty-five shipped invocations use.
  - **The guard is the one engine file whose ABSENCE reads as health, so preflight certifies it (`test_preflight.py::TestThePackageInitialiserIsCertified`):** Python treats `agents/lib/` with no `__init__.py` as a namespace package, so on the partial copy preflight exists to detect, every sibling still imports, every roster entry still passes, and the host-interpreter guard is simply gone — reproduced, and the sibling computed a bracket on a host interpreter with no refusal. `ENGINE_MODULES` structurally cannot cover it: the roster is keyed on file stems, `__init__.py`'s stem is not its module name, and the CLI-dispatch check would look for a `lib.py` beside preflight that a package does not have. So `_guard_error` checks it apart, and checks it the way `_probe` checks the math — on a known answer in BOTH directions, because a guard reduced to `return ""` passes every existence check there is. The probe passes a `-c` INVOCATION on purpose, and passes all three of the inputs the exemption reads: the guard exempts preflight, so leaving any of them to default would take the exemption and read clean against any guard at all. `main_file` is the one that made that concrete rather than theoretical — with only the argv overridden the probe read the real `__main__.__file__`, saw preflight, and certified a stub. Then the WIRING, one level out and for the same reason: truncate the file after its last `return` and the function is still there and still right, while the module-level call that enforces it is gone — a valid module that certifies perfectly and refuses nothing. A source scan for the call is what `_cli_dispatch_error` does for `__main__` and is not enough here, since the whole argument is that present is not wired; so the file is EXECUTED, as a copy in a temp root that has a `.venv` this interpreter is not, and required to exit 2. The copy reads its inputs off `sys` rather than taking them as arguments, so `_as_a_dash_c_invocation` supplies all three and restores them, and the copy is never registered in `sys.modules`. Every one of those assignments happens BEFORE the probe's first filesystem call: they sat after it, under a `finally` holding an unconditional `del sys.orig_argv`, so an OSError during setup on a pre-3.10 interpreter raised AttributeError out of the `finally` and replaced the exit-2 MIN_PYTHON report with "could not run" — on exactly the interpreter preflight exists to diagnose. And a failure of the probe's own ENVIRONMENT is now carried apart from a verdict about the engine: `_probe_environment_fault` warns where `except Exception` used to block. ENOSPC on `/tmp`, or EACCES under a sandboxed `$TMPDIR`, reported `engine_missing` on a healthy clone in the correct virtualenv — blocking every taxpayer-facing skill and answering with a re-clone and a reinstall, the OS error redacted away. What is lost there is certification of the guard, not the guard: the package imported, so the refusal already ran for this process.
  - **A refusal is worth the status it exits with (`test_host_guard.py::TestTheRefusalReachesPytestAsAStatus`):** `tests/conftest.py` reports the engine's refusal once instead of forty times, and it called `pytest.exit(..., returncode=2)` from the conftest's module BODY — where pytest catches the `Exit` as a `ConftestImportFailure` and ends the run with an "ImportError while loading conftest" banner, a full traceback (the second copy of the message the file exists to prevent) and status 4, a usage error. The `returncode=` argument was dead. It runs from `pytest_configure` now, the one place the `Exit` is honoured, and is driven end to end with a control that runs the suite green: a scan of the source cannot see a correct-looking call being ignored.
  - **The launcher refuses a third state rather than falling through it (`test_preflight.py`):** `python3 -m venv .venv` on a system that packages the stdlib venv module separately fails inside `_setup_pip`, which runs before `setup_scripts`, leaving a `.venv/` with no activate script — exactly what `tax-setup` Step 6 produces there. `run.sh` had no branch for it and silently exec'd the HOST interpreter; the engine guard then refused, and the first two remedies that refusal prints are `sh agents/lib/run.sh ...`, byte for byte the command that produced it. The launcher is the layer that can tell the three states apart, so it exits 2 there with the whole remedy — including the `ensurepip` hint, which the rebuild recipe it replaced did not carry.
  - **...and a fourth, which is not the third (`TestTheLauncherActuallyRuns`):** `-f` answers false for an activate script under a directory this process may not search, exactly as for one that is not there, so an intact virtualenv behind a permission problem was refused with `rm -rf .venv` — the destructive half of the conflation `preflight._activate_path` already refuses, and since preflight is reached through the launcher its "do not delete it on this evidence" verdict was unreachable for the state it was written for. An unreadable script is the same state by another route: `-f` is true, `.` then aborts the shell with its own diagnostic, which names the file by its full workspace path and exits 1. The launcher now tests searchability along the path and readability of the script before either activation or the rebuild verdict, and refuses with `ls -ld` and no path. All four states are executed in a subprocess against a copied launcher; the third had shipped with no test at all.
- Expected values are hand-computed from IRS worksheets — update comments when changing assertions
- When adding tests, update the test count reference on the line above
- Test fixtures under `tests/fixtures/` are allowlisted from the PII **filename** scan because synthetic fixtures must share filenames with real workspace files; new fixtures MUST live under that path. They are **not** exempt from the content scan — add a synthetic SSN-shaped value to `.pii-allowlist` if one is genuinely needed
- Fixture files sharing a runtime filename (`result.json`, `taxpayer_profile.json`, `tax_law_*.json`, `projection_inputs.json`) match `.gitignore`'s unanchored rules — add new ones with `git add -f` (existing fixtures are already tracked)

- **NY nonresident/part-year (`test_ny_it203.py`):** published TY2025 worksheets
  pin annual deduction before source allocation, signed losses and ratios above
  one, pre/post allocation credits, City rate schedules and rounded-month
  exemptions, MCTMT isolation, jurisdiction-specific payments and actual-date
  plan validation. The documented launcher is executed; malformed plans,
  incomplete worksheets, competing NY variants and dropped City totals refuse.

- **1099-G benefits reach income, not just a tie-out field (`test_government_benefits.py`):**
  Both reviewed revisions reconcile source boxes, gross categories and program
  inventory. UI repayments and eligible UI contributions reduce only UI; family
  income stays gross. A separately confirmed signed non-benefit Schedule 1
  amount prevents reuse of an old aggregate. The builder derives actual line 8
  before federal AGI and checks that settlement before any scope waiver. CA
  subtracts only included UI and eligible PFL; unknown program treatment blocks.
  Refund-only forms still need a zero-benefit inventory. Rebuilds are idempotent,
  source changes invalidate receipts, and net-zero Schedule 1 still attaches.

- **MISC source boxes preserve tax character (`test_misc_income.py`):** Both
  reviewed revisions split nonbusiness box3 and box8 from Schedule C receipts.
  Withholding and direct-sales notices cannot inflate income. Legacy MISC
  inventories, unknown treatment and nonfinite amounts refuse. The separate
  signed Schedule1 remainder excludes tracked MISC and UI/PFL; when benefits
  also exist it supplies their non-benefit component. Actual line8, federal/CA
  AGI, withholding, idempotence, source receipt invalidation and the scope-waiver
  boundary are checked. Both detail workpapers attach even at a zero net total.

- **W-2G income and other-form withholding are separate (`test_w2g_intake.py`):**
  Covered-year source inventories distinguish W-2G from W-2GU, preserve gross
  reported winnings, reviewed casual/business contributions and box4 credits.
  A worksheet method needs explicit proof and a checked disclosure. Identical
  wagers are reconciled rather than blindly added. The existing Schedule1
  component excludes mapped casual winnings through a new confirmation; line25c
  separately adds W-2G withholding to confirmed remaining other-form credits.
  Actual lines8/25c, payments, state income, scope waivers, receipt changes and
  byte-idempotence are checked. W-2G CopyB attaches when federal tax was withheld.

- **Tip/overtime source revisions (`test_tips_overtime_intake.py`):** the linked
  source contract pins TY2026 TP/TT, NEC1a-1d and MISC13a/13b/14 and keeps the
  TY2025 fallback separate. Synthetic income and withholding remain unchanged
  by the informational subamounts; an already reported premium reaches the
  below-AGI deduction without a second division. Headless source reviews are
  required from intake or raw/allowed/disallowed workpaper evidence; overtime
  alone does not require a tip occupation answer. The existing occupation,
  Form4137, SE-income-cap and QBI-source limits remain explicit.


- **California owner credits (AA72, `test_ca_owner_credits.py`):** hand-worked
  FTB3510 PartII uses exemptions and earlier A credits before TMT, while
  FTB3804-CR code242 follows OSTC and can use regular tax below TMT. K-1 full
  credit dollars reconcile independently of the limited claim, and PTET
  vintages retain oldest-first usage, expiry and origin years through rollover.
  Old entered AMT allowable amounts cannot influence a rebuilt return. Missing
  eligibility/source facts and unfetched AMT rates/carry period refuse. The
  independent tie-out and checked builder both verify the workpapers and lines.
